The residue is stated rather than implied: a heading whose stale word is an ordinary one, a comment, a helper's option name and an assertion message still have nothing, ever.
- `2026-08-21-a-gate-that-only-fails-when-the-machine-is-busy` — `spec-gates.test.ts`'s escape-shape gate runs 24 in-process type-aware ESLint passes in ~2.7s against a 10s suite default, so it passes alone, passes on a quiet re-run, and failed once during a wave with six agents live. A test that fails only under load is worse than a slow one: it reads as a regression the reader's own change caused, and what makes it go away is re-running. The timeout is now `LINT_ZONES.length * ESCAPE_SHAPES.length * 2 * LINT_PASS_BUDGET_MS` — derived from the loop rather than written as a number, so a fifth zone carries its own headroom instead of landing the failure on whoever added it — and the wiring was proved by shrinking the budget and watching that one test time out. Both obvious alternatives are rejected in the rule: raising the suite default hands the same headroom to every unit test, where a ten-second unit test IS the bug.
- `2026-08-21-the-gate-that-holds-the-briefing-contract-does-not-read-the-contract` — `check-briefing-contract.ts` exists so the rules an agent is handed stay reachable, and its link scan reads the two `CLAUDE.md` files only, so `standards/briefing.md` — the document the check exists to protect — is a link TARGET and never a source of links. Measured both ways: a link to a path that does not exist, appended to the contract, leaves the suite wholly green; the same appended line in `CLAUDE.md` reddens it and names the target, so the reader works and the population is what is narrow. No live dangle exists, because the contract cites every path in backticks and writes no markdown file links at all — the gap is latent and surfaces the first time somebody adds a convenience link to a file another lane later moves. Resolved in a second lane, the one owning the checker: the scan iterates `linkingDocuments(bindingDocuments, briefingContract)` while `BINDING_DOCUMENTS` stays the population `unbound()` is judged over, since demanding the contract link itself would fail immediately. The same append now reddens the suite and names the target. Two things came out of doing it that the proposal did not predict — widening the population collided with the refusal case that deletes the whole standards directory, because that deletes the contract inside it, so refusals are now ordered by how specific the repair they name is with the directory read first; and the grep the finding cited to prove no live dangle existed returns nothing at all under ugrep, which matches neither `\(` nor `[(]` against a literal parenthesis, so it needs `-P` to report the one anchor it claimed.
- `2026-08-21-the-bundle-gate-is-in-no-pre-merge-run-and-fires-at-the-deploy` — `first-paint-budget.ts` is the only thing in the tree that can see what a visitor downloads, it is correct, and `editor#build` appears in exactly one task graph: the deploy's. `turbo run typecheck lint test test:e2e --filter='!agents-inc' --dry=json` — the four tasks CI's check-web job runs — returns `editor#lint`, `editor#test`, `editor#test:e2e` and `editor#typecheck` and no `editor#build`; both hook tiers agree, since `.husky/pre-commit` runs `lint typecheck test` and `.husky/pre-push` runs `lint test test:e2e`. The `build` nodes that DO appear in that graph are `^build` on the other tasks — the dependencies' builds, seven of them reporting `command="<NONEXISTENT>"` — which is why the graph reads as covering the build and does not. So a first-paint regression is green through every pre-merge gate, green on the merge, and then fails the shared deploy job on the next push to `main`, blocking the site shipping any web change until the bundle is fixed. Both sibling workspaces already solved this, each with a workspace-local `turbo.json` adding its OWN `build` to a task CI runs, and `packages/cli/turbo.json`'s comment names the editor as the case that correctly does not need one — "its Playwright config boots a dev server and never reads its own dist". The question that answers is whether the SUITE needs the build; the question nobody asked after it is whether anything runs the build at all, and `apps/editor` has no `turbo.json`. The finding that installed the gate asserts the opposite in prose — "there is no way to land a regression through a green build" — which is true of a build and was the half that got checked. Durable rule: a check that lives inside a build is only as good as the run that invokes that build, and the second half is a separate claim; a `package.json` naming a `build` is not evidence anything runs it, `turbo run <what CI runs> --dry=json` is, and it costs a second. Headroom on the live gate measured the same day: 304.4 KB gzipped against a 330 KB budget, 25.6 KB spare — one statically-imported library.
- `2026-08-21-an-ordinal-in-an-explanatory-comment-is-a-count-and-four-shipped-with-the-rules-that-ban-them` — three passes landed on 2026-08-21 whose whole subject was a claim nothing executes, and each made its reasoning concrete with an ordinal or a total. Four of those numbers were wrong: `expectCancelledExit` "not from the 35 sites that abort" over 36 `abortAndDestroy` call sites in a tree-diagram annotation, `UI_SYMBOLS` with "three identifier-valued members" over four in a document whose own `UI_SYMBOLS` section names both glyph pairs, and "a fifth zone" twice over a three-element `LINT_ZONES`. `documentation-bible.md` § "A Count Lives in Exactly One Document" opens with exactly the tree-diagram case and then argues from DUPLICATION, so it reads as silent on a number no document owns and none repeats — and an ordinal inside an explanatory sentence reads as rhetoric rather than as a falsifiable claim, which is the form a narrative docblock reaches for. All four corrected by deleting the number rather than re-deriving it. No checker is proposed and the reasoning is in the finding: each population is a different shape, none is an enumeration, and a per-sentence registry row would cost more than the sentences are worth.
- `2026-08-21-the-hand-rolled-reader-and-the-shared-one-stopped-at-the-same-semicolon` — the finding that retired one hand-rolled copy of `readGeneratedUnion` recorded a divergence that does not exist: `[^;]+` cannot cross a `;` and `[\s\S]*?;` is lazy, so both stop at the first one, and they differ only in the leading whitespace the copy trimmed. The scenario named as the consequence — a union body containing a semicolon — truncates BOTH identically, and it is emitted today: `generateStackAgentConfig` writes `StackAgentConfig` as an object body with one `"<category>"?: …;` line per category, so the shared reader answers its first property. Latent, because every alias it is pointed at is a flat string union — and undocumented, which is what invites copy number three. Closed by stating the boundary in the reader's docblock and pinning it with a fixture whose assertion reddens only when the reader is taught to span braces; the false sentence is deleted rather than rewritten, at both ends. The open half is the class: a finding's recorded MECHANISM is prose, and nothing runs it.
- `2026-08-21-an-assertion-named-for-a-discrimination-its-fixture-could-not-make` — `generated-types.test.ts` carried an assertion titled "reads only the requested alias when another alias shares its suffix" that could not fail for the reason it names: `readGeneratedUnion` anchors on `export type <alias> =`, `.exec` answers the FIRST match, and the fixture declared the SHORT alias first — so a reader anchored on a bare `<alias> =` reads the same body and the assertion holds either way. Only the reversed order tells them apart, and it is the order `assembleConfigTypesSource` never emits, which is what made the gap costless and invisible together. Found by mutation rather than review, because a test title is prose and nothing reads it against what its fixture can distinguish. Closed by a reversed-order fixture and one assertion, which is now the only one of twelve that reddens when the anchor is dropped.
- `2026-08-21-a-refusal-wrapped-in-a-regexp-is-as-vacuous-as-a-bare-constant` — the row that raised the vacuous-refusal defect proposed two fixes and one of them is not one: `toThrow(new RegExp(CONSTANT))` was written down as the shape where "an undefined constant is a TypeError rather than a wildcard", and `new RegExp(undefined)` is `/(?:)/`, the empty pattern, which is a substring of every error message there is. So the wrapped form is exactly as vacuous as the bare one while reading as the deliberate repair — and `vacuousThrowAssertions`, the scan written to end the class, condemns a bare imported identifier and passes a `NewExpression`, so it reports clean over the sites in `check-briefing-contract.test.ts` that use it. Nothing is vacuous in fact today: every constant they name is exported, so every one of those assertions still discriminates, and what the shape costs is the guarantee rather than a live green. Not fixed here — the call sites belong to another lane this round and widening the scan first would redden a suite this pass cannot repair — so both halves are written out in order: convert those sites to `expectRefusal`, then teach `isVacuous` to look through a `new RegExp(x)` / `RegExp(x)` wrapper before judging `x`, with a fixture for the interpolated-local form that must stay allowed.
- `2026-08-21-the-task-id-ban-holds-at-its-two-enforceable-locations-and-nowhere-else` — the ban enumerates three locations and its sibling finding explains the roster by saying all three are string literals a `no-restricted-syntax` selector can match. Two of them are. A comment is not: `no-restricted-syntax` registers each selector as a VISITOR KEY, so it fires only on nodes the linter walks, and comments are attached trivia reachable only through `sourceCode.getAllComments()` — exactly the position the filename was in. The tree splits along that line and the split is the finding: over 432 specs, **zero** `describe` / `it` / `test` titles carry a tracker-shaped run, while **97 lines across 56 files** did in comments and other body text. The half a tool could hold is the half nobody breaks, and because the enumeration reads as one rule the two enforced members make the third look enforced too. CLI-560's five files cleared 15 of the 97 with the meaning preserved at every site rather than the sentence deleted; **82 across 51 remain and they are CLI-547's**, which is also why no gate was written — a content-side twin of `spec-filenames.test.ts` that is red on 82 files acquires an exclusion list, and an exclusion list is the snapshot-of-a-moment the whole class is about. Two design inputs measured while filing: the digit run must be `{2,4}`, because `{1,4}` condemns the `D-1`…`D-7` E2E phase labels CLI-574 rules need no rename and moves the census from 157 to 97; and the gate would condemn `spec-filenames.test.ts`'s own `NAMES_A_TASK` fixtures, which are the subject guard that makes it a gate rather than a recogniser answering `false` to everything — so those fixtures compose from the prefix constant rather than the gate carving out its own sibling by path; and a four-digit run that is a plausible YEAR must be excluded, since `skills-2026` inside the plan slug `"custom-skills-2026-08-06-investigation"` is the one false positive among the 82, a class with no filename-side twin because a basename never carries a date while a body quotes dated finding slugs routinely — so 81 across 50 are real. `no-warning-comments` is not the interim answer either: its `terms` schema is `array of string` in the installed eslint 10.8.0, literal terms with no pattern, so expressing `CLI-\d+` means enumerating every ticket ever issued — the `todo/`-derived roster that answers clean in a published checkout because it cannot see anything.
- `2026-08-21-a-count-over-declared-keys-and-a-document-saying-appears-in-are-two-numbers` — `built-in-catalogue.md` said `cli-developer` and `cli-tester` "appear in the same 8" stacks. The number is right and the sentence is wrong: `Object.keys(stack.agents)` counts DECLARED sub-agents, and seven of `cli-tester`'s eight slots are `{}`, so one stack actually equips it. The document's own re-derivation invocation takes the declared reading, so it was internally consistent, re-runnable, and still told a reader eight stacks ship a CLI tester. This is the adjacent failure to count drift — the count never moved, was correct every time anybody checked it, and named a different quantity than the sentence around it, which is why re-deriving reproduces the ambiguity instead of catching it. Every other agent's two readings agree, so the one ambiguous figure sat invisible beside twelve that were not. Fixed: invariant 4 states both readings, `EXPECTED_EMPTY_AGENT_SLOTS` pins the seven by name (not by arity) beside an assertion that the one filled slot is filled, and both were mutation-checked. Proposes a documentation-bible rule that a count states whether it counts keys or non-empty values.
- `2026-08-21-hoisting-a-guarded-mechanism-to-a-base-class-outruns-the-gate-that-guarded-it` — the completed-with-failures ending widened from `edit` to `init` and `eject`, which moved its recorded-work list onto `BaseCommand` and expired the premise its gate rested on. While the mechanism was private, no other command could file a failure into a list nobody read, because no other command could file one at all; shared, recording and answering are two independent inherited calls and the pair is made by hand — so a command can warn, record, and still exit 0, which a per-file warn roster cannot see because the defect is a call ABSENT from a file the list does not name. The durable half is the test to apply at the moment of any hoist: name the failure mode that was unrepresentable before, or say that none exists. The live instance is not hypothetical — `reportPropagatedRecompile` stays warn-only because two of its four callers answer for no recorded failure at all, which is a different reason from the one previously on record.
- `2026-08-21-the-link-citation-is-followed-almost-everywhere-and-checked-nowhere` — `{@link X}` means "resolve this" and a backtick does not; the split is followed almost everywhere in this package and checked nowhere. An unresolvable `{@link}` injected into an e2e spec passed `bun run typecheck` and `bun run lint` in silence. The census is nine unresolvable citations out of 252 (3.6%), so the backlog is fix-in-one-pass rather than roster-as-debt — but the three candidate instruments are each wrong in a different direction, and that is the durable half. `jsdoc/no-undefined-types` reads ESLint's scope rather than the type graph, so it catches four of nine, misses every `{@link import("./mod.js").thing}` namepath (which `{@link}` cannot parse at all), and condemns two class-member citations `tsc` resolves — a permanent tax on a correct construct. TypeDoc resolves all nine and emits more "resolved but not exported" warnings than defects. The exact instrument is a checker walk over `JSDocLink` nodes, which the row's "not a bespoke scanner" ruling was probably not aimed at. Two traps recorded for the next attempt: `ts.parseJsonConfigFileContent` ignores `extends` and silently built a program holding 212 of 413 files, and grepping the plugin's compiled rule for `link` returns zero while the rule reports links anyway. Convention landed in `documentation-bible.md`; no gate landed, because every reported site is in a file the lane did not own.
- `2026-08-21-an-eject-test-copies-from-a-fake-home-a-previous-test-already-deleted` — `eject.test.ts` goes red under load with `Could not copy N of 238 skills`, which reads as a skill-copy or cache fault in the product and is a fixture leak: one failing report named 150 source paths under a PREVIOUS test's fake home and one destination under the running test's, and the `cc-eject-test-` prefix has exactly one producer, so both directories are that file's own `beforeEach` temp dirs. Green alone (55/55) and green across the whole `commands` project (451/451); red only in a full three-project run. The lead, not proved and said so: the file isolates `$HOME` by env var alone and carries zero `homedir` spies, which `isolated-home.ts`'s own docblock calls insufficient under bun — "the two mechanisms are NOT interchangeable". Left unfixed on purpose; the durable half is the one-line detector nobody has — assert that every path a failure report names sits under the running test's own temp directory.
- `2026-08-21-resolving-every-backticked-name-would-have-missed-the-defect-that-asked-for-it` — the answer to whether the "document names a symbol that never was" class is mechanisable, measured rather than argued. The proposal was a checker resolving every backticked identifier in `.ai-docs` against source. Over `.ai-docs/reference/` that is 2707 distinct identifier-shaped names in 9782 occurrences; 2634 resolve across the seven source trees and 73 do not, as 88 (name, document) pairs. It fails twice on the very instance that motivated it. **It misses the defect**: three of the five phantom schema names — `skillIdSchema`, `categorySchema`, `agentNameSchema` — resolve as file-local non-exported consts in `packages/matrix/src/built-in-matrix.ts` and `built-in-agents.ts`, so a resolver answers "present" for all three while `boundary-map.md`'s claim that they live in `src/cli/lib/schemas.ts` stays wrong. **And its only two hits on that class are false positives**: `boundSkillSchema` and `skillSourceTypeSchema` occur in exactly one place in the corpus, the `zod-schemas.md` sentence saying they do not exist — the sentence that caught the defect. That is `briefing.md` § 3's ruling arriving as a measurement, and 45 of the 88 pairs (an undercount — the phrasings are open-ended) sit in a sentence whose subject IS the absence, with most of the rest naming things correctly outside the tree (`tsconfigRootDir`, `parserOptions`, `sideEffects`, `sumBy`). **Existence is not the predicate; existence AT THE NAMED LOCATION is**, and in the founding instance the location was a `File` column. The alternative proposed, not built: a **subset** verdict on `check-enumeration-drift.ts` — `table-pairs` over a symbol column and a file column, reporting `namedButAbsent` only and never `presentButUnnamed`, because these tables are deliberately partial where every existing reader binds an exhaustive claim in both directions. It is immune to both failure families by construction and the population is already shaped for it: 67 tables and 518 rows across `.ai-docs/reference/` pair an identifier with a `.ts` path, 22 of them in `boundary-map.md` alone. Its limit is stated rather than sold: it reads tables, so it would have caught one of CLI-610's three instances and neither of the two that were prose.
- `2026-08-21-git-cannot-tell-a-stale-tracker-row-from-one-recording-a-deletion` — the measured answer to whether CLI-623's gate can be built, taken before building it. Its premise holds (`check-finding-citations.ts` really does check only basenames) and its scoping rule does not: "the useful signal is a symbol that USED to exist, which git can answer" asks git a question git does not answer, because `git log -S` and a patch scan match a NAME in any removed line anywhere in seven workspaces of history rather than the row's subject. Two consequences, both measured over the whole of `todo/`. **Proposed symbols are flagged** — the five typed accessors a row asks somebody to write (`readProjectSkills`, `readProjectStack`, `readProjectAgents`, `readProjectDomains`, `readProjectConfig`), plus `envFlag` — which is exactly the class the scoping rule was written to spare. **And the filter selects FOR documents whose job is to record a removal**, since a row correctly saying a thing is gone is the likeliest row to name a symbol that used to exist: `archive.md` is the record of what landed, `CLI-450-source-switching-removal-map.md` says so in its title, and CLI-579 is flagged by a sentence reading "a type that no longer exists". Two of the five dispatches the row itself cites (D-307, D-214) are flagged whether their rows are right or wrong. On the most favourable scope available — live trackers only, excluding archive and plans — 17 candidates survive and at most 4 are the defect, so **roughly 24% precision with the misses concentrated in the protected class**. No gate was written, because one at that precision acquires an exclusion list in its first week and an exclusion list is the snapshot-of-a-moment the class is about. Same conclusion as the sibling audit `2026-08-21-resolving-every-backticked-name-would-have-missed-the-defect-that-asked-for-it`, reached independently from the tracker end rather than the document end: existence is not the predicate. What would work is stated so the next attempt does not restart from the refuted mechanism — resolve a symbol PAIRED WITH A PATH against that file, which needs no history at all, or make the citation explicit the way `{@link}` already is in source comments, which is an owner ruling of CLI-629's shape.
- `2026-08-21-a-selector-keyed-on-a-bare-callee-reaches-one-of-the-five-shapes-a-test-title-is-written-in` — the refinement its sibling `…-the-task-id-ban-holds-at-its-two-enforceable-locations-and-nowhere-else` licenses and does not make: the two selector-REACHABLE locations were not selector-REACHED. All three of `eslint.config.js`'s task-ID selectors key the call on `[callee.name=/^(describe|it|test)$/]`, and `callee.name` exists only for a bare `Identifier` callee — so `it.skip`, `it.only`, `it.each([…])(…)`, `describe.skipIf(…)(…)` and the tagged-template form each put a `MemberExpression` or a `CallExpression` there and escape, measured one planted title per shape through `ESLint.lintText` against the real config. Three more gaps in the same `value` regex, independent of the callee: `SKILLS-` is missing from its prefix list though `todo/skills.md` is one of the six trackers, the hyphen is mandatory so `d227` (the one spelling this repository actually broke the ban with) is not a match, and it is case-sensitive so `cli-551` and `p4-17` pass. Four of five shapes unreached, and 46 titles in this tree are already written behind a second call. **The durable half is why it was invisible: a selector that fires on nothing because there is nothing to fire on and a selector that fires on nothing because it cannot see the node file the same report** — the config declares the rule, the lint run is green, the title census is zero, and every angle a reader has says enforced. Closed for TITLES by one `it` in `spec-filenames.test.ts`: one in-process ESLint pass over every spec (~4s/435 files) under a config that REPLACES `eslint.config.js` rather than overriding it, since `no-restricted-syntax` takes options and does not merge across blocks; `noInlineConfig` so a spec cannot disable it; red-then-green proved by planting `describe.skip("CLI-593 …")` and `it.each([1])("d227 …")` in a real spec. Two of the sibling finding's three design constraints turned out to belong to the CONTENT gate rather than this one — `NAMES_A_TASK`'s eight lines are array members, not titles, so a title gate never sees them, and the `skills-2026` false positive is a body line — both implemented anyway because they cost nothing and the content gate will want them. `eslint.config.js` was not edited (another lane owned it) and the ASSERTION-MESSAGE half therefore remains bare-form-only, with the four-line replacement written out. Proposes the general rule beside `clean-code-standards.md` § 6.18: `[callee.name=X]` reads as "calls to X" and means "calls to X written as a bare identifier", so a `no-restricted-syntax` selector lands with a planted fixture per shape it claims to reach, in the same change.
- `2026-08-21-a-closed-union-refuses-a-marketplaces-own-slug-and-answers-with-somebody-elses-catalogue` — `skillRefInRules` held every slug a marketplace's `config/skill-rules.ts` names to the public catalogue's generated union, and Zod's enum text reports the options and never the input — so an author naming a skill they themselves ship was handed ~250 names belonging to someone else, with their own slug the one thing missing. Two corrections to CLI-502's own framing, both from driving the binary rather than reading: such a marketplace does not "work" at all (the rules file fails the whole load, so `search` exits non-zero and `Marketplace Reachable` fails), and the same union is applied one layer earlier to `metadata.yaml`'s own `slug`, which an author meets first — that one has an undocumented escape hatch in `custom: true`. Fixed for the rules side by an `error` function reaching `issue.input`; the metadata side is left because CLI-498 owns the question. A third defect recorded beside them: phase 3 of `validateSource` blames `config/skill-categories.ts` for every matrix-load failure, so an author whose `skill-rules.ts` will not parse is sent to open a file that is fine — `fileHoldingDefect` is an exhaustive switch precisely to stop that, and the catch sits outside it.
- `2026-08-21-a-fake-home-is-outlived-by-a-frozen-constant-and-a-detached-child` — the mechanism `…-an-eject-test-copies-from-a-fake-home-a-previous-test-already-deleted` left unproven, found and reproduced. TWO carriers, not one. `CACHE_DIR` and `GLOBAL_INSTALL_ROOT` in `src/cli/consts.ts` are computed from `os.homedir()` at MODULE LOAD, and `runCliCommand` drives oclif through `dist/`, a second module graph first imported by whichever test runs a command first — so both freeze to that test's fake home and every later test in the file reads and writes under a directory its own `afterEach` deleted; reproduced by reading `CACHE_DIR` out of the dist chunk in two consecutive tests. And `@oclif/plugin-warn-if-update-available` spawns a DETACHED `unref`ed child that `mkdir -p`s `<home>/.cache/agents-inc/version`, which outlives the test and recreates the tree after cleanup — the `ENOTEMPTY: rmdir` from the other side. Measured on `commands/eject.test.ts`: 26 resurrected temp directories per run, 0 with the update-check door closed, 1 with the door closed and the frozen constant still live — that one holding a full clone of the marketplace, which isolates the first carrier from the second. Also corrects the helper's own JSDoc: `vitest.setup.ts` already installs a process-wide `os.homedir` spy that honours `process.env.HOME`, so the env var DOES reach it under both runtimes; what removes it is `vi.restoreAllMocks()`, registered in a `beforeAll` and therefore gone for every later test in any file that restores mocks. The detached-writer half is fixed in `isolated-home.ts`; the two frozen constants are product code and are reported rather than changed.
- `2026-08-21-hand-written-plugin-state-is-not-uninstallable-so-a-departure-cannot-be-asserted` — widening `ProjectBuilder.pluginProject` to write `enabledPlugins` is necessary and is not sufficient, which is the half the tracker row did not have. Measured on Claude Code 2.1.239: `claude plugin uninstall` answers `not found in installed plugins` for hand-written state — settings.json alone, and settings.json plus an `installed_plugins.json` record at either scope — and `claudePluginUninstall` swallows exactly that wording by design, so an assertion placed on such a fixture accuses the product of the fixture's gap. A departure needs a REAL install upstream of it in the same test, which also supplies the subject guard the negative needs. Doing that surfaced a genuine inconsistency: after a real install and a real plugin→eject switch, the registry record is gone and `.claude/settings.json` still names the plugin enabled, so the project declares an enabled plugin that is not installed beside an `origin: "eject"` config entry. Registry departure now asserted and mutation-checked; the settings.json half is a KNOWN GAP at the site with an owner ruling owed.
- `2026-08-22-a-backticked-citation-is-still-a-citation-and-the-rule-reads-as-if-it-is-not` — two things the compiler-API walk found on its first run that the catalogue of non-resolving `{@link}` forms did not carry. A FOURTH form does not resolve: a bare name another module declares and this one does not import — it greps beautifully, ten call sites use it, and namepath resolution is lexical, so the jump lands nowhere. And backticks do not demote a citation: the JSDoc parser does not read them, so a rule written as _"a backtick is prose"_ invites a reading that is false, which the first draft of the checker proved by reporting six citations in its own docblock. Both amendments are in `documentation-bible.md` and both are now gated by `scripts/check-symbol-citations.ts`, which resolved 271 citations across the package's three tsconfig projects with two genuine defects and no false positive.
- `2026-08-22-a-spec-was-green-because-a-mock-split-one-path-into-a-writer-and-a-reader` — a spec pinning the STANDALONE `config-types.ts` branch passed for months over a state no run can produce: a file-wide `vi.mock` pointed the READER (`GLOBAL_INSTALL_ROOT`, via `getGlobalConfigTypesPath`) at `/tmp/nonexistent-global-root` while the WRITER (`globalPairPaths()`, always `os.homedir()`) wrote under the fake home, so the call under test created the global pair and was then asked about a different directory. The two never diverged in production. Making the constants call-time functions reconciled them and reddened the spec, which is how the premise was found — and what it was hiding is the opposite invariant, unpinned until now: a project-context write ESTABLISHES the global pair before generating the project's types, so the import form is the only outcome rather than a branch. The generalisable half is that an override redirecting ONE of several readings of the same fact manufactures a state the product cannot be in, and the tell is a spec asserting an ABSENCE the call under test is itself responsible for creating. No mechanical gate is proposed and the note says why: no type or call graph joins the two symbols, and what removed the class was deleting the divergence's subject.
- `2026-08-22-an-inert-fixture-leaves-every-spec-built-on-it-green` — one class from both ends. `ProjectBuilder.minimal()` wrote a `metadata.yaml` with no `category`, `completeMetadata` filled the `local` pseudo-category, and `local-skill-loader.ts` refuses that value unconditionally — so eighteen compile specs across six files compiled sub-agents from a project whose only skill was invisible to the matrix, with the refusal printed in their own output and `Domain`/`Category` emitted as `never`. Beside it, `compiler.test.ts` copied a fixture `agent.liquid` to `<temp>/src/agents/_templates/`, which is no root `createLiquidEngine` resolves, while the spec that looked like it covered template overriding asserted `typeof engine.renderFile === "function"`. Content no product path accepts and a location no product path looks at are the same defect: the fixture writes, the spec passes, and the artefact under test is not the one the product would have.
- `2026-08-22-a-reader-following-bindings-in-one-of-two-questions-condemns-what-it-accepts` — `producerRostersIn` asks two questions of every assertion — does it name the roster, does it reach the tree — and only the second followed the file's own bindings, so `expect(walk).toStrictEqual(SITES)` over a `SITES` mapped off the roster read as underived. It cost a lane a diagnosis and was worked around at the call site rather than fixed. The general half is the shipping rule rather than the bug: a widening that can only turn a verdict red→green cannot redden its own gate, so its suite going green is not evidence — it ships with one planted refusal fixture per shape it could newly reach.
- `2026-08-23-fixture-stack-categories-derived-from-id` — thirteen hand-written stack literals in four E2E files key an assignment on `"web-testing"` (once `"web-mocking"`) for a skill the catalogue puts in `"web-e2e"`, which is exclusive. The key was derived from the id's `web-testing-` prefix — the derivation `CLAUDE.md` forbids in fixtures as well as in product code — while the same fixtures write the CORRECT category into the metadata.yaml beside it, through the lookup that would have answered. It stayed invisible because `normalizeStackRecord` relocates the assignment on LOAD, so nothing reads the key that was written. Found by rendering fixture configs through the product's own writer and then reading them back: 19 of 150 configs fail that round trip, all four files, and three fail because the writer REFUSES a config with two skills in one exclusive category — so `dual-scope.e2e.test.ts` asserts a compiled sub-agent body no CLI-written configuration can produce. Filed rather than fixed: correcting the keys forces two exclusive skills into one stack, so one fixture skill has to change identity across five specs, which is a change to what they cover.
- `2026-08-24-an-exhaustive-list-states-a-count-its-checker-never-reads` — `check-enumeration-drift.ts` holds an exhaustive documentation list against the symbol it enumerates and does that job: adding `STEP_TEXT.DOCTOR_STATUS_SKIP` reddened two documents in the same run, naming the member. The list in `e2e-infrastructure.md` is introduced by a stated COUNT — `**185 members, exhaustive as listed:**` — and that number is prose the check does not read, so a complete list satisfies it whatever the sentence above claims. It stood at 185 against 187 actual before this session: two members had been appended correctly and the number in front of them had not moved, by the same hand, in the same file — only the half with a checker behind it stayed true. The census is one document, so this is a narrow gap rather than a class, and the finding prefers DELETING the count to enforcing it: the checker already guarantees the list is complete, the sibling `standards/e2e/README.md` states no total and has never drifted, and the repository's own ruling is to prefer deleting a claim to rewriting it. Count corrected to 187 while landing CLI-329; the gap itself is untouched.
- `2026-08-24-one-command-answered-the-same-count-two-ways-and-only-the-piped-half-was-wrong` — `List.run` branches on `process.stdin.isTTY` and derives the skill roster differently on each side: the interactive branch filters `config.skills` on `!s.excluded` and is the answer the owner ruled correct, while the piped branch prints `getInstallationInfo()`, whose `countInstalledSkills` asks the plugin registry for a `plugin` install and counts `.claude/skills/` directories otherwise. So one command shipped both the ruled behaviour and its contradiction, eleven lines apart, with nothing comparing them — and `mixed`, matching neither test, fell to the directory count and reported `Skills: 2` over a configuration declaring eleven. The dashboard reads the same wrong producer, so two of three surfaces were wrong and the third was already right. The class is on the record once already (`todo/archive.md`, `2026-08-24 — CLI-816`, the two dashboard paths); what that fix did not do is ask the same question of `list`. Proposes the rule on the mechanism rather than on TTY — a branch no spec reaches is a second implementation nobody is holding to the first — plus a cheap `isTTY`-in-commands scan, measured on `list` alone and stated as a sample.
- `2026-08-24-a-population-gate-rested-on-a-comment-a-product-removal-was-told-to-delete` — `check-finding-citations.test.ts` holds a population gate: each of its three scopes must cite at least one finding that resolves, because a scope reading NOTHING reports exactly what a scope whose every citation resolves reports. The gate is right; what it rested on was one COMMENT. On 2026-08-24 the whole of `packages/cli/e2e`'s population was the note above `STEP_TEXT.ONLY_SKILL_IN_CATEGORY` in `e2e/pages/constants.ts`, and CLI-822's brief correctly told a product lane to delete that constant — so the scope's citation count went to zero and a repository gate reddened in a lane whose brief named no documentation scope and whose author had no reason to look. The class is that a gate asserting "this directory is non-empty for property P" is coupled to every deletion anywhere in the directory, invisibly from the deletion end: nothing at the call site said the comment was load-bearing, and no grep a product brief would run reaches prose. Filed rather than fixed — the gate's own docblock rules that moving the value is worth a second look rather than a silent edit, so whether `packages/cli/e2e` should acquire a real citation or has legitimately stopped citing is an owner decision. Prefers putting the diagnosis in the assertion message over marking the comment.
- `2026-08-24-a-resolution-note-for-a-deletion-cannot-name-what-was-deleted` — `TEMPLATE.md` tells a resolving agent to cite the mechanism of resolution in `resolved_by:`, and for the commonest resolution shape — a fix that deleted or renamed something — the natural note names what went. `check-findings-frontmatter.ts` extracts backticked identifiers from EVERY frontmatter field and holds them against the working tree, so that note reddens a repository gate whose only escapes are rephrasing or appending to a pin in a test file a documentation pass may not edit. Reproduced while resolving the `list` skill-count finding: naming the four removed counting helpers produced four undeclared symbols and one red suite; naming the surviving producer instead cost nothing, because the body already spells the dead names and the scan does not read the body. The tension is acknowledged in exactly one place no author of a note will read — a docblock arguing that a rename note unable to spell the old name says nothing, which pins rather than rephrases. Four findings have now hit it and all four grew the pin. Proposes the rule on deletions only and flags the rename half as an owner call, since that docblock has a point about renames that does not carry to deletions.
- `2026-08-25-a-mocked-collaborator-can-make-loads-and-validates-assert-neither` — `matrix-loader.test.ts` mocks `loadConfig`, the function that owns `schema.safeParse`, then asserts the loader returned what the mock was handed; two specs name validation in their titles while the schema never runs, and the code under test reduces to one property access. Invisible to every gate — it type-checks, lints, passes, and survives a field being retired from the schema precisely because the schema is absent. The retired `required` flag sitting on both sides of the `toStrictEqual` was the only visible symptom, and removing that residue deleted the symptom while leaving the shape. Proposes, alongside the arity/length/absence rule it sits next to: a spec whose name claims validation must exercise the validator, or say what it really checks. Census of three files mocking a loader; the other two are clean and one models the honest title.
- `2026-08-25-an-optional-match-for-a-deleted-token-is-green-forever` — a product removal deleted a glyph the build-grid category header used to paint between a category's display name and its pick-one counter. The assertion matching it HARD went red and was fixed; an OPTIONAL match for the same glyph inside `getExclusiveCategorySelectedCount`'s own regex, and the same accommodation in the docblock above it, could not go red at all — an optional match for something that can never appear is green forever, and neither `tsc` nor the suite can see one. Carries the census half as well: a sweep written as a basic regular expression matches the RENDERED form of a token and misses the SOURCE form, where a regex literal puts a backslash before it, so `grep -F` finds one and plain `grep` the other and neither finds both.
- `2026-08-26-a-writer-branch-with-no-production-caller-is-pinned-by-a-spec-block` — `generateConfigSource` branches three ways on its options object and one branch, the import-from-global form, has no production caller: `writeConfigFile` is the only site that forwards options, its only caller always passes `globalConfig` alongside `isProjectConfig`, and that pair routes to the inlining branch every time. The dead branch nonetheless carries a long, well-written, entirely green spec block, which is what hides it — per-branch coverage reads as evidence a branch is live and it is the only evidence most readers look for. No gate can see it: `tsc` sees an imported export, ESLint sees a called private function, the suite sees full coverage, and `knip` is deliberately not a gate. The cost landed on architecture rather than on bytes — the editor-v6 preview decision was written around "three writer variants, three byte shapes" and around an absolute machine-specific path emitted only by this branch, and an extraction pass would have moved the branch, its helper and its specs. Filed rather than fixed: whether to delete it or give it back a caller is an owner call, since the import form produces a smaller project `config.ts` than the inlined snapshot. Prefers a docblock naming each branch's production call site over a checker, and says the census that would justify a checker has not been run.
- `2026-08-26-a-token-named-for-one-panel-holds-the-palettes-primary-ink` — `globals.css` binds the design's two ink names the wrong way round: `--color-ink` holds the design's `dark` (`#242320`), while the design's `ink` (`#161513`, its **primary text** colour) is reachable only as `--color-roster-ink`, a name claiming it belongs to one panel. A census of the shipped prototype finds ten distinct selectors carrying `#161513`; six are inside the roster, which is why the name has held, and **three are outside it and all three are being built right now** under EDITOR-09's phase programme — the preview dialog's tree twice, and the composer's active mode chip in the main column. Editor consumption is still four sites in one file, which is the window the finding is filed in: done now it is mechanical, done later it is a seven-site change across three surfaces. Nothing would catch it — a wrong-but-existing custom property type-checks, lints, builds and renders, and there is no drift check binding `globals.css` to the design's palette table; the file's own header claims one and cites a section that does not exist, which is a second symptom of the same absent binding. Proposes the rename as its own change (it crosses into `apps/www`, whose stylesheet states no colour is repeated there) plus a rule: namespace a token by surface only when the value is genuinely specific to that surface, since the prefix is an unenforceable claim that no other surface may use it.
- `2026-08-26-a-vitest-workspace-that-collects-only-stories-swallows-a-unit-test` — `packages/ui` declares `"test": "vitest run"` and its config declares exactly one project, the Storybook browser one, whose test list the addon derives from `stories: ["../src/**/*.stories.tsx"]`. No project has an `include` matching a `.test.ts`, so a unit test anywhere under `src/` is never collected and the workspace still exits 0: a run with a 19-assertion `src/lib/syntax-theme.test.ts` sitting on disk reported `11 passed (11) / 63 passed (63)`, every one of them a story. The two gates that normally notice a stopped test both read the file and pass — `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` reported on it — and `deps:check` positively classifies the workspace as `opted-out`, printing the `//no-shared-vitest-config` note as evidence the divergence is deliberate; it is, and the note records only the browser half, not that opting out also opted out of ever collecting pure logic. Census comes back clean at one: `apps/editor`, `apps/server` and `packages/matrix` all build from `@workspace/vitest-config/node`, and `packages/cli` declares its own projects over `src/**` and `scripts/**`. The class is already diagnosed exactly in `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md`, for `e2e/helpers/`, and scoped to that tree — so the rule that describes this defect is on the page and points at a different workspace. Filed rather than fixed: the fix is a second project plus a devDependency plus an amended opt-out note, all in another lane's files. Proposes generalising the existing rule off `e2e/helpers/`, and a verification habit — a new test file's first run must show its own NAME, since `Tests 63 passed` reads identically whether the file was collected or ignored. Prefers that to a checker, whose census is one and which would have to resolve a Storybook addon's derived test list to avoid reporting this workspace forever.

- `2026-08-26-an-e2e-spec-cannot-land-green-inside-a-lane-that-does-not-own-the-journey-page` — `spec-gates.test.ts` requires every file the `e2e` project collects to be named on `user-journeys.md` or listed in `SPECS_BELONGING_TO_NO_JOURNEY`, a list its own docblock says may only shrink. Both live outside `e2e/`, so **adding one e2e spec is a two-file change whose second file belongs to no lane** — and a brief that names its lane exclusively, as `briefing.md` requires whenever more than one agent is live, cannot include it. Observed while a Phase B lane created the one e2e spec its brief named by path: the gate went red with `+ "lifecycle/preview-matches-install"` and all three escapes were closed to the agent — edit the page (out of lane), extend the backlog list (out of lane and forbidden by its contract), or move the spec where the project does not collect it (the failure `e2e/vitest.config.ts` already records for `smoke/` going unrun for months). Neither standard is wrong on its own: `briefing.md` never says a file list must reach the gates the change will trip, and `user-journeys.md` never says who writes a row for a spec an implementation lane creates. Filed rather than fixed, because both repairs are in another lane's files. Proposes two lines in `briefing.md` — a list reaches its gates, and a gate a lane cannot legally satisfy is a brief defect rather than an agent failure — and rejects the alternative of a tolerance window in the gate, since the backlog only shrinking is the whole of its value.
- `2026-08-26-a-re-export-facade-unbinds-every-gate-keyed-on-a-modules-path` — extracting the config-pair and compiled-agent renderers into `@workspace/compile` turned seven CLI modules into re-export facades; every call site kept working and `tsc` and `eslint` stayed green, while **seven separate repository gates went red and each named a different subject** — two enumeration-drift bindings (`exports: "function"` reads DECLARATIONS, and a facade declares none), a boundary-cast backlog entry that stopped offending, a spec name citing `DOMAIN_ORDER` after the last non-spec module holding it left, the config-gate write-privileged zone and its private-import allowlist, `tested-exports-reach-production` after `getGlobalConfigImportPath` lost its last production caller, and two ESLint zones. The class: **a gate keyed on a module's PATH is keyed on where a symbol is DECLARED, not on where it is READ FROM**, and a facade is exactly the change that separates the two. The ESLint import ban is the one that would have failed SILENTLY — a `group` matching nothing reports nothing and `reportUnusedDisableDirectives` cannot see it. Proposes a census command in `briefing.md` rather than a checker (the seven gates share a path string, not a construct), and records the design pressure it produced: a module that is half facade and half implementation is unbindable by either `exports` or `reexports`, which is why `config-types-io.ts` was split out of `config-types-writer.ts`.
- `2026-08-26-a-config-entrys-own-key-order-is-the-producers-so-the-writer-is-not-a-fixed-point` — `cleanForEmission` canonicalises the config's top-level field order and `canonicalizeStackOrder` canonicalises the stack's, and the docblock claims the result is a fixed point; one level down, each element of `skills` and `agents` is `JSON.stringify(entry)`, so an entry's own key order is its producer's. Three producers disagree: the wizard mints a tombstone as `{ id, scope, excluded, origin }`, `maskCollidingGlobalSkills` spreads one into `{ id, scope, origin, excluded }`, and the Zod loader rebuilds every entry in `SkillConfig`'s declaration order — so writing a config and re-rendering it after a load produces different bytes for identical values. Reproduced in-process in four lines, no install needed. Invisible to every config-level check, because both configs deep-equal each other and only the bytes differ; it surfaced only when a second surface was asked to draw the same bytes, in editor-v6's `preview-matches-install.e2e.test.ts`. Live CLI defect as well as a preview one: an `edit` that touches nothing rewrites a tombstoned skill's line. The existing `CLAUDE.md` rule covers "a record of records" and its live example is the stack; an array ELEMENT is the same problem and is not covered by the sentence as written. Proposes widening that rule to array elements, and a test shape nobody writes — a round trip inside ONE installation (render → write → load → render → compare the two strings), which is the only thing that would have failed on the day the tombstone shape landed.
- `2026-08-26-a-store-on-zustands-default-storage-has-no-persist-handle-in-node` — `apps/editor`'s unit suite runs on `environment: "node"`, and zustand's persist middleware resolves its storage once at store creation with a default of `createJSONStorage(() => window.localStorage)`. `createJSONStorage` swallows the resulting `ReferenceError` and returns `undefined`, and `persistImpl` then returns **before** `api.persist` is ever assigned. The two consequences fail in opposite directions: anything asking the store about its own persistence is unreachable, because `store.persist` is `undefined` and `store.persist.getOptions()` throws a `TypeError` naming zustand rather than the missing browser; and anything calling only ACTIONS passes while persisting nothing, because `set` still runs and only the write is gone. So a test that changes state and then claims something about what was saved is vacuously green, and the only signal is a `console.warn` per `set` that no assertion in the suite subscribes to. Surfaced while writing the first `ui-store.test.ts`, whose whole subject is a persisted-schema migration hazard.
- `2026-08-26-an-e2e-set-locator-keyed-on-a-bare-aria-attribute-widens-silently` — `roster.spec.ts` reached the roster's sticky bands as `button[aria-expanded]` inside the panel scroller and asserted band N pins at N × the first match's height. Exact when written; Phase A put a grouping menu in the panel header inside the same scroller, and Base UI writes `aria-expanded` on a menu trigger **unconditionally**, so the trigger — which precedes the bands — became `first()` and `step` became 14 instead of 26. The failure mode worth recording is not that the test broke but **how**: an attribute-only locator standing in for "the set of X" does not report that it has stopped meaning X. It keeps resolving, keeps counting, and starts measuring a different element — had the new control been 26px tall the suite would have stayed green while asserting nothing about band 5. Fixed by scoping through the `section` each band sits in.
- `2026-08-26-a-discriminated-union-on-component-props-collapses-storybook-args-to-never` — making an invalid prop pairing unrepresentable, which is what CLAUDE.md asks for elsewhere, compiles and leaves the component correct, and then breaks the STORIES file in a way none of the component's own gates would report: Storybook's `Meta<typeof Component>` intersects the union's arms, `args` collapses to `never`, and the errors land on stories that predate the change and pass none of the new props. Five errors, two of them on untouched stories. Recorded because the idiom is repository-wide and the cost is invisible from the component — the union was written, reverted, and replaced with two distinctly named optional slots, with the rejected shape and its reason kept in the component's docblock.
- `2026-08-26-the-binding-vi-mock-needs-hoisted-is-the-one-in-its-argument-list` — `packages/compile/src/node-free.test.ts` is the only mechanical check that the package is browser-safe and the only one that can catch a **transitive** node builtin, and it had never run: `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'record' before initialization`, reported as `src/node-free.test.ts (0 test)`. The file declared two mutually-referencing bindings and hoisted the wrong one — `trap`, which is read only inside the factory and therefore runs at import time when everything has initialised, carried `vi.hoisted`; `record`, which is _invoked in the `vi.mock(...)` argument list_ and so is dereferenced above every `const` in the file, did not. The rule that separates them is argument-list-versus-factory-body, and the author applied the ceremony to the safe binding on the adjacent line. What the failure cost is meaning rather than visibility — CI does reach this workspace, so the run was red, but a reader seeing `1 failed` cannot tell that the file contributed zero assertions about its subject and that the browser-safety property was simply unverified. Fixed, then **proved it can fail**: a transitive node reach was planted in `src/string.ts` (reached only through `config-types-source.ts`) and removed, leaving the file byte-identical. The two probe shapes redden it differently and only one reaches the gate's own assertion — a named import (`import { sep } from "node:path"`) dies on vitest's "No 'sep' export is defined on the 'path' mock" because the factories return `{}`, so the commonest real shape produces a message about a mock rather than about a browser, with `trap.reached` still empty; observed and left, since the contract that a node reach cannot pass holds either way. Census is exact at one file, thirteen lines, via a two-stage grep that first narrows to `vi.mock` calls carrying a factory and then drops every inline arrow / `async` arrow / object literal. Proposes the argument-list rule for `clean-code-standards.md`, plus a habit: a gate written after the fact has no red phase unless somebody manufactures one, which generalises `CLAUDE.md`'s "a test that has never failed has not been shown to test anything" off the tests-first workflow it is currently stated about. Deliberately not merged with `2026-08-26-a-vitest-workspace-that-collects-only-stories-swallows-a-unit-test` — that is the workspace-level version where the run is GREEN and the file is absent from the output entirely; here the file is named with `(0 test)` beside it and the run is red, so the two have different tells and different fixes.
- `2026-08-26-an-import-ban-repointed-at-the-package-missed-the-cli-module-the-same-split-created` — `CONFIG_WRITER_IMPORTS` in `eslint.config.js` refuses four renderer symbols by import specifier, and one of them, `regenerateConfigTypes`, is no longer DECLARED in any module the ban names: the `@workspace/compile` extraction moved it to `src/cli/lib/configuration/config-types-io.ts`, which matches neither `**/config-types-writer*` nor `@workspace/compile/config-types-source`. Reproduced with `eslint --stdin` — the probe naming the facade is refused and the probe naming the declaring module is accepted, so the rule looks like it is working from every angle a reader has. No production site used the hole, which is precisely the silence its predecessor warned about: a `group` matching nothing reports nothing and `reportUnusedDisableDirectives` sees only directives. The sharp part is provenance — `config-types-io.ts` exists BECAUSE of `2026-08-26-a-re-export-facade-unbinds-every-gate-keyed-on-a-modules-path`, which diagnosed this exact class, named the new module in its own prose, and proposed the census command that would have found this; **a remedy that moves a declaration is itself an instance of the class it is remedying**. Fixed by adding both spellings of the declaring module to the group while keeping the facade spellings (a re-export must not become the bypass — B1.5's stated principle). Census of the other three banned names run rather than assumed: each is declared in a module the group already matches, exact at one. Proposes one line on the predecessor's unlanded census habit — run it for the module a change CREATES as well as the one it empties, since the emptied module is the one everybody remembers. Notes that `config-writer-import-ban.test.ts` has no probe for `config-types-io`, so nothing mechanically holds the restored line.
- `2026-08-26-a-discriminated-union-widens-its-own-discriminant-at-every-un-annotated-call-site` — generalises `2026-08-26-a-discriminated-union-on-component-props-collapses-storybook-args-to-never` off component props, with a different mechanism and no tool involved. `ConfigSourceOptions` admitted `{ isProjectConfig: true }` with neither `globalConfig` nor `globalImportPath` and answered it from the standalone writer, returning a global-shaped config carrying the `projects` tracking array a project root must never hold. The union that makes the pairing unrepresentable was written first, because that is the stated preference, and measured: `packages/cli`'s `tsc` and its e2e project both stay at zero, and `packages/compile` reports `TS2345 … Type 'boolean' is not assignable to type 'true'` at the one site that builds the options in an un-annotated `const` — a call that is correct in every way that matters, failing with a message about inference rather than about the mistake. Discriminating on `isProjectConfig` requires its LITERAL type and an object literal with no contextual type widens it; discriminating on `globalConfig`'s presence instead leaves the defect legal. What the two findings share is the shape of the cost rather than the mechanism: **a union's blast radius lands in a file its author never opened, invisible to the declaring module's own gates.** Reverted; the pairing is refused at runtime in `generateProjectConfig`, as the exhaustive arm of the three-way choice rather than a guard bolted onto the orchestrator, with the rejected union and its compiler message kept in the type's docblock. Records the price rather than a verdict — the union cost exactly one line in one test file the lane did not own.
- `2026-08-26-a-hand-verified-half-of-a-fix-is-an-unpinned-half` — `2026-08-26-a-config-entrys-own-key-order-is-the-producers-so-the-writer-is-not-a-fixed-point` closed `resolved`, and its `resolved_by:` says the agent-side twin of the fix was "fixed in the same pass and hand-verified". It was: removing the agent arm of `withEntriesInSchemaOrder` in `packages/compile/src/config-source.ts` left 217/217 files and 7337 tests green, so a load-bearing fix was owned by a sentence. The recurring part is WHY nobody pinned it — a 19-hit census across 6 files shows no production producer of an `AgentScopeConfig` disagrees with the loader's key order (every one writes `excluded` last or appends it by spread), so the only order-disagreeing producer in the tree is the test factory `buildAgentConfigs`, which puts `excluded` before `model` and `effort`. That makes the pin writable and makes it fragile: tidy the factory and the fixed-point comparison agrees with itself forever. Landed the pin plus an `Object.keys` guard on the input's key order, so a factory change reddens on the line that owns the assumption instead of going vacuous. Proposes that a `resolved_by:` naming a hand-verification say the consequence — nothing will report a regression — rather than the method; declines a checker and says the census that would justify one has not been run.
- `2026-08-26-a-partially-emptied-module-keeps-its-copy-where-the-emptied-module-guard-cannot-look` — the `@workspace/compile` extraction moved `byCategoryDeclarationOrder` and **both** its callers into that package, and left the CLI's declaration of it in `src/cli/lib/matrix/matrix-provider.ts` with **zero callers repo-wide**. The bodies were semantically identical, so nothing was wrong in the bytes; what was wrong is that `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` named that exact declaration as _"where the rule itself lives so the builder and the writer cannot disagree about it"_ — the sentence whose whole purpose was to prevent a second copy had become the sentence advertising one, and two reference docs agreed with it. Three gates are each out of population for a different reason: `tested-exports-reach-production` scans exports the SUITE invokes and production does not, and nothing invoked this at all; `knip` would report it and is deliberately not a gate; and `renderers-come-from-the-shared-package.test.ts` is exactly the right shape — its `EMPTIED_MODULES` pairs a module with symbols that must no longer be DECLARED in it, and its docblock says private helpers are listed because that is where a copy actually survives — but all six entries are modules the extraction emptied **whole**, and this one was emptied of one symbol while legitimately keeping seven. **The class: a guard keyed on the modules a change EMPTIED shares its population with the human reviewer, and a partially emptied module is in neither** — it still declares things, so the guard has no entry for it, and it still reads as a live module, so nobody thinks of it as gone. Census exact at one: all 22 symbols declared in both `packages/compile/src` and `packages/cli/src` have a CLI-side caller (floor `addToDomainSelections` at one, ceiling `validateSelection` at 63), the six same-named locals checked rather than dismissed by eye. Deleted rather than re-exported, and the three prose sites repointed — `skills-and-matrix.md` as a NEGATIVE claim, since a reader arriving from the old sentence needs telling where it went. Enforcement half deliberately unlanded: proposes the one-line `EMPTIED_MODULES` entry (in another lane's file, and with the instruction to manufacture its red phase first), a third case for the census habit `a-re-export-facade-unbinds-every-gate-keyed-on-a-modules-path` and `an-import-ban-repointed-at-the-package-missed-the-cli-module-the-same-split-created` have been building — empties, creates, and now **partially** empties — and a rule for `documentation-bible.md`: an instruction naming a symbol's LOCATION as the reason a rule is centralised is a pointer with no referential integrity, and moving the symbol inverts its meaning silently.
- `2026-08-26-a-playwright-spec-importing-an-absent-module-collects-zero-tests-suite-wide` — the repository's tests-first order is safe under Vitest and not under Playwright, and the two were measured against the same absence in the same package. A red-first `apps/editor/e2e/specs/output-preview.spec.ts` importing its not-yet-written subject produced one line — `Error: Cannot find package '@/features' imported from …` — and **no count of any kind**: no project started, and all 353 passing tests across the suite's other 33 files were skipped by one unresolvable import in a thirty-fourth. Vitest, given the identical absence, isolated it and still reported `17 passed (20)` with the two unloadable files named and `(0 test)` beside them. What makes it worth a row is that **the exit code cannot separate the aborted run from the one where 27 assertions genuinely failed** — both are 1, and Playwright's reporters do not run when collection aborts, so there is no file list to read either. The neighbouring rule in `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` covers the Vitest tell only. The import is not gratuitous and "do not import app source from a spec" is the wrong lesson: a Playwright spec runs in Node, and reaching the app's own pure model is what lets a rendered assertion bind to the code that produced the bytes rather than to a transcription of them. Proposes an ORDERING rule for `standards/e2e/README.md` — stand the module up first, then watch the assertions fail, because the loader's red phase is not evidence about the test — and declines a checker, since anything that would count collected specs runs after the collection that aborted.
- `2026-08-26-a-chunk-group-ranked-above-the-catalogue-swallows-it-and-lands-a-lazy-chunk-on-first-paint` — `apps/editor/vite.config.ts` already carried the rule in its own docblock — _"a group captures its matches' dependencies as well as its matches"_, with `vendor` outranking `catalog` so the catalogue's chunk does not swallow zod — and it still did not transfer, because it is written as a fact about ONE PAIR whose order the reader is asked to preserve rather than as a constraint every new group is placed against. A `compile` group added at priority 20, above `catalog` at 10, claimed `@workspace/matrix` along with `packages/compile`; the eager half made the merged chunk statically reachable from the entry, and the 226 KB gzipped vendored agent corpus — reached in source through nothing but `import()`, and behind a SECOND `import()` even there — landed on the first-paint path: `First paint is 533.8 KB gzipped, 203.8 KB over the 330.0 KB budget`. **Every source-level check was green while that was true**: the dialog's static-graph trap passed, and a repo-wide grep finds `@workspace/compile` in one lazily-imported module and nowhere else. The laziness was undone downstream by a number in a config file, and the only thing that reported it was the budget plugin, which names the chunk and not the cause. Fixed by ranking the group at 5, below `catalog`; measured both ways with the budget temporarily at zero (533.8 KB against 331.4 KB). A second consequence surfaced in the same measurement and is recorded rather than absorbed: stripping the phase's three static additions reads 329.5 KB, so the feature is +1.9 KB and the 330 KB ceiling had 0.5 KB of room rather than the ~6% its own comment claimed — the budget moved to 352 KB with all three figures written into its docblock. Declines a checker on the grounds that the one that exists caught it on the first build; proposes only that the placement rule be restated as a question a new group must answer.
- `2026-08-26-a-design-pixel-asserted-against-a-rendered-box-is-wrong-wherever-the-app-has-a-sizing-knob` — `packages/ui/src/styles/globals.css` sets `font-size: 110%` and calls it THE SIZING KNOB, naming **column widths** among what it scales and borders and viewport units as the only two exemptions. So a 250px design column is written `w-[15.625rem]` and renders at 275, exactly as `DialogContent`'s `wide` is `w-[38.75rem]` for a 620px design and `DialogPane side="right"` is `w-[12.25rem]` for 196px — not one shipped conversion renders at the number it was named for. `apps/editor/e2e/specs/output-preview.spec.ts` asserts `expect(tree?.width).toBe(250)` against `boundingBox()`, which reports CSS pixels, and the spec's own comment says "the number is asserted on the rendered box" — so the author knew which side they were measuring and not that the two sides differ. Both ways of satisfying it are wrong silently: pin the column in `px` and it stops scaling while its own row height, font, padding and indent still do, losing the designed proportion (250/760 is 32.9% of the design's dialog; a 250px column in the rendered 836px sheet is 29.9%) and quietly falsifying the docblock's "column widths" clause. Census rather than sample — fifteen `boundingBox` hits across six spec files, and these are the only two comparing a box to a literal; every other geometry assertion in the suite is RELATIVE and therefore immune to the knob entirely. Discovery only, deliberately: the product keeps the rem and the assertion is reported rather than edited, since the verifier is never the fixer and bending a product to satisfy a wrong test hides the error where nobody looks. Proposes a rule for `standards/e2e/assertions.md` — prefer relative geometry, and where an absolute number is the subject derive it from the design value AND the knob in the spec so both are visible — and declines a checker, because deciding whether a literal is a design pixel or a rendered one needs a browser. Sits beside `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md`'s rule against binding a rendering assertion to the constant the product renders without contradicting it: mirror the product's strings, convert the product's numbers.
- `2026-08-26-a-page-objects-multi-element-read-is-non-retrying-so-it-reports-a-loading-screen-as-an-empty-one` — `allInnerTexts`, `allTextContents`, `evaluateAll` and `count` are documented as NON-RETRYING: Playwright's auto-waiting is a property of actions and of `expect`, never of a locator, so each answers `[]` or `0` for a selector matching nothing rather than waiting for it to match something. `OutputPreviewDialog.rowNames` and `.rowPaths` in `apps/editor/e2e/pages/dialogs.ts` ran immediately after `previewButton.click()` while the model behind the tree arrives through `import()` **by design** — `useOutputPreview` returns `{ status: "loading" }` until the chunk resolves and `Preview` renders its whole shell in that state, tree pane mounted and zero rows inside it. So the legitimate loading DOM is byte-identical to "the tree drew nothing". The assertion on the other end is `toStrictEqual` against a named constant, which is the RIGHT assertion and is why this fails loudly rather than silently — but the message names a nineteen-member expected list against an empty received one, which reads as a product bug and not as a read taken 40ms early. Currently green across two full runs, which is the worst state to leave a structural race in. The existing `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` rule is the same mechanism and the same remedy stated for WRITES (a key press that no-ops), and its closing clause — "non-wizard page objects need their own screen-specific sentinel" — is the sentence that covers this, written as a parenthetical on a rule whose subject is a key press, so nobody writing a getter reads it. Fixed with a `waitForTree()` sentinel on the first ROW rather than on the pane, because the pane is mounted in all three states and a row exists only in `ready` — a locator `waitFor` rather than an `expect`, per `roster-panel.ts`'s stated "locating is this file's job; expecting is the spec's". Census exact at four page-object sites, and the two left alone are the interesting half: `lines()` must NOT get a sentinel because `[]` is a legitimate expected value there (`a directory row shows an empty pane` asserts exactly it), which is why the proposed rule is phrased as a question — is an empty answer a possible RESULT, or only a possible EARLINESS — rather than as an API blacklist. Proposes a grep-based gate over `e2e/pages/**` that would REPORT rather than refuse, since two of its four hits are correct; not landed, and says so.
- `2026-08-26-a-shared-scenario-contract-counts-its-runners-nowhere-so-a-third-implementation-joined-with-none` — `SELECTION_SCENARIOS` exists **because two implementations of one rule diverged**, and its header says so; two runners existed and each opens by naming the other and calling the arrangement bilateral. The `@workspace/compile` extraction then created `packages/compile/src/selection.ts` — a THIRD live implementation of conflicts, requirements and exclusive categories, called on every `seed-to-config` decode — with no runner and no test file of any kind. **Nothing reported it, because a contract's coverage is asserted in prose**: both bilateral docblocks stayed literally true (the two runners they name still run) while the population they describe went from complete to two-thirds, and every gate was green — `tsc` sees three modules that compile, the two runners pass because their subjects did not move, and the compile package's own suite collected 8 tests with the fourth file simply absent, which is not a state a runner can report. The mapping needed DERIVING rather than copying, and that is the recurring part: the other two runners ask a REACHABILITY question that forgives what a pick-one swap resolves, `validateSelection` asks a VALIDITY question about the set as handed, so the obvious `inReach ⇒ valid` mapping is false — a pick-one sibling is in reach precisely because clicking it swaps rather than adds. The three claims that hold were probed across all 20 scenarios rather than assumed (closure carries no conflict or `categoryExclusive`, 0 violations; out-of-reach produces a `missingRequirement` naming it, 18/18; an in-reach conflict is contained by a `categoryExclusive` in the same result, 8/8), and the third implementation AGREES on all three — no divergence, scenario file untouched. Landed with its red phase MANUFACTURED, because a contract runner is green the day it is written and "a test that has never failed has not been shown to test anything" otherwise has nothing to satisfy it: three mutated copies of the implementation, one rule broken in each, 4/9/5 failures respectively, each claim caught by a different mutation, all copies deleted. Notes that `emission-scenarios.ts` is the same pattern one layer down with the same unmeasured property. Proposes a declared runner roster in the scenario module — the shape of `EMPTIED_MODULES` — plus the rule that extracting a rule creates implementation N+1 and the runner is part of the extraction; the checker is not landed and the manual grep substitute is given.
- `2026-08-26-a-sentinel-is-a-legal-value-of-the-field-and-an-invalid-coordinate` — `SkillConfig.origin` carries a marketplace NAME for a plugin skill and the sentinel `EJECT_SOURCE` (`"eject"`) for an ejected one, both as plain `string`, and the output preview's ejected-directory note interpolated it into `Source: ${origin}/src/skills/${id}` — rendering `Source: eject/src/skills/web-framework-react`, a repository that does not exist. **The site that renders it is the one site where the sentinel is the only reachable value**, since the note exists solely to explain an ejected directory: 100% reproducible, never intermittent, and still invisible, because a sentinel does not produce a crash or a blank but a well-formed plausible path. Two things carried it through review — the docblock asserted the false half in as many words (_"built from the skill's own `origin`, which is the marketplace name the config records for it"_, true of the plugin note thirty lines below and false of the function it sat on, so a reader checking code against comment finds agreement), and nothing in the type distinguishes the arms, so no narrowing exists to forget and `tsc` has no opinion. It matters here specifically because the dialog's whole premise is byte-honesty — _"the moment someone diffs it against reality and it is off, they stop trusting the configurator"_. Census returns exactly two sites and the OTHER ONE IS CORRECT: `pluginReferenceNote`, in the same module, renders for a plugin skill where `origin` genuinely is the name — two identical expressions side by side, one right, which is why the mistake reads as consistent. Nearest existing rule is `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md`'s `displayName`-in-a-path ban, the same class stated for one field, and unfindable by anyone not grepping for `displayName`. Fixed by resolving the coordinate from the seated catalogue through a named `primarySourceName` mirroring `sourceForSkill`; pinned by "names the marketplace an ejected skill is copied from, never the eject sentinel", which reads the marketplace off the origin the DECODE recorded for the same skill installed as a plugin, so two answers that disagree redden it. **A second half worth the row on its own**: calling the single implementation was written and reverted, because exporting `sourceForSkill` from `packages/compile/src/seed-to-config.ts` reddened `check-enumeration-drift.test.ts` with `presentButUnnamed: ["sourceForSkill"]`, and closing that needs the exhaustive table in `reference/config/scope-split.md` AND the checker's `from:` anchor, which carries the ordinal _"so a seventh export cannot land without this table naming it:"_ — so a new export from a drift-bound module is a three-file change across two workspaces, and an ordinal inside an anchor has to move every time the table grows.
- `2026-08-26-a-budget-whose-margin-is-a-percentage-stops-being-a-gate` — `apps/editor/scripts/first-paint-budget.ts` names the defect it exists to catch in its own header and cites `posthog-js` at 74 KB as the case that proved it; the ceiling was then raised 330 → 352 KiB for a feature the same docblock measured at +1.9 KB, on the stated ground of restoring _"~6% over the measurement"_. **Six percent of this payload is 19.9 KB, and 19.9 KB is a library** — `react` is 60.2 KB gzipped on this very first-paint path, `catalog` 47.9, `observability` 28.8, so a dependency a third the size of the file's own worked example arrives green. The general shape is the keeper: a margin expressed as a FRACTION of the measurement grows with the thing it constrains, so every legitimate addition raises the absolute slack and raises how much can arrive unnoticed next time. The previous ceiling was wrong in the other direction for the same reason — 330 KiB against a 329.5 KB payload left 0.5 KB, so a genuine 2 KB feature broke the build; both readings came of choosing a percentage instead of asking what the gate must let through. All three figures re-derived with the budget temporarily at zero, `bun run build` and `npx vite build` agreeing on each (the property the 2026-08-21 reading lacked at 304.4 against 311.5): 331.6 KB as it stands, 329.5 KB with the preview's three static additions stripped — reproducing the earlier stripped reading exactly — so +2.1 KB, the old +1.9 plus ~0.2 this pass added. Set to 336 KiB, 4.4 KB over: twice the whole feature's static cost so ordinary component work does not fail a build, and smaller than any dependency worth the name. Proposes that a margin be stated in bytes and justified against the change it must ADMIT, with the zero-budget measurement and its runtime pasted beside any raise; declines a checker as circular — a gate on the gate's constant can only compare it to a number someone else chose.
- `2026-08-26-a-shared-helper-agrees-about-its-computation-never-about-the-rows-it-is-handed` — a helper is extracted so two callers cannot disagree; the extraction guarantees they compute the same FUNCTION and nothing whatever about the ARGUMENTS they hand it, and where the two runtimes build those arguments differently the shared helper returns two answers while reading as the thing that made disagreement impossible. `activeAgentNames` says twice in its own docblock that it is scope-blind ("at either scope") and then states why it was exported ahead of a second caller — _"the emitted `SelectedAgentName` union and the wizard's agent hydration must derive the same set from the same rows"_ — which is a promise about the function that every reader takes as a promise about the system, so checking that both surfaces call the shared symbol is where the review stops. The CLI hands it the rows that sentence describes: `regenerateConfigTypes` reads back the `config.ts` it has just written, and a project's file comes from the INLINING writer, so the array carries the inherited global rows ahead of the project's own. The editor's preview handed it `projectSplit.agents`, so a union whose sibling `config.ts` still declared five sub-agents named one — measured against a real `setupDualScope` install of the same shape, `"web-developer" | "api-developer"` on disk against `"api-developer"` drawn. **Same class, second mechanism, same module**: `sourceForSkill` reads `skill.availableSources`, populated by the CLI's multi-source loader and by nothing a browser runs (`grep -c availableSources` answers 0 in both the vendored matrix and the mock marketplace catalogue), so every origin collapsed to the default and the plugin note told a visitor on their own marketplace that their skill resolves from ours.
