Three things kept it invisible and each generalises: a KNOWN GAP comment in `preview-matches-install.e2e.test.ts` named ONE reason (an unknowable import specifier) for an exclusion covering the WHOLE project `config-types.ts`, and a named exclusion is read as an exhaustive one; the other half of the bilateral contract, `emission-scenarios.test.ts`, renders even its project-root scenario through the STANDALONE types writer, so `generateProjectConfigTypesSource` is called by neither runner while both docblocks correctly describe an arrangement they call bilateral; and the CLI's own gate for exactly this invariant, `global-agent-propagation-type-consistency.e2e.test.ts`, stayed green because it drives a real CLI and cannot reach a browser — the property was written down, tested, and broken by the one surface the test could not see. Fixed by deriving the union from the inlined view the CLI itself uses (`buildProjectTypesExtras(inlinedProjectView(...))`, mirrored as `projectTypesExtras`) and by reading the SEATED marketplace from `catalog-store`'s `activeMarketplace()`, rendering a ref AS a ref rather than in the slot a name belongs in, because `seedPayloadSchema` says the name is read from a `marketplace.json` this app never fetches. Proposes one clause on CLAUDE.md's export-before-a-second-caller exception (say which rows each runtime is expected to hand the helper, and name the gate that would notice divergence — or say none exists) and one on prose KNOWN GAPs (state the EXTENT first and the cause second). Declines a checker as non-lexical — every call site looks identical and the difference was built three functions earlier — and names the test that WOULD close it, plus the reason it is not landed: neither suite can reach the other's catalogue.
- `2026-08-26-two-derivations-of-one-structurally-empty-field-agree-so-the-pin-between-them-cannot-fail` — the product half was already filed and deliberately deferred by `2026-08-26-a-shared-helper-agrees-about-its-computation-never-about-the-rows-it-is-handed` ("Not fixed, reported instead — a third site of instance 2's class, same module"): `ejectedCatalogueNote` rendered `Source: ${primarySourceName(skill)}/src/skills/<id>` off `availableSources`, which the CLI's multi-source loader fills and nothing a browser runs does (`grep -c` answers 0 in both the vendored matrix and the mock marketplace catalogue), so a visitor seated on `github:acme/skills` read `agents-inc/src/skills/acme-web-widgets` — while its immediate neighbour `pluginReferenceNote` had been fixed one change earlier to read the seat, leaving **two adjacent notes disagreeing about one fact in a pane a user sees whole**, which is worse than a uniform error because it says the surface was not thought through. **The half that is new is why the deferral cost nothing visible: the claim WAS pinned, by a spec named "names the marketplace an ejected skill is copied from, never the eject sentinel" carrying a 22-line docblock arguing its own rigour, and it could not fail.** It took the marketplace off the PLUGIN variant's recorded `origin` and asserted the ejected note contained it — but `origin` is written by `sourceForSkill` off `availableSources` and the note's own source came from `primarySourceName` off `availableSources`, so **both sides of the comparison were derived from the same structurally-empty field**, both collapsed to `DEFAULT_PUBLIC_SOURCE_NAME`, and the assertion compared `"agents-inc"` to `"agents-inc"` forever. It read as rigorous precisely because the two expressions do not look alike — `config.skills.find(...).origin` against `catalog.skills[id].availableSources.find((s) => s.primary).name`, different objects, different layers, which is the shape of a genuine cross-check, and the docblock argued for it on exactly those grounds while ruling out the weaker alternative of "proving only that the note agrees with itself"; it did prove that, by a longer route. Three things made it invisible and each generalises: `tsc` cannot object because `availableSources?:` is legitimately optional and both runtimes are valid programs; the suite cannot, because every one of its 21 specs ran on the RESTING seat where the public catalogue is the honest answer, so the note was correct in every state a test could observe; and `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md`'s existing rule names the wrong mechanism — it forbids binding to a shared CONSTANT and calls the failure a "diverged-constant vacuum", but no constant appears on either side here, so nobody grepping for that rule arrives at this. Fixed by giving the question one owner: a `seatedMarketplace(): SeatedMarketplace` discriminated union (`{kind:"public",name}` / `{kind:"ref",ref}`) that a caller cannot reach `ref` on believing it holds a name, rendered two ways — `marketplacePhrase` for prose, `ejectedSourceLine` for the coordinate, where only the public arm has a bare name that can stand as a path segment and a ref is labelled AFTER the path (`Source: src/skills/<id>, in the marketplace at github:acme/skills`) rather than dropped into the slot a name belongs in, since `github:acme/skills/src/skills/<id>` parses and resolves to nothing. Pin split by SEAT rather than by install mode — the axis the defect actually lives on — into four specs: public seat byte-unchanged, acme seat names the ref and not `agents-inc`, both notes agree, and the eject-sentinel half kept. **Red phase manufactured twice** (a pin written after a fix has never failed), the defect reinstated and the file restored byte-identical by `sha256sum -c` each time. Census of the class run rather than assumed — seven hits, no instance left: the two surviving `origin` readers use it as the plugin/eject DISCRIMINATOR, which `EJECT_SOURCE` makes correct regardless of `availableSources`. Proposes a second clause on the constant rule (the shared term need not be a constant — the tell is an optional field whose only writer lives in another runtime, and the question to ask is what would have to change for the two sides to disagree) plus one line for `standards/e2e/assertions.md` (a suite that only runs on the resting seat can only observe states where the default is right). Declines a checker as non-lexical and names what would close it mechanically instead — a browser-side catalogue type that does not carry the field at all, so a read is a compile error rather than an `undefined` with a plausible default. Also records, unapplied, that `2026-08-26-a-sentinel-is-a-legal-value-of-the-field-and-an-invalid-coordinate` now carries a `resolved_by:` citing a deleted symbol and a retired pin, though the sentinel defect it reports is still fixed.
- `2026-08-26-a-set-of-controls-has-no-accessibility-tree-assertion-because-the-name-matcher-has-no-array-form` — `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` states the suite's rule as "Assert on the accessibility tree" and `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` states the set rule as "assert the members with `toStrictEqual` against a named constant"; **for a SET of controls the two cannot both be followed, because Playwright ships no bulk read of accessible names.** Verified against the installed types rather than recalled — `grep -n "  toHaveText(\|  toHaveAccessibleName(" node_modules/playwright/types/test.d.ts` shows `toHaveText` and `toHaveClass` taking `ReadonlyArray<string|RegExp>` while `toHaveAccessibleName` and `toHaveAccessibleDescription` take `string|RegExp` and nothing else, and `Locator` has no `allAccessibleNames()`: the only bulk reads are `allTextContents`, `allInnerTexts` and `evaluateAll`, all three of which read the DOM. **Where the two readings differ the difference is invisible**: a control drawn as an `aria-hidden` mark plus a label has a `textContent` carrying the mark and an accessible name that does not, so the obvious `toHaveText([...LABELS])` fails against a string nobody wrote — and both ways out are worse than the problem, since pasting the mark into the expected constant asserts a decoration the accessibility tree says is absent, and loosening to a `RegExp` array stops asserting the label at all. **The workaround is already in this tree three times and is explained nowhere as a rule** — `options-panel.ts`'s `sectionLabels` (`[data-slot="field-label"]`), and `dialogs.ts`'s `rowNames` (`preview-row-name`) and skill-contents list (`contents-file`) — each reaching for a `data-slot` on the element holding the NAME rather than on the control, so the fourth author meets the wall from scratch; this pass was the fourth, and the Phase C suggestion openers (`aria-hidden` `→` then a sentence-case label) needed a `suggestion-label` hook requested of the developer before their set could be asserted at all. **It compounds with an already-filed defect rather than being independent of it**: all three bulk reads are non-retrying (`2026-08-26-a-page-objects-multi-element-read-is-non-retrying-so-it-reports-a-loading-screen-as-an-empty-one`), so the one expression of a set assertion this suite has is also the one that answers `[]` for a row that has not mounted — a set assertion needs BOTH a structural hook and a sentinel, and neither requirement is written beside the other. Discovery only; nothing changed in the three existing instances, which are all correct, and the gap is that the technique has no name. Proposes one paragraph in `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` naming the mechanical limit, the four live hooks and the sentinel requirement, cross-checked as weakening no existing rule and licensing no `getByTestId` — the hook sits on a label the control already renders and the control is still located by role and name. **Declines a checker as non-lexical**: a row asserted with `toHaveText` looks identical whether its controls carry a decorative mark or not, and whether they do is a property of the component rather than of the spec; what would close it mechanically is on the product side, and is a different finding on a different tree. Census exact at three, from `grep -rn "allTextContents\|allInnerTexts" apps/editor/e2e` read line by line with the two non-set hits excluded by inspection.
- `2026-08-26-a-caret-assertion-is-satisfied-by-the-browsers-value-setter-not-by-the-code-that-places-it` — a React handler that writes state and then writes the DOM runs the DOM half BEFORE the commit, so `setSelectionRange(prefill.length, …)` is clamped against the OLD value and does nothing — and **the caret still lands in the right place, because React then assigns `node.value` and a textarea's value setter moves it to the end on its own.** The correct behaviour and the no-op are therefore indistinguishable to every assertion anyone would write. Measured both directions rather than argued: replacing the call with `setSelectionRange(0, 0)` — a deliberate caret-to-START — leaves Phase C's caret spec **green** without `flushSync` and **red** with it (received 0/0 against an expected 19/19), so `flushSync` is what makes the component's own placement the thing under test rather than the browser's. **The spec is not weak and was not changed**: it reads `selectionStart`, `selectionEnd` and `value.length` in one `evaluate` precisely so it cannot answer about three different moments, and its own comment says so — it asserts the right property, which is simply also true of an implementation that does not have it. Nothing else catches it either: both orderings are valid programs so `tsc` and eslint have no opinion, and the symptom is not a wrong caret but a caret that is right for the wrong reason, which no screenshot can show. Nearest existing rule is `packages/ui/CLAUDE.md`'s "A class in the DOM is not a class in effect" — the same shape one layer over, stated about CSS and unfindable by anyone reasoning about a DOM method. Fixed with `flushSync` around the state write, the two-direction measurement pasted above it so the next reader need not re-derive why a `flushSync` is there for a one-character effect. Proposes one paragraph for `apps/editor/e2e/README.md`: a post-state-write DOM read or write needs `flushSync`, several such writes agree with the intended result by accident (the value setter's caret, a re-layout's scroll reset), and a spec asserting one must be verified to FAIL against a deliberately wrong VALUE of that property rather than only against the removal of the code — removal is the case the browser covers for you. **Declines a checker as non-lexical**: the two orderings are the same three statements in the same order, and omitting `flushSync` is legitimate wherever the next statement does not read the DOM; what would close it is a mutation run, the manual version of which is the table in the body. Census exact and the class has one member — 21 hits from `grep -rn "setSelectionRange\|selectionStart\|scrollTo("` read line by line, of which 17 are test-side document or element scrolls driven from Playwright with no pending commit to run ahead of.
- `2026-08-26-a-matrix-cells-aria-label-overrides-the-word-that-is-its-whole-state` — `MatrixGrid`'s cell is a `<button>` whose visible content **is** its state, and the component's own docblock says so: _"the word in the cell **is** the state — the design has no legend and no icons."_ It then carries ``aria-label={`${row.label} ${cell.label}`}``, and **`aria-label` replaces content in the accessible-name computation** — so the name is `web dev`, the word `lazy` / `pre` reaches the accessibility tree through no channel at all, and cycling the cell announces nothing new. What is left is colour. **Nothing in the repository can notice, and both gates look like they should**: axe runs in `error` mode and passes, because the button HAS a name and there is no rule for a name that discards what it names; and `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` states the suite's rule as _"Assert on the accessibility tree"_, which the LOCATOR honours (``getByRole("button", { name: `${domain} ${role}` })``) while every state assertion beside it reads `toHaveText("pre")` — `textContent`, the one thing a screen reader never receives here. Locating through the tree and verifying through the DOM is the gap. Census exact and both halves run: one hit in `packages/ui/src/components`, this one; eighteen in `apps/editor/src` and **all eighteen correct**, so it is an outlier rather than a habit — `roster-panel.tsx`'s `Load mode: ${skill.load}` and `Model for ${agentId}: ${model}` are the same control kind done right, one package over. Found while specifying the composer proposal's diffed matrix (`todo/plans/editor-v6/phase-c-spec.md` §11), whose read-only mode is specified to announce the full sentence — so the NEW surface will say more than the shipped one. Not fixed there: the label is a shipped accessible name that two suites query by (two `matrix-grid.stories.tsx` play functions and `options-panel.ts`'s locator, reached by four E2E specs), and folding that into a feature diff would hide it.
- `2026-08-26-a-hand-written-subset-of-a-generated-roster-is-reconciled-by-tsc-in-neither-direction` — the editor's skill options panel drew columns for two of the roster's three per-domain roles, so `web-researcher`, `api-researcher`, `ai-researcher` and `cli-researcher` were hand-assignable nowhere: **14 of 18 sub-agents placed, four unreachable**, with 452 unit tests and 419 E2E specs green throughout. **The shape is nastier than "an agent was missing" — all four were visibly present and silently uneditable**, because the BEHAVIOURAL half was fixed on 2026-08-06 (`default-assignments.ts` went data-driven, `DOMAIN_REACH.web = 5` counts the researcher) and `derive.ts` takes `SUB_AGENT_GROUPS` whole, so a user could watch a researcher carry a skill, switch it off in the roster, and reach no control that could put it back. **The mechanism is the keeper: `tsc` reconciled `ROLE_COLUMNS` in NEITHER direction.** The CLI's equivalent hazard is one-directional and `src/cli/lib/wizard/agent-roster.test.ts` says so in its own docblock — _"`tsc` reconciles them in one direction only … ADDING one produces none, and six agents shipped in the package, documented, and unreachable through the wizard because nothing asked the other question"_ — but `BUILT_IN_AGENT_GROUPS` holds `AgentName` literals, where `ROLE_COLUMNS` holds role SUFFIXES matched at runtime by `roleOf` slicing `<domain>-` off an id into a `Map<string, SubAgent>`, so nothing in the chain is typed to any union of roles. Measured rather than argued: a `{ id: "archaeologist", short: "arc" }` column naming a role no agent has leaves `tsc -b --noEmit` exiting 0 with no output. Nothing else could notice either — the unit suite is `environment: "node"` by deliberate design so nothing rendered the panel, and the E2E locator is an aria-label composed of a domain label and a COLUMN short, which cannot name an agent that has no column. **The rule existed and was one workspace away**: `agent-roster.test.ts` is this exact guard for this exact failure mode, referenced from no CLAUDE.md and no `standards/` doc, findable only by someone already reading it; the nearest written rule governs how to write such an assertion and is silent on when one is owed. Fixed: the column added with `short: "res"` derived from the design's own `MX_ROLES` abbreviation rule (`reviewer → rev` gives `researcher → res`) rather than guessed, width measured only to establish it decides nothing (columns compute to 89.14px, `res` inks 16.98px — identical to `dev`); placement extracted to `agent-placement.ts` because `react-refresh/only-export-components` fails the lint on a non-component export from a `.tsx`, so a testable placement CANNOT live in the panel file; `PLACED_AGENT_IDS` held against `SUB_AGENT_GROUPS` with `toStrictEqual` on sorted arrays plus a non-empty guard, red earned twice. Class census run rather than assumed — nine roster-reading editor modules, every other product consumer taking it whole (`SUB_AGENT_GROUPS.flatMap`, `[...AGENT_NAMES]`, `agentId in SUB_AGENTS_BY_ID`), and `CORE_ROLES`, named as a second instance by EDITOR-10, no longer exists. Records what the new test does NOT catch (a column naming a role no agent has stays green, drawing a column of inert gaps) so it does not read as complete. Proposes one clause beside CLAUDE.md's count rule — ask both what breaks if the union LOSES a member and what breaks if it GAINS one, and write the assertion where the second answer is "nothing" — and a second for `apps/editor/`, which has no CLAUDE.md: a non-appearance claim about a component must be extracted to a `lib/*.ts` to be testable at all, since the lint forbids the export and the node suite cannot render. **Declines a checker as non-lexical**: `ROLE_COLUMNS` is deliberately partial — `pm` and `reviewer` are excluded on purpose — so a rule saying the array must equal the union would be false here.
- `2026-09-01-refused-http-body-discarded-at-network-boundaries` — `publish-seed.ts` rendered every refused `POST /configs` as `Sharing this configuration failed (HTTP ${response.status}).` and never read the body, so the worker's own issue — naming the skill and the sub-agent that cannot hold it — was discarded and a real user was left with a bare `HTTP 400` and a debugging session for a fault the server had already described. **The field named `message` is not the message**: `@hono/zod-validator` answers `c.json(result, 400)` and Zod renders its ISSUES into `error.message` as a JSON document, so an implementation that quotes that field verbatim prints `[\n  {\n    "code": "custom",` at a terminal and the sentences a person can act on are one level further in. Both halves of the class are fixed. The inbound sibling in `fetch-seed.ts` needed a different extraction because `getConfig` refuses with `c.text(...)` rather than an envelope, so `refusalMessage` there gates on the wire's own content type rather than on an envelope shape — see `2026-09-01-a-refusals-body-is-quoted-only-where-the-cli-is-its-audience`.
- `2026-09-01-a-zod-rendered-refusal-carries-its-path-so-an-assertion-on-those-names-cannot-see-the-message` — `formatZodIssue` prefixes a path-bearing issue with `issue.path.join(".")`, and a refinement CHOOSES that path: `installableSeedPayloadSchema` in `packages/matrix/src/seed.ts` raises at `["skills", skillId, "assignments", agent]`, so both halves of the pair are spelled out before the sentence starts. `share-stdin.e2e.test.ts` asserted `toContain("web-developer")` under a comment saying the message must name the pair — the one claim the assertion existed to carry and the one it could not reach. Measured end to end rather than argued: re-rendering each issue as its path plus `Invalid input`, the whole user-facing explanation deleted, left the spec green. **The keeper is why an author following the existing sentinel rule still lands here — the prefix is invisible where the message is written**, since the refinement shows its author a sentence and nothing about the path a renderer in another workspace will put in front of it. Fixed by asserting the path AND a fragment of the sentence, through `flattenCliOutput` because oclif hard-wraps at the terminal width. **Its census under-counts, and the method is why**: keying on `formatZodErrors`/`formatZodIssue` finds only tests that IMPORT the renderer, so it reports two files and misses `plugin-validator.test.ts`, whose `toContain("name")` and `toContain("version")` are two more path-satisfied assertions — both correct, because a path is the honest carrier of WHICH FIELD and those specs claim nothing further. The proposed standard is now in `standards/e2e/assertions.md` § "A Sentinel Must Name the Substantive Claim", rewritten on two counts: the body's "every issue" is false in two arms of the renderer — `unrecognized_keys` returns the keys and no path, and an empty path returns the bare message, the second pinned by `schemas.test.ts` — and the file holds seven specs rather than the six the mutation run reports.
- `2026-09-01-an-enumerated-tools-list-is-an-allowlist-and-the-skills-key-does-not-grant-the-skill-tool` — every sub-agent this product has ever compiled was told, in the strongest terms `agent.liquid` can manage, to invoke a tool it had never been granted. A `tools:` frontmatter list is an **allowlist**: an agent that omits the key inherits everything including `Skill`, so enumerating tools is precisely what removes it, and `grep -L '^  - Skill$' packages/cli/src/agents/*/*/metadata.yaml` returns all 18. **The half nobody could have guessed is that `skills:` does not close it** — that key preloads skill CONTENT and grants no tool, so a compiled agent can declare five preloaded skills, carry the whole activation protocol, and read as fully skill-aware from its own frontmatter while being unable to load any of them. Confirmed from the inside rather than inferred: four sub-agents in one session reported the Skill tool disabled after evaluating skills as YES, and the repository's own step-3 requirement (apply `meta-design-expressive-typescript` after implementing) had gone unperformed across several tasks because of it. Fixed in `withSkillTool` in `packages/compile/src/agent-source.ts` rather than in the 18 metadata files, because `buildAgentTemplateContext` is the single assembly point both front doors render through and a metadata fix would leave the next user-authored agent in the same trap; granted unconditionally, appended, and identity-returning when already present, verified as a byte fixed point by a second real `compile` reporting `0 rewritten, 13 unchanged`. Also worth keeping: a tools list is lossy **without complaint** — `cli-developer.md` v0.160.0 declares `Grep, Glob` and the agent launched from it had neither.
- `2026-09-01-a-refusals-body-is-quoted-only-where-the-cli-is-its-audience` — the completion of the refused-body class, and both halves of it would have been wrong done naively. `POST /configs` answers a stale seed version with `409` and `Reload the page: …`, which is right for the browser tab that refusal was designed around and is the one instruction a terminal cannot carry out — so quoting it is **worse than the bare status it replaced**, on the single refusal whose entire purpose is naming a remedy. The actionable form of the same fact was sitting there unstated: `SEED_VERSION` is a `z.literal` bundled into the binary, and `read-piped-payload.ts` validates a piped payload against it before the POST, so a 409 cannot be the payload's fault by either door and only a newer CLI can change it. The read half needed the opposite correction: `getConfig` refuses with `c.text(...)` and carries no envelope, so there is no shape a foreign body fails — a naive quote (written and run, as stage one) printed `<html><head><title>Request blocked</title></head></html>` at the terminal from a captive-portal fixture. Standard proposed: **a refusal's body is repeated only where the CLI is its intended audience and the wire says the store sent it**, with the budget a measurement of one route rather than a policy about terminals (300 for a list of schema issues, 120 for a one-line refusal). The residual it named — the write half discarding the plain-text 413/429/503 bodies, asymmetric with the read half — is closed by CLI-855: `publish-seed.ts` gained a prose arm beside its envelope arm, so 503's `Could not store this config` now reaches a terminal, and `restatesItsOwnStatus` suppresses 413 and 429 by comparing the trimmed body against `node:http`'s `STATUS_CODES` rather than by listing them — a rule about the class, so the next refusal written the same way is suppressed on the day it ships.
- `2026-09-01-a-write-routes-test-double-minted-what-the-worker-refuses` — both `packages/api-mocks`'s `createConfig` and `packages/cli`'s `e2e/fixtures/seed-config-store.ts` `mintedHandler` answered `POST /configs` with `201 { id }` for any body at all, while the worker gates that route with `installableSeedPayloadSchema` and refuses with 400, 409, 413 or 503. **A double more permissive than the service it stands in for cannot fail, and one that cannot fail is not a test of whatever posts to it**: the CLI's own local pre-POST check used the weaker BASE schema for a week, and every suite touching a config POST stayed green throughout, because nothing here could refuse what the worker refuses. Two comments in `handlers.ts` had stated the resulting gap as settled fact — one called 409 and 503 "the two statuses `POST /configs` refuses with" where the worker declares four, and `storeRefusedHandler`'s called KV refusal "the one failure ... no request can provoke" — both falsified by the production incident this same week. **The general shape**: a double's READ side can be faithful by construction (a fixture parsed against the real schema once, at import), but its WRITE side has no fixture to parse — faithfulness there means running the SAME schema over the CALLER's body and answering exactly as the framework's own validation failure does, envelope included. Fixed in both files by importing `installableSeedPayloadSchema` and restating no rule of it by hand; every opt-in refusal (`configRefusedHandlerFor`, `storeRefusedHandler`) still shadows the validating default, covered by new specs. Both suites watched RED first against the demonstration payload that reached a real user — a project-scoped skill on a sub-agent resting at the `global` default. Residual: `configRefusedHandlerFor` still answers 409 with no body while the worker's real 409 carries a sentence, so no spec exercises that envelope against a handler shaped like the worker's.
- `2026-09-01-a-new-specs-directory-can-break-an-unrelated-symbolic-journey-reference` — adding the first real spec to `e2e/fixtures/` broke four `spec-gates.test.ts` tests over a name the new file never mentions: `fixtures/dual-scope-helpers.initGlobalWithEject`, a code SYMBOL already declared non-spec in `RECOGNISED_NON_SPEC_NAMES`. `classify()` in `journey-page.ts` never consults that allowlist — it decides purely from whether ANY real spec shares the name's first path segment, so the moment `"fixtures"` held one, the same symbolic reference started throwing. **The failure names the symbol, not the new file**, so nothing about it points at what actually changed; traced by checking `git status` showed `dual-scope-helpers.ts` and journey 1's row untouched, then reading `classify()`. Worked around rather than fixed (outside this task's file ownership): the new spec was placed in `commands/`, already spec-bearing, following the `commands/warn-suppression-stops-at-the-harness.e2e.test.ts` precedent for a harness-subject spec sitting outside its thematic directory. It proposed that `classify()` check the allowlist before the directory heuristic. **Resolved by CLI-856 (2026-09-02)**: a directory's kind is now stated in `SPEC_DIRECTORIES` rather than inferred from what happens to sit in it, and the derivation survives only as a roster gate holding the two against each other. The proposal this row records — threading the allowlist into `classify()` — was considered and refused, because it would hand the reader the roster it is then measured against.
- `2026-09-02-the-unit-suite-downloaded-a-github-marketplace-once-per-test-and-the-only-symptom-was-a-timeout` — CLI-853 was filed as a 10s budget question about "a cold spawned process"; both premises were false. `runCliCommand` spawns nothing (it calls `@oclif/core`'s `run()` in-process), and the budget was being eaten by a live download of `github:agents-inc/skills` from `api.github.com` — **once per test**, because `setupIsolatedHome` gives every test a fresh HOME and `cacheRoot()` is `os.homedir()/.cache/…`, so the cache was always empty and `classifyCachedCopy` always answered `unrecorded`. Instrumenting `globalThis.fetch` through `NODE_OPTIONS=--import` counted 64 requests to `api.github.com` for `doctor.test.ts`'s 23 tests (21 tarball `GET`s), 31 tarball downloads across the project. **The network was the small half** — 212ms mean latency, 5.1s of a 56s file — and the rest was gunzip plus ~500 file writes, which is why the failure tracked machine load rather than code. One download equalled one slow test, exactly: 21 slow tests and 21 `GET`s in `doctor.test.ts`, 8 and 8 in `eject.test.ts`, while eleven of the fifteen `runCliCommand` files had no test over 1s beyond their own first (a real, bounded cold module import). So it was four outliers, not a family budget. **It had already been met and answered with a longer fuse** — `COMMAND_TIMEOUT = 30_000` over eight specs in `search.test.ts` and one `{ timeout: 30_000 }` in `doctor.test.ts`, both this defect patched in whichever specs lost that day's race; both deleted with the fix, which is what makes the diagnosis falsifiable. The e2e suite had solved this and written down why (`e2e/fixtures/default-source-cache.ts`, "offline by construction, twice over") but no rule generalised it, so the unit side rediscovered it from the symptom end. Fixed with `shared-source.ts`'s own shape one directory over: one checkout fetched in `globalSetup`, published by `rename`, recorded with no `etag` so nothing revalidates, symlinked into each fake home at `.cache`. Sum of test durations 130,447ms → 44,519ms, GitHub requests 31 → 0; under 20-way manufactured contention 2/2 red before and 3/3 green after. Residual: nothing mechanical would catch the next spec that reaches the network, and a naive `fetch` guard is not the answer because `useMockWorker` intercepts `fetch` through `msw/node`.
- `2026-09-02-a-fixture-at-a-machine-wide-path-had-no-rule-about-who-may-write-it` — three tracker rows (CLI-859, CLI-860, CLI-862) that arrived separately and are one subject: two test fixtures at fixed `os.tmpdir()` paths, and nothing written down about who owns them. The path is fixed because `globalSetup` runs in vitest's own process while the specs run in forked workers, so neither side can be handed the location — and **the address then belongs to the machine rather than to the run**, which nothing said. `shared-source.test.ts` is collected by the `unit` project and called `buildSharedSource` on the real E2E path in six specs plus `removeSharedSource()` in an `afterEach`; reproduced by planting a file under `/tmp/agents-inc-e2e-shared-fixtures/` and running that one file — seven passes, planted file gone, and both suites green either way because the E2E side is not running yet. Beside it, `ensureSharedMarketplaceCheckout` published by `rename` and recorded afterwards into a SIBLING path the rename does not carry, so a run killed between the two lines left a directory the `directoryExists` guard accepts and a record `classifyCachedCopy` answers `unrecorded` to — **permanently**, since every later run returns early over the directory. Measured through the real binary against a fake home linked to the shared cache: 0.76s recorded, 3.80s interrupted, all 3739 files rewritten; the docblock asserted the invariant the code did not hold. And the two `linkSharedCache` calls that make the checkout reach anything had no pin at all, so deleting both left the suite green with a timeout as the only symptom. **The proposed one-line fix — move the `writeFile` above the `rename` — is correct and was still not taken**, because nothing could pin it (the only difference is the order of two statements, observable only by mocking `fs/promises`, and the function short-circuits once globalSetup has published, so the mock would have to run at the real shared path) and because it closes one window where writing the record UNCONDITIONALLY repairs a checkout that lost its record for any reason, at the cost of one small write and never a fetch. Fixed by giving each mutating helper a root-taking mechanism (`buildFrozenSourceTree`, `ensureRecordedCheckout` — whose fetch is a parameter too), stating the rule in `shared-source.ts` and in the two places that already discuss the suites running concurrently (`package.json`'s `//test` note and `.husky/pre-push`, both of which explained only the `dist/` tsup race), and enforcing it in `shared-fixture-writers.test.ts`, which compares each writer's INVOKING files against a stated roster and fails on a new caller and on a rename alike. Carry-forward: a shared fixture's docblock always argues for the sharing, because that is the decision its author was making — it has no reason to name who may write it until a SECOND suite exists, by which point the first module is the precedent the second was copied from, silence included.
- `2026-09-02-a-justified-absence-reads-as-an-instruction-and-no-vocabulary-census-finds-it` — the push hook got its lint, unit and e2e suites back as separate sequential `turbo run` invocations, and two reference documents still described the hook without them. The self-contradiction was harmless (`monorepo-layout.md` denied the suites in one paragraph and asserted them forty lines below); the danger was that both documents carried a **reason** for the absence — "running them is the pusher's job now … **separately**, because running the two in one turbo invocation reproduces the same race", and "**The suites left this hook on 2026-08-23**" — and a stated reason reads as an instruction to restore the state it justifies. Acting on either reason takes the suites back out of the hook, which is the regression REPO-42 names; nothing mechanically reads the hook. (The collapse-into-one-invocation framing this row first carried was a different regression and was corrected 2026-09-02.) **The bible's instrument does not reach this shape**: its absence-vocabulary census (`declares no|no equivalent|is absent|is a gap|does not exist|untested|no spec|vestigial`) returns two hits over the pre-repair text of both files and neither is one of the four defect sites — a passive absence says "there is no X", a justified absence says "X is not here, and here is why", and the two share almost no vocabulary. And the sharper half: `.ai-docs/` is Prettier-formatted at `proseWrap: preserve`, so `grep -nP 'left this hook'` exits 1 on a file that contains the phrase, because it straddles a hand-wrapped line — the same hazard `TEMPLATE.md` records against its own enum value, generalised to **no line-oriented phrase census over `.ai-docs/` returning a trustworthy zero**. Proposes a second census keyed on decision vocabulary (`is a decision rather than|rather than an omission|deliberately (absent|omitted)|left this (hook|file|command|suite)|is the whole (hook|file|thing)`), which finds five hits over the same text — two defect sites and three correctly-written justified absences, the ratio being the point — and a stated fold-first remedy (`tr '\n' ' ' | grep -oP`) on every phrase census the bible carries.
- `2026-09-02-a-rate-limiter-above-a-free-guard-charges-for-work-it-refused-to-do` — `POST /compose` called `COMPOSE_CALLS.limit()` as its first statement, above the two guards that refuse an empty or over-long sentence **before the model is reached**, so eleven over-long pastes exhausted a 10-a-minute allowance that exists to bound Anthropic spend without one call to Anthropic. The damage is that two failures compound into a third true of neither: the first refusal was already mis-narrated as "The model did not answer", and enough of them turned that into "too many requests", which is caused by the first and describes nothing that happened. The census is the whole population — two `.limit(` sites in the worker — and the other one is **right for the opposite reason**: `CONFIG_WRITES` is deliberately the first thing `POST /configs` does, because it meters request VOLUME from anonymous callers and wants a cheap refusal to stay cheap, where `COMPOSE_CALLS` meters SPEND from an identified caller and must sit above the call that spends and below everything free. The two were written in the same worker three weeks apart and the difference is not visible from the binding name; the tell that a limiter is too high is a refusal message caused by an earlier refusal. Carries a second half — `/compose` spends one 400 on two guards and names which in the BODY, so a client mapping by `response.status` alone narrated a length refusal as a model failure that provably never happened and paged the alert channel for a request that cost nothing. Nothing static reaches either half: a limiter one line too high type-checks, lints and passes, and a status-keyed refusal map is a total function with no missing case to report.
- `2026-09-02-a-replacement-that-fails-leaves-the-user-with-neither` — the editor's stack grid draws an account's stacks IN PLACE OF the one local snapshot, made safe by a first sign-in minting that snapshot to the account. CLI-851 gave the mint a refusal (an unwritable scope pair), the guard receiving it was `if (!minted.ok) return stacks`, and `stacks` is empty because adoption only runs against an empty list — so signing in drew neither list and the work vanished with nothing said. The mechanism generalises: the code that HIDES B and the code that PRODUCES A are different modules, only one can see a refusal, and returning the prior value type-checks perfectly. Proposes the rule that the condition hiding B must be "A arrived" rather than "A was attempted", plus the three questions to ask of any such pair. Census of consumer refusal guards in `apps/editor/src` found no siblings; names one adjacent-but-different case and one pre-existing 401/500 conflation in `listStacks` left for its owner.
- `2026-09-02-a-page-object-modelling-only-a-controls-resting-state-pushes-every-lifecycle-locator-into-the-specs` — `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` says all locators live in `pages/` and never in a spec, and eleven assertions build one anyway, all the same shape (`grep -rn 'roster\.root\.getByRole' apps/editor/e2e/specs`). The cause is an omission rather than carelessness: `RosterPanel` locates Save and Share by their RESTING word, both buttons narrate their outcome through their accessible name — a refused save produces no cell and a refused share no link, so the words are the only feedback either has — and the page object's locator stops matching the instant either reports anything, leaving a spec whose subject is an ENDING nowhere to go but `roster.root`. **The generalisation: a page object that models only a control's resting state pushes every assertion about its other states into the specs**, which is not a rule anybody broke but one nobody could obey from where they were standing. Closed for Save (`saveNarrating(label)`, name-parameterised so the locator lives in `pages/` and the app's words stay in the spec); the eleven existing sites sit in three files another lane owns and were left. Nothing reports any of it — the rule is one row of a README table with no lint rule, test or script behind it — and the finding argues a checker should be scoped to `.root.getByRole(` rather than to `getByRole` at large, which appears 55 times across fifteen spec files and is legitimate in most of them.
- `2026-09-02-a-refusal-body-that-restates-its-own-status-reason-phrase-explains-nothing` — the refused-body rule's first question ("was this written for a terminal?") was answered by "read the server's handler", which tells an author what a body SAYS and nothing about whether saying it adds anything; the other two questions have mechanical tests and this one had a reading. **A body equal to its own status's reason phrase up to case is the status spelled out**: over the whole population of `POST /configs`'s `c.text` refusals, `Payload too large` and `Too many requests` are `Payload Too Large` and `Too Many Requests` re-cased, while `Could not store this config` against `Service Unavailable` is the only one naming a cause. The imprecision propagated both ways from one sentence — the predecessor's Residual filed all three as a single gap and called the write half "asymmetric with the read side", true of the mechanism and false of the content, since the read route writes prose where two of the write route's three are reason phrases; then the scrutiny pass that narrowed CLI-855 applied a content test for the first time and **inverted it**, disqualifying 413 correctly, keeping 429 on the ground that it says "waiting is the remedy" (it says `Too many requests`), and dismissing 503 because `STORE_REFUSED_BODY` mirrors it in `packages/api-mocks` — a fact about a fixture, bearing on nothing a user sees, and the CLI drops that string today. **The one body of the three that names a cause is the one that was ruled out.** Reproduced through the real binary against a stub answering as the worker does; building the row as written would have printed `... (HTTP 429). The store said: Too many requests.` — longer than what it replaces and carrying nothing the first sentence did not. Also unimplementable as scoped: `arrivedAsText` gates on content type and all three bodies are `c.text`, so quoting 429 alone needs a status branch deciding whether to READ a body, which contradicts the posture `publish-seed.ts` documents for itself. Discovery only — `publish-seed.ts` and its spec are unchanged, on the rule that a row not describing the tree is reported rather than justified. Proposes a fourth row on the editor-and-worker table (compare against `node:http`'s `STATUS_CODES` before quoting), plus per-status-not-per-route framing and "a mock fixture mirroring a body is not evidence the body reaches a user".
- `2026-09-02-a-parsers-error-message-carries-the-input-that-broke-it` — the CLI printed remote HTTP bodies, piped stdin and third-party catalogue metadata with no control-character stripping and no sanitiser anywhere in the package (`grep -rn 'x1b\|stripAnsi\|\\u001b' packages/cli/src/cli` returns one file, `utils/terminal.ts`, which WRITES escapes), so a 503 body carrying `ESC[2K` and a carriage return repainted the line above it and forged a sentence in the CLI's own voice. **The filed row named five print sites and both real chokepoints were in none of them**, because a census keyed on WHERE TEXT IS PRINTED finds print sites while the class is defined by WHICH RENDERER IT FUNNELS THROUGH: `truncateText`, all five of whose call sites are foreign text — it was already the "bound foreign text" function and nothing said so — and `formatZodIssue`, ~20 call sites, whose every sentence part comes from the REFUSED DOCUMENT rather than the schema (a path segment and an unrecognised key are keys the document chose), reached by a stranger's `SKILL.md` frontmatter, `metadata.yaml`, marketplace manifest and stdin alike. **The sharp half survived a first implementation and was caught only by asserting on the whole rendered message**: the stdin excerpt was sanitised and the REASON was not, because the reason is an `Error` from V8 and therefore looks like the CLI's own text — but `JSON.parse` writes the offending input into the message verbatim (`Unexpected token 'h', "here is<ESC>[2"...`), so **provenance follows the bytes rather than the object, and "produced by trusted code" is not an argument that a string is trusted**. That class is eight sites (`grep -rn -A4 -E '(JSON\.parse|parseYaml)\(' packages/cli/src/cli --include='*.ts' | grep -E 'getErrorMessage|read\.reason|\.message'`) and all eight close by sanitising inside `getErrorMessage`, whose own docblock already promised a "human-readable message" — 74 call sites, full unit suite unchanged, which is the evidence it is a no-op on honest text. Nothing mechanical reaches any of it: a raw interpolation type-checks, lints and passes, and `no-control-regex` fires only on code that MATCHES control characters, never on code that PRINTS them — which is why the disable on the sanitiser was deliberately left inline rather than moved to `eslint.config.js`, so the next such regex is still reported. Proposes four rules: sanitise at the renderer rather than the print site; **strip before truncating** (reproduced — `truncateText` returned `'abcd<ESC>…'`, a fragment a terminal holds open to eat the ellipsis and whatever follows); keep newline and tab and drop the rest of C0/C1/DEL and every escape; and pin a permitted case beside every refused one, since a sanitiser tested only on hostile input cannot be told from one that strips everything. Residual: the wizard's own Ink rendering of catalogue `displayName`/`description` is still raw across six components and `edit.tsx`, and closing it needs a ruling this lane did not have standing to take — sanitise the matrix once at `initializeMatrix` (which also feeds `seatCatalog`, so compiled agent bodies change for a hostile catalogue) or patch 499 `displayName` occurrences across 19 files.
- `2026-09-02-a-wire-discriminator-restated-in-the-spec-that-guards-it-moves-with-every-rename` — `/compose` spends one 400 on two guards and names which in the BODY, so the editor branches on `parsed.data.error === TOO_LONG` rather than on the status. That string lived in four places (`grep -rn '"too long"' apps/server/src apps/editor/src packages/api-mocks/src`) and the fourth was a restatement inside `apps/server/src/compose.test.ts` — **the spec whose job was to hold the worker to it**. A rename is not an edit to one line but to a line and the assertion beside it, because that is the shape in which a red test is made green: renaming the worker's literal together with its own assertion was measured to leave all three suites green (10 + 9 + 27) while the shipped editor reverts to "The model did not answer. Nothing changed." for an over-long sentence — false, since both guards run before the model is called — and pages `reportIssue` for a request that cost nothing, which is exactly the defect EDITOR-69 was filed to fix. **The part nobody could read off the file is the boundary against the correct opposite rule sitting three lines above it**: `PAST_THE_CAP`'s comment says, rightly, that a constant the guard compares against is written out and never imported, and to any reader that argues against the fix. The missing half is the discriminator between them — not "is it a constant" but "does a second process hold a copy it must agree with" — so a cap is written out and a wire discriminator gets one definition both specs import, and stating either rule alone is worse than stating neither. Three mutants, each watched red: renaming worker + assertion (caught by `toStrictEqual(COMPOSE_TOO_LONG_BODY)`); renaming the fixture too, which the first now forces (caught in the EDITOR's spec, since its production `TOO_LONG` is the one copy no spec may import, `@workspace/api-mocks` being a test dependency); and a double rewritten to carry a literal instead of the fixture, which reopened the whole gap with everything green and is why the editor's spec now guards `composeTooLongHandler` against the fixture before standing on it, the same self-check `handlers.test.ts` makes on `OUT_OF_SCOPE_PAYLOAD`. Nothing mechanical reaches any of it: the schema declares `error: z.string()`, so the FIELD is typed and the VALUE is invisible to every type in the system by construction. Census over the three workspaces is the whole population and it was one — the editor reads exactly one response body as a discriminator, everything else keys on status — and the single literal body assertion left (`{ error: "empty" }`) is deliberately not the same shape, no client reading it and `api-mocks` modelling that 400 as bodiless on purpose. Names one adjacent case left alone: `/compose`'s two 502 bodies are the same one-status-two-bodies shape with nothing branching on either, so it is where the class recurs rather than a defect today.
- `2026-09-02-a-double-that-differs-in-the-dimension-under-test-cannot-test-it` — four assertions across three suites could not fail and one wire gate was tested only against a wire that does not exist, and they are one shape: **a double or a harness that differs from production in exactly the dimension the code discriminates on cannot test that dimension**. `runCliCommand` returns `stdout` through `ansis.strip`, so `expect(stdout).not.toContain(ESCAPE)` in `search.test.ts` was vacuous by construction while the `CARRIAGE_RETURN` negation beside it still bit — a pair written as one invariant, half of it a test, with nothing in the two adjacent lines saying which half; measured by leaking an escape and no carriage return out of `toResultRow`, which all ten specs passed. The raw buffer for that run also settles what `@oclif/table` does, and it is not what the CLI-854 hand-run's "SGR reset" reading suggests: the table **passes catalogue escapes straight through and sizes the column over them** (706 raw bytes against 697 stripped, border misaligned) and emits its own `<ESC>[22m` beside them, while a reset APPENDED to a cell value is normalised away and never reaches stdout — so an SGR-reset mutant is not a probe for this and an unsanitised-content mutant is. On the wire half, `arrivedAsText` in both `publish-seed.ts` and `fetch-seed.ts` gates quoting with `.startsWith("text/plain")` while hono's `c.text` sends `text/plain; charset=UTF-8` and MSW's `HttpResponse.text` defaults to a bare `text/plain`; every fixture in both suites took that default (`grep -rn 'charset'` over both spec files and `packages/api-mocks/src` returned nothing), so regressing the prefix to `===` left all 32 specs green while silencing, against the deployed worker, `Could not store this config` — the one body on `POST /configs` that names a cause and the whole subject of CLI-855. Two more of the same family beside them: `restatesItsOwnStatus`'s docblock claims a comparison "against the trimmed body" and every fixture was already flush, so moving it off the trimmed value left all 17 specs green; and two of `formatZodIssue`'s three sanitisation points — the key list and the message — were unpinned in a renderer with ~16 call sites, their removal leaving the **entire `unit` project (6778 tests, 190 files)** passing. The message turns out to be sanitised at TWO call sites rather than one, in the arms of a ternary the path selects, so a spec carrying a path can never reach the other. Seven mutants, each compiled into `dist/` and watched red. Proposes four rules: a negative assertion about a character class is read off a value the harness has not normalised (with the census, which returns exactly one instance in this package — the other three sites assert on pure-function output and are sound); a double matches the production emitter in any field the code discriminates on, a library default being nobody's choice and therefore nobody's claim; where a docblock names the FORM of a comparison — trimmed, case-insensitive, prefix — a fixture exists differing from the naive form only in that; and sanitisation CALL SITES are counted rather than conceptual points. Residual, both another lane's: every `HttpResponse.text` in `packages/api-mocks/src/handlers.ts` announces the bare type (exact diff given, unmade because `apps/editor` resolves the same handlers), and `cli-runner`'s `stderr` and `error.message` are stripped with no raw reading beside them, so the same vacuum is one spec away on either.
- `2026-09-02-a-refusal-promoted-to-held-state-acquires-a-lifetime-that-its-own-fix-does-not-pin` — EDITOR-67 stopped discarding an adoption refusal and started HOLDING it (`unadopted`), and its permitted/refused spec pair — a model application of the repository's own twin-pinning rule, both halves in one file, each commented as meaningless without the other — asserts at exactly one moment: the first paint after sign-in. Executed rather than argued: sign in with a scope-conflicted snapshot, then save an unrelated catalogue stack, and `save`'s `unadopted: null` drops the local slot and its `role="alert"` notice off the grid, permanently for the session — `adoptLocalStack` short-circuits on `stacks.length > 0`, so the reason is never recomputed and a reload does not restore it. The snapshot is still in localStorage and still in neither list, which is word for word the shape the predecessor (`2026-09-02-a-replacement-that-fails-leaves-the-user-with-neither`, `status: resolved`) named as the defect. **The keeper is that the rule was obeyed and could not reach this**: the twin rule constrains WHAT a refusal is paired with and says nothing about WHEN either half is observed, so a pair complete in the permitted/refused dimension is blind to every moment after the first. Two things made the gap read as covered — the store asserts the invariant in prose ("an invariant rather than a coincidence"), which is the most effective way to stop the next reader checking it; and the predecessor's census looked for other consumer refusal GUARDS and correctly found none, while promoting a refusal to a field creates a second population, its WRITERS, which nothing directed it to. `unadopted` has one reader and four writers, and the offending one is in another feature. Proposes a second axis for the permitted-twin rule (drive one unrelated successful operation between the refusal and the assertion) and the writer census that finds it. Fixed by EDITOR-73: `save` stopped writing `unadopted` at all — a writer that cannot know what it is asserting should not assert it — and `adoptLocalStack`'s account-has-stacks branch recomputes the reason from the write contract locally, with no request, because a payload that contract refuses could not have reached the account by any route. The second axis this finding proposed is pinned in `accounts.spec.ts` with its permitted twin, and in an `account-store.test.ts` that pins the store's decision table directly. The row's earlier `test.fail()` pin is gone with the defect.
- `2026-09-03-a-double-narrower-than-production-collapses-a-dimension-a-consumer-reads` — EDITOR-73's permitted twin drives two signed-in saves, which the product names identically (`roster-panel.tsx` passes `SAVED_STACK_NAME` on every signed-in Save), and the api-mocks double derives a stack's id from its name (`s_${slug(name)}`) where the worker mints `crypto.randomUUID()` — so two rows production guarantees are distinct arrive equal, and `stack-grid.tsx` keys its account cells on `saved.id`. React warns and calls the result unsupported; measured with a deliberate `toHaveCount(-1)`, it renders **2** cells today, so nothing is asserted wrongly and the assertion stands on undefined reconciliation. **The mirror of the rule already recorded for `captureCreateConfig` (CLI-861), and it fails differently rather than less**: a double LOOSER than its route cannot fail, while a double NARROWER than production _manufactures behaviour production cannot have_ — no false green, but console noise, unsupported behaviour, and a ceiling on what any spec may drive. The keeper is why narrowing looked like a service: a derived id is more readable in a failure message, greppable and stable across runs, and the fixture's docblock names all three correctly — **determinism was worth having and collapsing distinctness to get it was not, and the two are separable, since a counter is as deterministic as a slug**. The dimension is IDENTITY specifically: a double may narrow a timestamp or an ordering because consumers read those the same way whatever they hold, but an identifier's whole contract is that two differ, so equalising two has removed the guarantee rather than simplified the value. Root cause is a premise that expired unwatched — the docblock justified the derivation with "nothing on either side reads its form — both schemas say `z.string()`", every clause true when written, and `key={saved.id}` arrived later in another workspace; **the survey behind it counted VALIDATORS where an identifier's consumers are keys, maps, sets and caches, none of which appear in a schema and none of which fail loudly** (`e2e/fixtures.ts` installs no console guard). Proposes that a double may simplify a value but not collapse a distinctness production guarantees, that a comment claiming nobody reads a field is a claim about consumers rather than schemas, and the two-grep census that pairs minted ids against React keys. Not fixed — another lane's file — with the exact diff given, plus the two things to check before applying it (the module constants' ids stop being stable ACROSS runs, and `@workspace/api-mocks` is resolved by both the editor's Vitest and Playwright suites, so both must run).
- `2026-09-03-the-schema-validating-a-compiled-agent-refuses-two-keys-its-own-template-emits` — `agent.liquid` writes eleven keys into a compiled sub-agent's frontmatter and `agentFrontmatterValidationSchema` is `.strict()` over nine of them; the two it omits are `isolation` and `experimental`, which is the whole class rather than a sample — the difference between the template's emitted keys and `Object.keys(schema.shape)` is exactly those two. Both readers of compiled frontmatter parse through that schema, so an agent carrying either is reported invalid by `validate` and makes `compileAgentPlugin` throw, under a message naming `name` and `description` as the required fields on a file that has both. **One missed site in a change still in the working tree, rather than an old latent gap**: `git diff HEAD` shows the same uncommitted change adding both sub-schemas, adding the template's two branches (neither key was emitted at HEAD) and wiring them into three of four sites — `grep -n "permissionMode:" schemas.ts` returns exactly the three agent-shaped schemas, `agentYamlConfigSchema` and `agentYamlGenerationSchema` were reached, `agentFrontmatterValidationSchema` was not, and `agent.schema.json` was regenerated with both. The one missed is the only one that reads a COMPILED agent back. Two disagreements left beside it: `AgentFrontmatter` declares `isolation` on the very shape being refused and no `experimental` at all while `BaseAgentFields` gained one, and the refusal is reachable from outside the repository, since the CLI must consume what the editor produces and both keys are in the vendored frontmatter vocabulary. Latent today — no shipped agent declares either — which is why nothing reported it. Pinned by `agent-frontmatter-schema-accepts-the-keys-the-template-emits.test.ts`, watched fail first: four red cases and two controls that must stay green, one of them holding the `.strict()` on the `experimental` sub-object that `agentExperimentalSchema` does not carry and its other use site applies at the call — a fix that omits it strips a mistyped option name in silence and satisfies every acceptance case. **`partial`, and it went partial mid-pass**: the lane owning `schemas.ts` landed the code half hours after the filing and independently of it, arriving at the same `.strict()` on the sub-object — the rosters are now equal at eleven each and all six cases pass. The finding stays open because its subject is the absent gate rather than the two keys, with one residue beside it: `AgentFrontmatter` still declares no `experimental`, so the type now understates both the schema declared next to it and the template that writes the file it describes. Proposes that a template-written, schema-read artefact has its key rosters held against each other by a gate, as `agent-template-reads-its-model.test.ts` already does for the template's model READS and nobody did for the keys it EMITS — a gate rather than a checklist, because the author did remember three times out of four and nothing distinguishes the fourth site, twelve lines below one they updated in the same file.
- `2026-09-03-a-spec-pasted-prose-from-a-source-file-in-its-own-repository` — `generate-matrix-package.test.ts` spot-checked two generated `AGENT_DEFINITIONS` entries with pasted literals, each carrying a full copy of the agent's `description` paragraph whose only home is that agent's `metadata.yaml`. `agent-summoner`'s wording was reworded this session, the generator and `packages/matrix` both moved, and the spec was left asserting a sentence no file in the repository contained; `PM_ENTRY` was the identical sibling, green only because `pm`'s wording had not moved yet, and one grep says those two are the whole class. **The cost was the misreading rather than the red test**: a diff of two long prose sentences differing by one clause reads as generated-file drift, and this agent first reported it as another lane mid-edit rather than as an expectation it owned — a value-shaped failure hides which side is stale. Fixed: both entries now come from one `agentDefinitionEntry(flavor, agent)` that reads the source `metadata.yaml`, with the seven fields still spelled out one per line because the FIELD ROSTER is what the tests are named for. **Deriving an expectation from the file the code under test also reads risks a tautology, so non-vacuity was demonstrated rather than assumed** — pointed at a mutated copy of `src/`, the expectation built from the real source stopped matching and the one built from the mutated source matched. What that keeps over a keys-only assertion is PROVENANCE, which the neighbouring byte-identity test cannot supply because regenerating moves both of its sides together. `partial`: the RULE half is open. CLAUDE.md's binding rule offers two rows — bind a SYMBOL, keep the literal for TEXT the product renders — and repo-internal data copied by a generator is in neither, so "prose" resolves to the second row and the pasted form is what the rule appears to ask for. Proposes a third row (read it from the source file) as a clause on the existing rule, since there is no independent authority to mirror when the source file IS the authority.
- `2026-09-03-a-gate-asserted-a-roster-over-files-the-product-documents-as-optional` — `agent-partials.test.ts` pinned, for each of three partials, that the agents holding a copy equalled every generated agent name; two of the three are optional in `readAgentFiles` (`readFileOptional` with a `""` default), in `agent.liquid` (`{% if criticalReminders != "" %}` around the whole section) and in the shipped `agent-summoner` playbook, so the gate could only ever fail on an agent authored by following the product own instructions. **A roster is a claim about the contract and this one was written from the tree** — all eighteen agents happened to hold all three when it was written, while the optionality it contradicts had been in the compiler since `461f60ca`. It also made a prompt technique mandatory two documents away: requiring the FILE of every agent transitively required `<self_correction_triggers>` of every agent, which `prompt-bible.md` #7 says has no floor. The roster is traded for the guard rather than deleted — the optional contracts now assert the `{% if %}` that makes absence legal, which is the stronger claim, since losing it compiles an empty wrapper around nothing into every agent that omits the file.
- `2026-09-03-a-sanitiser-that-spreads-then-overrides-passes-through-every-field-it-does-not-name` — `sanitizeCompiledAgentData` strips Liquid delimiters by **spread-then-override**, so a field nobody names is not merely unsanitised but silently forwarded: no missing key, no `undefined`, no type error, nothing to read but the rendered output. It had already missed `hooks` and `experimental`, both recorded in its own docblocks, and a third was live when the gate that would have caught them was finally written — `hooks[].hooks[].type` rides `...action` past `command`, `script` and `prompt`, and `agent.liquid` emits the whole map through `| json`. "It is a closed vocabulary" is not the defence: `sanitizeExperimental`'s docblock rejects that argument for `cacheTtl` on the ground that `renderAgent` is also the editor's preview path, where none of the CLI's Zod parsing has run, and `type` is the one member of that argument's own class the enumeration skipped. The gate landed first and the sanitiser followed inside the same programme, so the finding is `partial`: the instance is fixed and the general rule is not written down anywhere. While the two were out of step the live field was pinned BY NAME rather than by a shorter `toStrictEqual` — a gap encoded in arity is one no search for the gap can find — and retiring that pin was the fix landing, which is what a pin is for. Neither roster is written down: the fixture is `Required<AgentConfig>` so the compiler holds it against the model, every field carries a self-naming marker made of Liquid delimiters, and the render is `renderAgentFromCorpus` — the same one an install performs. Shown red for the right reason by deleting the `model` spread and again by deleting the `experimental` spread, both restored.
- `2026-09-03-a-spec-name-claiming-a-field-it-never-checks-makes-the-defect-read-as-the-design` — the `- Use when:` line of every compiled sub-agent's `<skill_activation_protocol>` is the only thing telling it when to reach for a dynamic skill, and `resolveAgentConfigToSkills` built it as `` `when working with ${category}` `` — the config's grouping key — while every skill states a real trigger sentence in its `metadata.yaml` `usageGuidance`, required at `z.string().min(10)` and already reachable on `matrix.skills[id]` two lines above (the same function calls `hasSkill` there). `meta-design-expressive-typescript` says "Use when writing or refactoring TypeScript functions, data transformations, store actions…" and shipped as `Use when: when working with meta-design`. **What let it stand is a test NAME**: `resolver.test.ts`'s `it("should include usage guidance with category name")` asserted only `usage === "when working with web-framework"`, so the grep anybody runs to check whether guidance is wired found a green spec appearing to say yes — CLAUDE.md's existing rule covers this shape only when a MOCK removed the validation, and there was no mock, so it could not reach. Nothing mechanical could either: both fields are `string`, the engine runs `strictVariables: false`, and the rendered line is grammatical. Seven specs written red first across three files, including the chain end-to-end through the real `agent.liquid`. The absence case was DECIDED rather than left open, since it is the half a naive fix gets wrong — an unknown id and a matrix skill stating no guidance (optional on `SkillCore`) both fall back to the category sentence, both pinned, on the reasoning `liveCategoryOf` one function above already applies and `externalSkillMetadata` states in as many words. One thing measured and deliberately NOT pinned, because pinning it would expand the fix's contract: every `usageGuidance` in the generated matrix opens with the words "Use when" (a census, and the second command in the body is the one that gets it right — one value is single-quoted and a double-quote scan reports one fewer while looking complete), so the smallest correct fix renders `Use when: Use when writing or refactoring…` throughout, and which of three remedies applies is a product decision. **Now `partial`**: the fix landed, the template dropped its `- Use when: ` label so the producer writes a whole sentence, and the row's open question is answered — the body's `## Correction` carries what that cost, including a second defect it did not anticipate (when the wording moved, only the POSITIVE assertions reddened; one negative went wholly vacuous and its twin kept its teeth only because the new sentence contains the old as a substring). The RULE half is still open.
- `2026-09-03-a-scan-guards-only-what-its-fixture-renders-and-its-pattern-can-match` — the baseline guard over every compiled sub-agent's template text passed on the day it was written and would have passed through a full revert, for two reasons that are one defect at two levels. Its `SHOUTING` pattern demanded four consecutive capitalised words, and the literal it exists for — `**DISPLAY ALL 5 CORE PRINCIPLES AT THE START OF EVERY RESPONSE TO MAINTAIN INSTRUCTION CONTINUITY.**`, which `agent.liquid` carried twice at HEAD — is cut in two by the numeral, so the one line it was written for was the one line it could not see; the spelled-out `ALL FIVE` variant matches either way, which is what made the pattern look right. And its fixture rendered `dynamicSkills: []`, selecting one branch of a three-way chain, so the `<skill_activation_protocol>` block — where "COMPLETELY WORTHLESS", "LYING TO YOURSELF" and "MISS PATTERNS, VIOLATE CONVENTIONS, AND PRODUCE INFERIOR CODE" all lived at HEAD — was never read by either scan. The shape that hides both: `offendingLines` answers `[]` identically for a clean block and for a block that never rendered, so coverage arity is invisible from the assertion. Fixed by a numeral alternative in the pattern (padding checked: `ONE TWO 3 FOUR` stays quiet, as do "The CLI has 13 commands and 18 flags" and "Use TypeScript 5 strict mode"), by `renderBaselineWith` taking the skill posture as a parameter, and by a per-branch marker assertion that keeps a scan from passing on an unrendered block — the dynamic one being `<skill_activation_protocol>` itself, which doubles as the positive invariant after the owner ruled the block stays. Shown red against HEAD's template through a scratch per-project override: nine of twelve failed and the output names the retired literal verbatim. `BASELINE_BYTE_BUDGET` deliberately still measures the no-skills posture, since a budget over a render carrying skills is a fact about a project's configuration rather than the template. **Follow-up, same day:** the four coercive lines the first pass measured as unreachable by either fuzzy pattern were a design fault rather than a dead end — one mechanism doing two jobs with opposite requirements. Catching prose nobody has written yet is irreducibly fuzzy; catching a revert to text deliberately retired is not, because that set is known and enumerable, so exact matching gets zero false positives and a pattern is strictly worse at it. A third constant, `RETIRED_FORMS`, holds eleven exact case-sensitive substrings, each carrying a one-line statement of where it came from, derived by rendering the pre-slimming baseline and subtracting every line the two fuzzy scans already catch. All eleven fire against HEAD, so none is dead weight, and all four previously-unreachable lines are now caught — twelve of fifteen specs fail against HEAD, up from nine of twelve. Excluded and said so: the two `**CRITICAL: Never …**` openers (rewritten rather than retired, and `PROHIBITIONS` already holds them), the bare `You MUST read those files` construction (an instance of a shape, not a distinctive line), and everything in `improvement-protocol.liquid`, which no `{% render %}` tag reaches, so a row from it could never fire. Also corrected: the docblock's unqualified 16,994 B, which reproduces in none of the three postures — measured through `renderAgent` at HEAD they are 15,410 B no-skills, 15,442 B preloaded, 16,950 B dynamic — and now states 15,410 B **with the posture it was measured in**, an unqualified count over a three-branch render naming three numbers and settling none.
- `2026-09-03-a-default-preserving-assertion-stops-discriminating-when-the-roster-goes-uniform` — the owner ruled every bundled sub-agent onto `opus`, which emptied the premise two default-preserving e2e specs rested on: `api-tester`'s `sonnet` default was the thing that made an assertion of the metadata value mean anything. What they still catch is a default that is DROPPED, because `agent.liquid` falls back to `inherit`; what they can no longer see is a resolver answering a hardcoded `opus`. A second assertion went vacuous in the same move and was not what anyone was looking for — the OPUS row of `carries every model the contract allows` survives only on its `config.ts` check, where an override that never arrived is an absent key rather than a coinciding value. The unit-layer fix is still open: `RESOLVE_AGENTS_DEFINITIONS` pins both mock agents at `opus` and is the one fixture in the tree free to be pinned off it.
- `2026-09-03-a-fixture-modeling-a-skill-absent-from-the-matrix-stops-being-absent-once-compile-seats-locals` — `compile` used to render every agent against `BUILT_IN_MATRIX` and only seat the merged (local-skills-included) matrix afterward, inside `refreshConfigTypes`; fixed the same day by seating it once per pass, before `compileAgents`. `warn-suppression-stops-at-the-harness.e2e.test.ts` had built its whole fixture on the bug as ambient behavior: a genuinely well-formed, correctly-installed project-local skill, assigned in the stack, to model "installed on disk but the matrix does not know about it" and prove the `... not found in matrix` advisory reaches a user through three spawn paths. Once `compile` seats the merged matrix before rendering, that same fixture skill IS found — `hasSkill()` and `statedUsageFor()` read the identical singleton, right next to each other in `resolveAgentConfigToSkills` — so the advisory no longer fires and three of the file's four tests fail. Confirmed from the failing run's own output: `Discovered 1 local skills`, `1 global agents rewritten, 0 unchanged`, and a slug-collision warning naming the fixture skill by its own id, merged into the seeded matrix. Not a regression in the fix — the fixture's premise expired the moment the sibling defect (`hasSkill` reading the same unseeded matrix `statedUsageFor` does) closed. Discovery only; the dispatching brief excluded test files from that lane.
- `2026-09-03-four-named-claude-md-test-assertion-rules-had-live-unswept-violations` — a targeted review of four CLI spec files against already-documented `CLAUDE.md` Test Assertions rules found four separate live violations, none newly discovered as rules. `agent-baseline-is-slim-and-positively-framed.test.ts` defined `offendingLines` / `retiredFormsIn` as bare local scanning functions rather than extracting them, against the rule's own worked example; moved to `__tests__/helpers/text-scans.ts` with a discriminating spec. `resolver.test.ts` asserted `.not.toBe("when working with web-framework")`, a truncated fragment no code path emits — `FALLBACK_USAGE`'s own docblock names two OTHER files with this exact defect, fixed and re-verified, and this was a third, uncounted site; re-bound to `FALLBACK_USAGE["web-framework"]`. `loader.test.ts` and `resolver.test.ts` each read an absence check through `record["key"] ?? {}`, so a key that silently stopped compiling produces the identical `[]` the assertion expects from a correctly-omitted field; replaced with a new asserting lookup, `entryAt()` in `element-at.ts`. And `renderAgent`'s own docblock states the CLI version renders only inside the trailing `<system-reminder>` block precisely so a release bump does not rewrite the cacheable prefix — `grep -rn "Compiled by" --include="*.test.ts" packages/cli/src packages/compile/src` returns zero, and reintroducing the version directly under the title in `agent.liquid` compiled clean and reddened nothing; `agent-provenance.test.ts` gained a render-two-versions-and-diff guard. All four mutation-proved. Generalizes the same lesson two earlier findings already drew from other classes (`2026-08-19-five-class-fixes-were-scoped-to-the-file-the-defect-was-found-in`, `2026-08-19-a-second-partial-manual-mock-survived-because-nothing-compared-it`): a prior fix's own docblock naming which files it checked is a hypothesis, not a census. Sibling in the same pass, filed separately as its own class: `resolver.test.ts`'s `experimental` field, present in the fixture and absent from the roster assertion.
- `2026-09-03-a-roster-assertion-was-narrower-than-the-fixture-that-fed-it` — `resolver.test.ts`'s "fields the template reads" block carries its own docblock stating why it exists ("a per-key assertion cannot see the next one to be dropped"), which is CLAUDE.md's roster rule in the file's own words — and its fixture set five tuned fields while its assertion object read four, `experimental` set and never checked, in the exact shape the docblock beside it warns about. `resolveAgents` carries the field through correctly; only the spec had stopped watching it. Bound both specs to one named constant, `TUNED_OPTIONAL_FIELDS`, so the fixture and both assertions read the same roster rather than three independently maintained lists. Mutation-proved by force-dropping `experimental` from `resolveAgents`'s output and watching the roster spec name it.
- `2026-09-03-the-external-skill-seat-is-the-one-writer-of-the-catalogue-that-takes-an-id-unconditionally` — `init --from` installs a carried external skill over a catalogue-owned id, writing the payload's bytes into the catalogue skill's own directory; the run exits SUCCESS and `doctor` reports 12 passed, 0 warnings. `seatExternalSkill` assigns `matrix.skills[id]` with no check on the incumbent, one line before calling `claimSlug`, which refuses exactly that collision on the slug axis. The author-time half of the guard already exists — the editor's `holderOf` checks the minted id and its docblock names this failure — so this is journey 33's shape with only the consume-time half missing. Hard to see because the next load's local merge inherits the incumbent's displayName, slug and category, leaving the description as the only tell. Filed open, with the narrow-vs-`external-`-namespace choice left as a ruling.
- `2026-09-03-a-derivation-read-the-catalogue-of-the-command-that-triggered-it-rather-than-the-project-it-was-writing` — three instances in one day of one shape: a value describing ONE project read out of whichever command happened to be running.
