The skills catalogue is a module-level singleton seated by the running command, and two things depend on it that are properly per-project — the RENDER of a compiled agent (`statedUsageFor` / `liveCategoryOf`, which reach it through module scope so no argument can) and the DERIVATION of `config-types.ts` (`deriveCategories` / `deriveDomains`). `compile`'s own pass rendered against the process-start `BUILT_IN_MATRIX` and seated the merged catalogue only afterwards; `recompileRegisteredProjectAgents` compiled a registered project with the triggering command's seat still in place; and `propagateGlobalChangesToProjects` took a `matrix` PARAMETER that every caller filled with its own. **None reads as a bug, because all three self-repair**: the project's own next `compile` derives the value from its own catalogue and writes it back, so the two commands undo each other for as long as both are run, nothing throws, and each write is internally consistent — the pair a fan-out leaves behind agrees with itself and type-checks. Fixed by `withCatalogueSeatedFor` in the new `lib/loading/catalogue-seat.ts` (seat, hand the catalogue to the body as a VALUE, restore in a `finally`; the load outside the `try`, because a failed load seated nothing to restore) plus `seatMatrixForPass` returning what it seated — the parameter is deleted rather than documented, since one beside a per-project seat could only ever be the wrong one. Guarded by an e2e byte comparison of the SAME `config-types.ts` across global → project → global, which is the only assertion that can see a difference consistent within each installation; the permitted case (a global addition legitimately widening the unions) sits in the same block, because a refusal pinned alone cannot tell a scoped rule from one that has stopped writing. `partial` — the rule half is unwritten, and the testing corollary is CLAUDE.md's key-order lesson arriving a second time.
- `2026-09-03-a-covariant-satisfies-clause-over-a-zod-schema-binds-nothing` — `agentIsolationSchema` was `z.literal("worktree") satisfies z.ZodType<AgentIsolation>`, which reads as the thing holding the schema and the generated union in step and cannot be: `ZodType`'s output parameter is covariant, so a narrower schema satisfies a wider annotation and the clause reports one direction only — the one a widening does not move. Measured: adding a member to `AGENT_ISOLATIONS` produced zero `tsc` errors while every parse of the new mode failed at runtime. **A union of one is where the two forms are indistinguishable**, since `z.literal("worktree")` and `z.enum(["worktree"])` accept the same strings until the array grows, so neither review nor any test could separate them. The four sibling bridge schemas were always right for a reason that is not a better check but no check at all — they CONSUME the const array, so the accepted set is the union's members by construction and there is no second list to drift. Fixed to `z.enum(AGENT_ISOLATIONS) as z.ZodType<AgentIsolation>` with the reason in its docblock, so nobody simplifies it back on the grounds that the array has one member; the census `grep -rn 'satisfies z\.' src e2e scripts` now returns only that docblock. Three documents corrected with it, one of which had written the false claim out as an explanation. `partial` — proposes a `typescript-types-bible.md` rule (derive from the array, never assert the relationship) and names why CLAUDE.md's `as const satisfies` rule for expected-value constants is not the same case.
- `2026-09-05-a-wrapper-that-keeps-the-padding-leaves-the-field-a-sliver-and-every-locator-still-passes` — the shared `Input` is a bare field whose comment said the border belongs to the wrapper and said nothing about the padding, so all four of the editor's bordered fields kept it: a ring inside the border, on the field's own fill, belonging to neither and dead to a press. The search field's input was 803×22 inside an 838×57 box, and pressing 2px inside any corner left `document.activeElement` on `BODY`. 507 e2e tests passed throughout, because `grep -rn "mouse.click" e2e` returned nothing — every locator action in the suite dispatches at the field's own box, which was live. A `<label>` wrapper is the trap that looks like the fix: `marketplace-dialog.tsx` already had one, so its corners focused the field while dropping the caret at the END of the value (`selectionStart === 10` for `owner/repo`), and it was the one site of the four that passed the new spec's first red run
- `2026-09-05-a-background-paints-under-its-own-padding-so-collapsing-the-gutter-only-resized-the-button` — the editor's filter band collapsed its gutters when it pinned, under a comment saying that is "what lets #242320 reach the viewport edge" — but a background paints its own padding box, so the fill was already there and nothing had to move. The left half was still needed for a reason nobody wrote down (the search field's HIT AREA has to reach the bleed edge); the right half was copied from it and re-homed inside the add-skill block as `pr-gutter`, which does not relocate space, it resizes the child: 169.94px wide around an 81.94px label, its right edge on the band's own 1270 and 48.41px past where it belongs, so the design's hairline was drawn around a strip with the label adrift in it. **The suite had a test written for this very control and it could not see it** — `add-skill trades its fill for a hairline once stuck` pins `background-color` and `box-shadow` in both states, and a paint channel says what a control is painted with and nothing about the box the paint lands on; 514 tests passed, including the axe audit of the pinned bar. `grep -rln "toHaveCSS" specs | xargs grep -ln "boundingBox"` returned one file of five, and not the one owning this bar. Proposes **a treatment assertion is not an extent assertion** for `apps/editor/e2e/README.md`, beside the hit-area rule its sibling row added
- `2026-09-05-objectcontaining-relaxes-one-level-so-a-nested-literal-inside-a-negated-matcher-can-never-match` — `expect.objectContaining` matches its OWN keys as a subset and then compares every value structurally, so `objectContaining({ skill: { id } })` asks for a `skill` with exactly one field where `GridSkill` has seven and matches nothing the grid can produce. Under `not.toContainEqual` that is an assertion satisfied by every possible input, including one holding the skill it names — and the negation is why it was invisible, since the positive spelling fails on its first run. It had a green reason to pass as well (the domain chip really did filter the skill out), so it was right about the world and incapable of noticing when EDITOR-79 stopped that being true. Census by bracket-matching each call's argument for a `key: {` inside it: 39 `objectContaining` sites across both workspaces, one violation, now zero.
- `2026-09-05-the-router-scrolls-to-the-top-after-mount-effects-so-anything-positioning-the-page-on-arrival-is-silently-undone` — TanStack Router subscribes its own scroll reset to `onRendered` in the router constructor, and the initial load is a navigation it was never passed `resetScroll: false` for, so a mount effect that scrolls the page from the address runs, scrolls correctly, and is undone 37ms later with no error: traced by trapping `window.scrollTo` and printing a stack for every call. The router's designed escape is the `#hash` branch, unavailable to a position addressed through a validated search param. Two properties make it worse than an ordering bug — nothing in types, lint or a component test can see it, and StrictMode's double-invoke makes the broken version pass in development, which is what the production-build check caught. Resolved by subscribing to `onRendered` from a LAYOUT effect, since the emit is a sibling's layout effect and a passive subscription is installed after the only emit a plain arrival produces.
- `2026-09-19-codex-writes-its-home-on-every-start-version-included-unless-that-home-is-a-temp-dir` — Codex 0.155.1 links helper binaries under `$CODEX_HOME/tmp/arg0` on every start outside the temp dir, `--version` included, so an availability probe is a spawn door like any other, and a leak test run from a `/tmp` HOME passes because the write never happens. The lane pins its probe and snapshots mtimes; the README rule says neither yet
- `2026-09-20-a-path-helper-that-names-one-layout-turns-every-absence-it-asserts-into-a-tautology` — a shared test path builder that NAMES one of two live layouts does not merely point at the wrong directory: it reinterprets every assertion built on it, and in two directions a reader cannot tell apart. Positive assertions go red and get fixed (161 e2e, 50 unit, all repaired here); negative ones go silently, permanently TRUE. `expectCleanUninstall(dir, {removeConfig:true})` claimed only that `.claude-src/` was absent, which no install this release creates ever has — eleven call sites asserting nothing, and green with the whole new pair left on disk. `four-surfaces.ts` is the third shape and the worst, because it reads as positive: it handed `path.join(dir, DIRS.CLAUDE_SRC)` to two `tsc` probes, so "the generated pair type-checks" and "it still narrows" were being asked of a file that was not there. Fixed by resolving through the product's own `sourceFolderInUse` in the helpers and routing every whole-folder absence through `expectNoSourceFolder`. The rule proposed: a SHARED path builder resolves, a SPEC pins — and when a rename makes a suite greener, read the negatives, because no gate can see an assertion that stopped being able to fail.
- `2026-09-20-a-substitution-with-no-escape-eats-the-one-paragraph-that-teaches-it` — a compile step that rewrites one token in otherwise-verbatim prose makes exactly one sentence unwritable: the sentence about the token. So the casualty is the authoring guide, and here it was `agent-summoner`'s playbook — every compiled copy read "The one exception is `.agents-inc/claude`, which the compile step replaces with ...", naming the token nowhere and teaching the one agent that authors partials to hard-code the folder literal the token exists to prevent. The pinning spec was the other half: `not.toContain(the token)` over the compiled summoner could only be satisfied by spelling the folder or deleting the lesson, so it went red exactly when the defect was fixed. Resolved with a backslash escape that composes (`\@@SOURCE_FOLDER@@` compiles to the escape), the refusal narrowed to partials that ASK for a folder, and a split assertion — the compiled agent must NAME the token, and no PATH in it may be rooted at the raw one. `NO_SOURCE_FOLDER` gained its first test, with the permitted twin beside it.
- `2026-09-20-a-fault-injected-behind-a-refusal-proves-the-refusal-and-reads-as-proof-of-the-rollback` — `migrate` plans before it moves, so the two `chmod` faults that `commands/migrate-rollback` injected to reach the `mkdir` and the `rename` were caught by the planner's own writability check one phase earlier. Refusal and rollback print the same closing line, leave the same tree and exit the same code, so both specs passed while proving the phase they were not about — and would have passed with the rollback deleted. Fixed by injecting each fault where its step can meet it: a file standing where the parent has to be created, and a file standing where the folder has to land. **Its subject was deleted the same day: `migrate` was withdrawn by owner ruling and the specs and modules named here went with it. The finding stays for the CLASS it names, which is not about that command.**
- `2026-09-20-a-surface-naming-which-of-two-things-is-true-can-name-it-from-a-constant` — `migrate` and `doctor` share one sentence naming which of a scope's two source folders is live, and both call sites passed the destination unconditionally, so the answer was a constant wearing a derived parameter's clothes — wrong in the exact arrangement `--adopt` exists for. Two siblings in the same command: the state a migration's own marker creates had no name on any surface, so content written into the old folder afterwards was invisible everywhere and `--adopt` could not reach it; and the run's writes into registered projects outside either scope were absent from `--dry-run` and from the report, under a spec whose assertion message claimed the opposite and whose probe named a file nothing ever writes. The common property is that the assertion and the constant move together, which is why all three were green **Its subject was deleted the same day: `migrate` was withdrawn by owner ruling and the specs and modules named here went with it. The finding stays for the CLASS it names, which is not about that command.** **Corrected 2026-10-04:** the one instance that outlived `migrate` went with `.claude-src/` support (CLI-913), so none survives.
- `2026-09-22-a-declared-seam-member-with-no-caller-reads-exactly-like-a-finished-one` — three defects with one shape, all in the C3 plugin-host seam: a MEMBER with no caller (`PluginHost.listPlugins` was declared and nothing was moved onto it, so the whole plugin READ path — `discoverAllPluginSkills`, `listPluginNames`, `getVerifiedPluginInstallPaths`, and `doctor` through the last of them — went on composing `~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json` and `<project>/.claude/settings.json` by hand); a ROSTER nothing made binding (`offeredPlacements` exists so a refusal reads data off the host, and nothing refused from it — the review row saying "no caller consults it" was wrong, `pluginScopesToSweep` in `uninstall.tsx` does, but nothing made it binding); and a SPEC NAME claiming more than it checked ("leaves the spawn wrapper **and the argument validators** where every host reaches them" read a roster of one name, matched with `export async function <name>`, which could never match a synchronous validator — deleting all three left it green). The common property is that **neither gate over this seam can see a caller that NAMES nothing**: both the ESLint import ban and the vocabulary census scan for `claudePlugin*`, and this caller composed the paths out of shared constants. Fixed, each mutation-checked: discovery reverted off the seam reddens 10 of 15 cases now that the spec mocks `hosts/host-for.js` rather than `plugin-settings`; the placement guard reddens 1 refusing nothing and 6 refusing everything (three of them in the Claude wire spec, which is what proves the door wraps the host); the validator roster reddens 1 where it was green. `isAvailable` and `listPlugins` gained `HostCallOptions` on measured Codex behaviour — `codex --version` WRITES `$CODEX_HOME/tmp/arg0/`, and `codex plugin list --json` is a function of `CODEX_HOME` and not of the directory it is asked from — and the same measurement corrected the seam's claim that per-project enablement is Codex's only per-project control: a project's switch is honoured only where the GLOBAL config trusts that project. Rule proposed: a seam is not landed until something reads through every member, the check is the CALL not the name, and the cheapest guard is making the CALLER's spec mock the seam.
- `2026-09-22-a-seam-field-the-implementation-can-never-vary-reads-as-carried-at-every-layer` — `PluginHost.listPlugins` was widened to carry `enabled` for Codex, and the Codex host pinned every `codex` call to the home directory, so the field answered the GLOBAL switch to every caller and the one state it exists for was unreachable. Three layers read as though it were carried and none could disagree: the seam declared it with its reason, the host returned Codex's own `enabled` field, and the unit spec asserted the whole invocation with `toStrictEqual` — `cwd: HOME` included — so the defect was RECORDED as the contract by the file whose job is to say which command ran from where. **It corrects the claim in the row above**, that `codex plugin list --json` is a function of `CODEX_HOME` and not of the directory it is asked from: the MEMBERSHIP is, the switch is not. Re-derived on the pinned 0.155.1 with the global `config.toml` deleted first — trusted project asked from the project answers `enabled: false`, the same installation asked from the home directory answers `true`, and from a SUBDIRECTORY it answers `true` again unless the project is a git repository. Fixed by making the directory a required argument of `runCodex`, so every call site states where it asks from: writes from home, the listing from the project. Mutation-checked — reverting reddens exactly the listing case. Rule proposed: never add a field to a shared type for one implementation without a spec holding THAT implementation to producing more than one of its values.
- `2026-09-22-the-ban-written-for-the-class-no-selector-can-see-is-the-one-half-with-no-spec` — the host-path ban is two halves under two rules, and only the LITERAL half had a spec: `grep -rln 'A host path is resolved, not composed' src e2e` answered nothing. That is the wrong half to leave unheld — the symbol ban exists because the literal selectors cannot see `path.join(root, CLAUDE_DIR)`, which is how two of the four Claude leaks found by driving the Codex lane got in. `no-restricted-imports` does not merge its options across flat-config blocks and `eslint.config.js` restates that rule in twelve of them, so nothing could tell a restated group from a dropped one; nor could anything hold the eleven-file backlog to its own "may only shrink". Both sibling bans already carried both halves. Fixed by `host-path-symbols-are-funnelled.test.ts`, mutation-checked three ways (`importNames` dropped so the module is banned, a twelfth file added to the backlog, the funnel clause deleted). No product change. One trap recorded: `no-restricted-imports` renders its own sentence first and appends the configured message, so the sibling spec's `startsWith` answers false for every fixture and the file goes green having measured nothing — it has to be `includes`. Rule proposed: a ban written in two halves needs two specs, and the half with no literal spelling to match is the one to write first.
- `2026-09-22-a-control-whose-fixture-cannot-succeed-reddens-for-the-change-that-landed-beside-it` — the one positive control among six refusals in `codex-refusal-on-the-read-path` asserted `compile` exits SUCCESS over a `config.ts` naming an eject-mode skill that was never written to disk, so discovery found nothing and the run hard-errored `No skills found` — a fixture that could not have exited anything else. The expensive half is what its red LOOKED like: it stood beside the lane that had just landed the Codex sub-agent renderer and was twice attributed to it, while the identical configuration under `.agents-inc/claude/` fails with the same message and `runCompilePasses` is byte-identical to HEAD. The cause underneath is a helper that describes one host: `createLocalSkill` composes `.claude/skills` in its BODY, and an ejected project skill on Codex lives at `<repo>/.agents/skills`, so the planted tree reads to every command as an empty installation rather than as a mistake. Proposes that a control asserting SUCCESS owes a fixture that can succeed, that a reddening control is run against its other arm before being attributed, and that a layout a helper composes belongs in its signature.
- `2026-09-23-a-path-held-to-a-pattern-written-for-a-reference-refuses-the-machine-it-is-running-on` — `validateMarketplaceSource` held every marketplace source to `SAFE_PLUGIN_PATH_PATTERN`, which admits no space, and a marketplace source is either a reference a user types (`owner/repo`) or a DIRECTORY on this machine, whose name the filesystem decides — under `/Users/My Name`, one with a space. Codex accepts the path: measured on the pinned 0.155.1. The pattern now applies to a reference only; nothing re-reads a source, because `execCommand` never passes `shell`.
- `2026-09-25-a-mock-tool-call-no-spec-sends-was-never-checked-against-the-binary-and-the-binary-refuses-it` — `applyPatchCall` in `e2e/fixtures/codex-responses-mock.ts` builds a `custom_tool_call` named `apply_patch` and has no caller anywhere, so it had never reached the pinned binary — and the pinned binary refuses it. Measured on `@openai/codex` 0.155.1: a delegated sub-agent under `mockProviderConfig` is offered `exec_command` and no `apply_patch`, the call is refused on stderr with `unsupported custom tool call: apply_patch`, the file is not written and `codex exec` exits 0. Proposes: a helper standing in for a third party's wire format ships with a caller that drives the pinned binary, or is deleted.
- `2026-09-25-a-spawn-door-naming-the-binary-through-any-other-constant-is-invisible-to-the-door-scan` — `check-spawn-doors.ts` recognises a door by an array element that IS the identifier `BIN_RUN` or spells a path ending `bin/run.js`, and never follows any other identifier to its initializer. So a door naming the binary through a constant of any other name is no door at all: `check()` never judges its guard, and `e2e-runner-environment.test.ts`'s membership test stays green with it missing from the roster. Measured on a one-file fixture package, which threw "names no site that starts the binary" until the constant was renamed `BIN_RUN`. The scan is unchanged. Proposes: `isTheBinary` follows a local to its initializer, the hop `envSourcesOf` already makes.
- `2026-09-25-sideeffects-false-tree-shakes-the-compile-seat-so-the-shipped-cli-discards-compile-warnings` — `packages/cli/package.json` declares `"sideEffects": false`, so tsup drops `compile-seat.ts`'s bare import and says so on every build. Nothing in `dist/` seats `@workspace/compile`'s sink, which stays at its browser default of DISCARD. So the shipped CLI prints neither the Liquid sanitiser's warning nor the absent-skill one. Reproduced through the built binary: the injected syntax is stripped from the compiled agent and nothing is said. The unit suite runs `src/`, where the import executes, so it cannot see this. Proposes a `sideEffects` entry for the seat, or a seat the bundle must execute, guarded by a built-binary spec.
- `2026-09-27-a-refusal-keyed-on-what-was-discovered-refused-a-config-that-asked-for-nothing` — `compile` refused every run in which no pass discovered a skill, so an installation whose sub-agents the editor pinned on with no skill — which `init --from` compiles as base agents — could never be recompiled, and two `compile.e2e` specs pinned that refusal as a green invariant. The refusal now keys on a config that DECLARES skills none of which is on disk.
- `2026-10-03-a-count-line-that-pluralises-unconditionally-hides-from-a-positive-assertion-on-its-singular` — every count line in the install report pluralised its noun whatever the count (`Selected 1 skills`, `Compiled 1 agents`, `Installed 1 skill plugins`). Because the singular is a substring of the plural, a positive assertion on the singular cannot fail on the defect, and four text mirrors and regexes had recorded the plural as the expected value. `plural()` moved from `doctor.ts` to `utils/string.ts`, and the report, `utils/messages.ts`, `init` and `edit` use it. A census of the class still open in other commands is recorded.
- `2026-10-03-a-spec-claiming-a-terminal-by-istty-alone-stands-at-half-of-one` — specs meaning "a person is at a terminal" set `process.stdin.isTTY = true` on a worker stdin that vitest forks as a pipe, which has no `setRawMode`. Once `promptValue` put the terminal into raw mode before a prompt's first frame, every such spec that reached a prompt died on a TypeError. `standAtTerminal()` in `__tests__/helpers/terminal-input.ts` stands a whole terminal in.
- `2026-10-03-a-focus-ring-read-off-computed-style-passes-while-the-cells-clip-hides-every-pixel-of-it` — every focus-ring story in `packages/ui` asserts `getComputedStyle(element).boxShadow` is not `"none"`, which proves a ring was declared rather than that it can be seen. `LatticeCellButton`'s outset ring fell wholly in its cell's border, clipped by `overflow-hidden` or painted over by a selected cell's outline, so a focused cell and an unfocused one screenshotted byte-identical while `CellButtonFocusDrawsTheRing` passed. The ring is now inset and held by a focused-versus-blurred screenshot comparison in `skill-selection.spec.ts`; the eleven computed-style assertions are unchanged. Proposes a visibility check of its own for any control drawn inside a clipping or outlined container.
- `2026-10-03-a-spec-that-reaches-its-subject-through-another-fix-goes-red-on-the-way-in` — the bare-dashboard spec about the help listing chose Doctor with a key burst, which another fix and another spec own. With both fixes reverted it timed out on Edit instead of failing on `ROOT_HELP_USAGE`. It now chooses one key at a time. Proposes: a spec reaches its subject by a path no other spec's subject decides, and when fixes land together each is reverted alone.
- `2026-10-03-a-fan-out-that-keeps-one-list-of-skips-can-only-be-reported-in-one-sentence` — `PropagationResult.skipped` held every registered project a fan-out did not rewrite, whatever the reason, so `edit` and `init` said nothing about a project whose `config.ts` could not be loaded and `compile` reused uninstall's sentence about uninstalled content. Such projects now have their own `unreadable` list and one bare line, printed by `BaseCommand.reportFanOut`; every other skip (nothing installed there, another provider's installation, a failed rewrite) has its own list and line too. The Proposed Standard is unwritten.
- `2026-10-03-a-host-state-fixture-written-from-the-readers-premise-cannot-catch-the-premise` — every spec of the Claude trust check wrote `~/.claude.json` keyed by the project folder, the key the reader looks up, so fixture and reader shared one premise. Claude Code 2.1.288 keys the record by the git repository root and shows no prompt below a trusted plain folder; both were observed under a scratch HOME, and both now have red specs. Proposes that a fixture writing another program's file names the host version it was observed on.
- `2026-10-03-a-finding-naming-a-gitignored-file-resolves-only-where-someone-generated-it` — a finding lists the generated, gitignored `e2e/helpers/handrun.gen.mjs` under `affected_files`. The `check-findings-frontmatter` repository spec resolves entries with `existsSync`, so it passes only where the hand-run bundler has been run and fails on a fresh checkout, which is what CI runs. Proposes that `affected_files` names tracked files only. Discovery only.
- `2026-10-03-a-spread-over-a-record-of-arrays-replaces-a-row-it-meant-to-join` — `withKeptStackRows` put a kept skill's stack rows back with an object spread, which replaced the payload's array for a shared category. A destructive `edit --from` left the payload's skill assigned to no sub-agent. The rows are now appended per category with the file's existing joiner. Proposes: a join over a record of arrays uses the joiner; a spread only overrides.
- `2026-10-03-a-label-cut-for-display-was-read-back-as-the-description-it-was-cut-from` — `readCarriedSkill` read a carried skill's shortened `cliDescription` back as its description, so every re-share cut the description. It now reads the SKILL.md frontmatter. Proposes: a value derived for display is never read back as its source.
- `2026-10-03-a-findings-reference-to-a-gitignored-artefact-resolves-only-where-it-was-generated` — `resolvesUnder` in `scripts/check-findings-frontmatter.ts` resolves a frontmatter path with `existsSync` alone, so a reference to a gitignored file resolves only on a machine that generated it. The 2026-09-03 finding names `e2e/helpers/handrun.gen.mjs`, so the dangling-reference spec is green where the hand-run was built and red on any tree without the bundle. Proposes asking git, as the identifier scan already does, and naming the bundle's source.
- `2026-10-03-a-refusal-only-the-install-step-asks-lands-after-the-install` — both `--from` producers resolved a payload's `stackId` only when writing the configuration, so a stack the marketplace does not ship was refused after the plugins were installed or the skills copied. The pre-flight now calls the install's own lookup (`refuseUnofferedStack`) before the lists and the question. Proposes: a pre-flight refusal calls the write step's lookup rather than restating it, and the install path's throws are the census.
- `2026-10-04-a-mock-category-is-exclusive-unless-declared-so-a-join-spec-pinned-a-row-the-writer-refuses` — `createMockCategory` makes a category exclusive unless told otherwise. Two round-5 specs in `seed-apply.test.ts` therefore expected a kept skill joined beside the payload's in an exclusive category, a row the config writer refuses, and so pinned the `edit --from` defect as correct. They now use categories their matrix declares open, beside exclusive siblings that expect the payload's skill alone. Proposes: a spec whose expected row depends on exclusivity names it through the matrix.
- `2026-10-04-the-config-writers-refusal-landed-after-edit-from-had-removed-skills` — the config writer refuses a category that holds one skill given two, and `edit --from` reached that refusal after its removals, leaving a half-applied tree. `refuseUnwritableStack` now runs the writer's own compaction in `refuseSharedConfigBeforeAsking`, before the question. A second instance of the 2026-10-03 class above, whose census read the install path only. Proposes widening that census to every module a `--from` write passes through.
- `2026-10-14-a-findings-reference-that-climbs-does-not-say-which-root-it-climbs-from` — the dangling-reference scan now skips a reference that resolves outside the repository, and it judges "outside" from the repository root. A `../compile/` or `../matrix/` path, written from the package to a sibling workspace, therefore reads like the `../skills/` climb to a sibling checkout, and would be skipped instead of reported if its target went. Proposes: a frontmatter path never climbs to reach a file this repository carries.
- `2026-10-18-a-block-assertion-ending-on-its-own-last-line-passes-with-a-line-printed-under-it` — a spec pinning a block of output with `toContain` ends on the block's own last line, so a line printed under the block still matches: `edit --from`'s `+ web-developer [P] (agent)` spec stayed green while `~ web-developer (agent: skills reassigned)` printed under it. Census of `Changes:` blocks in the command specs: one open. Proposes that a pinned block end with the line after it. **Resolved 2026-10-19:** the rule is in `CLAUDE.md` › "Test Assertions", the open spec ends on the line after its block, and a census under any heading left 10 more for the owner.
- `2026-10-20-stryker-never-mutates-code-under-a-typescript-as-and-the-report-does-not-say-so` — StrykerJS 10's instrumenter skips every `as` expression whole, so the code under it gets no mutant: a list written `as const` or `as const satisfies`, and the `??` in `(config.skills ?? []) as SkillConfig[]` in `classify.ts`. No option or released version changes it; the upstream fix is stryker-js PR #6228, open. `docs/architecture.md` › "Mutation testing" now says to break such code by hand; no site has been broken by hand yet.
- `2026-10-20-a-list-built-from-a-directory-scan-comes-out-in-the-order-its-reads-finished` — fast-glob over several directories lists matches as each directory's read finishes, so `glob()` answered one unchanged tree in a different order on each run and a `build plugins` spec flaked. `readdir` order depends on the runtime (Node sorts, Bun does not). `glob()` now sorts bytewise, `plugin-validator.ts` scans through it so `doctor` lists a plugin's errors in one order, `listDirectories` sorts bytewise too, and `eslint.config.js` refuses a `fast-glob` import under `src/cli` outside `fs.ts` and `dist-staleness.ts`. Nothing stops a new order-sensitive `readdir`, and no standards doc carries the rule. Partial.
- `2026-10-20-a-control-for-a-host-refusal-ran-the-other-hosts-binary-and-ci-has-none` — the Claude control beside Codex's `plugin + project` refusal in `codex-offered-placements` ran a full ungated plugin install, so it spawned `claude` and failed on CI, which has none. It stayed latent for two weeks because no main push reached the e2e step. The control now stops at the PTY confirm and denies. Census: one sibling, `marketplace-name-refusal`, whose name claims an install it does not check. Proposes: a control paired with a refusal stops where the refusal decides.
- `2026-10-20-oclif-table-drops-its-borders-under-ci-so-a-border-bounded-cell-assertion-is-red-there-and-its-negatives-check-nothing` — `@oclif/table` prints borderless output when `CI` is truthy, and the e2e runners pass `CI` through, so `search-static.e2e.test.ts`'s `│ <name> │` cell assertion failed in CI while its two negatives checked nothing there. The cells are now space-bounded; proposes a `standards/e2e/README.md` rule that output a dependency renders differently under CI is asserted on what both renderings share.
- `2026-10-20-the-agent-frontmatter-matcher-reads-tools-as-a-list-and-compiled-frontmatter-writes-a-string` — the `tools` option of `toHaveAgentFrontmatter` in `e2e/matchers/agent-matchers.ts` read the parsed `tools:` as a list, while `agent.liquid` writes one comma-separated string, so any `tools` expectation failed, naming the first tool as missing. No call site passed the option. It now splits the string (`grantedTools`), and `e2e/matchers/agent-matchers.e2e.test.ts` runs a real `compile` and pins it passing on every tool web-developer is granted and failing, naming the tool, on one it is not. Both specs were red before the fix. Resolved.
- `2026-10-20-removing-a-default-hook-left-a-negative-about-its-notice-with-no-subject` — once compile stopped adding a default Stop hook, `edit-from-says-what-it-did`'s check that a failed `edit --from` prints no folder-trust notice had no hook to speak for, and passed with `edit`'s `hasIncompleteWork` guard deleted. The spec now declares a Stop hook on web-developer and checks the compiled file carries it; deleting the guard from the built `edit` turns it red. Proposes: a change that removes a product default greps for negatives about what that default caused, and either gives each its own input or deletes it.
- `2026-10-20-a-build-that-writes-over-its-earlier-output-keeps-every-file-the-source-has-since-dropped` — `compileSkillPlugin` copied a skill over its earlier plugin folder and never removed anything, so a file deleted from the source kept shipping in every later build of the skills repository's `dist/plugins`. A rebuild now carries the version forward, clears a folder holding a plugin manifest and writes it fresh. It refuses a skill name that is not one folder name, a plugin folder that would hold the source, and a non-empty folder with no manifest, which the next build would otherwise delete whole. `copySkillTo` in `skill-copier.ts` has the same shape and waits on an owner ruling on whether an ejected folder is the user's. Proposes: a step that regenerates a folder replaces its earlier output, removes only what it recognises as its own, never writes into a non-empty folder it does not recognise, and is tested by running it twice. Status partial.
- `2026-10-20-the-skills-readme-catalogue-is-hand-kept-and-nothing-compares-it-to-the-tree` — the skills repository's README lists every skill by hand, per domain, and nothing compares the list to `src/skills/`. It had fallen behind, with no Mobile or CLI section, under a headline count held in three places, and `docs/guides/commit-protocol.md` and `package.json` still described `stacks` and `agents`, which the tree no longer has. Repaired once by hand; proposes generating the catalogue from `src/skills/*/metadata.yaml`, or failing `scripts/validate-metadata.mjs` on a `displayName` the README lacks.
- `2026-10-20-a-skill-major-version-fix-scoped-to-import-paths-leaves-the-majors-shape-changes-behind` — SKILLS-18 named two skills' drift from their library's current major by import paths, but the same majors changed call shapes no import grep sees: Storybook 9's a11y `element` → `context` and viewport/backgrounds `options` plus globals; Apollo Client 4's single-`error` `onError`, untyped `ApolloClient`, and the removed `addTypename`, `variableMatcher` and `testing/experimental`. Apollo 4's hooks also lost their `TData` default, so an untyped `gql` document infers `unknown` whatever it imports. The shape changes were fixed from the published declarations. Proposes that a move to a new major is checked against the library's breaking-change list rather than a search for the old package names.
- `2026-10-20-a-skill-example-typed-by-hook-type-arguments-rides-an-overload-the-library-deprecated-and-useMutation-turns-it-into-a-compile-error` — the Apollo skill typed its hooks with type arguments (`useQuery<Data, Vars>`), and @apollo/client 4.3.5 marks every such overload `@deprecated`. A review suggested extending that convention to the untyped blocks; on `useMutation` that does not compile, because two explicit arguments select the deprecated overload, which keeps the hook's variables required at the mutate call (TS2554 at `createPost()` / `deletePost()`). Documents are now annotated `TypedDocumentNode` and the hooks infer. Proposes reading the overload's declaration at the pinned version before copying an example's existing shape. Resolved: the blocks were compiled verbatim under tsc 5.9.3 and 7.0.2.
- `2026-10-20-a-skill-example-can-pass-a-value-its-librarys-types-reject-and-nothing-compiles-it-against-the-library` — the TanStack Form skill passed a bare string to `setErrorMap`'s `onSubmit` on a form with no validators. It runs, but @tanstack/react-form 1.33.5's declarations accept only a `GlobalFormValidationError` there (read from the `.d.ts`, not a `tsc` run); it now passes `{ form, fields: {} }`. Nothing in the skills repository compiles a fence against its library, and the 2026-09-04 linter proposal finds undeclared symbols, not wrong argument shapes. Proposes compiling a block that calls a library API against that library at the version the skill documents.
- `2026-10-20-a-skill-claim-about-library-runtime-behaviour-can-hold-only-in-the-librarys-development-build` — a next-intl skill fix said `t()` on a tagged message reports `FORMATTING_ERROR` and renders `namespace.key`, but use-intl 4.14.9 compiles such a message only under `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`, so without values the production build returns the tags as literal text. Every source the fix cited ran in development. The skill now states both cases. Proposes for skill-atomicity-bible.md: a runtime claim is checked against the `default` export condition's build, and against `development` where `exports` declares it, by running the probe under `node` and under `node --conditions=development`.
- `2026-10-20-a-skill-lane-is-briefed-to-run-tsc-and-the-skills-gate-by-an-agent-whose-tools-hold-no-shell` — the skills lanes were briefed to type-check blocks with `tsc`, gate on `bun run validate` and read library APIs through context7, but the reporting lane ran as `skill-summoner`, whose `tools:` hold no shell and no MCP tools. `briefing.md`'s Gates field asks for the exact commands and nothing asks that the receiving agent can run them. Proposes, for the owner to choose: Gates names only commands the dispatched agent's `tools:` can run, or `skill-summoner` gains `Bash`. Discovery only.
- `2026-10-20-a-skill-typecheck-stubbed-the-block-it-imported-and-hid-that-the-library-had-retyped-it` — react-error-boundary 6.1.0 retyped `FallbackProps.error` from `Error` to `unknown`, so examples written earlier that read `error.message` are TS18046. The React Query skill's Pattern 8 was one, and a lane's type check missed it because it replaced the imported block with a stub carrying the same props. Fixed there with `getErrorMessage(error)`; web-error-handling-error-boundaries carries the rest of the census and is untouched. Proposes: a type check of a new block compiles every in-skill block it imports verbatim, never a stub. Status partial.
- `2026-10-20-the-last-fixtures-passing-the-whole-skills-registry-were-dead-exports-nothing-imported` — Five exported matrices in `mock-data/mock-matrices.ts` passed `...Object.values(SKILLS)` to `createMockMatrix`, which CLAUDE.md forbids, and nothing imported any of them (knip's unused-export list, confirmed by grep). They are deleted, and `grep -rn 'Object.values(SKILLS)' src e2e scripts` now finds nothing. Proposes a `no-restricted-syntax` selector for that call. The dead-fixture half belongs to the knip-report finding, which leaves gating to the owner.
- `2026-10-20-a-spawn-mock-recording-the-options-it-is-handed-cannot-see-what-the-child-inherits` — `e2e-runner-environment.test.ts` mocked `execa` and recorded `options.env`. execa 9.6.1 merges `process.env` underneath that before the child starts, so a variable a runner forgot to clear never showed up in what the test recorded, and the clearing check had to read each runner's code through `clearances()` instead. That check failed on any runner that cleared variables a different way, even with the same result. The mock now records the merged environment and the check is a behaviour test, shown red with an uncleared variable. Census: 5 spec files mock a spawner; the 4 host specs mock `child_process.spawn`, whose `env` is already merged by `utils/exec.ts`. Proposes a CLAUDE.md 'Test Assertions' rule: a mock of a library that merges its options records the merged result.
- `2026-10-20-an-absence-assertion-whose-allowed-half-observes-a-mock-checks-nothing` — two `init --from` specs mocked `writeProjectConfig` and asserted that a refused or failed plugin install left no `config.ts`. Nothing ever wrote that file, so the check held for any behaviour. Each file did pair the refusal with an allowed case, as `CLAUDE.md`'s refusal-pairing rule asks, but the allowed case read the mock's call count rather than the file. A dashboard spec's `not.toContain("Source:")` could not fail either: the label is `Marketplace:`. The writer is now unmocked and both allowed cases read `config.ts` off disk. Proposes: the allowed half observes the same artefact the refused half asserts absent.
- `2026-10-20-a-stubbed-collaborator-wrote-the-expected-value-so-a-spec-pinned-the-stubs-answer` — specs in `lib/operations` and `lib/installation` mocked project modules and then asserted either the stub's own answer or the arguments the stub was called with. `local-installer.test.ts` expected `skills: []` from a stubbed resolver where the real one answers a reference, and 7 config expectations held a generator stub's sentinel skill. All were rewritten against real disk; only the `PluginHost` seam, `os`/env and the console stay doubled. Hand mutations confirmed the rewrites. Two gaps the deletions opened in `compileAgents` (the skills it is handed, the caller's scope map) now have specs. Proposes: an expected value may not come from a stub of a project module.
- `2026-10-20-a-mocked-read-answered-by-call-order-pins-the-order-files-are-read-in` — `loader.test.ts` fed a mocked `readFile` with `mockResolvedValueOnce` chains (7; census of the file at HEAD). They pinned the order the loader reads metadata.yaml and SKILL.md, and the order glob lists files. One spec served unparseable YAML to every read, so its "instead of loading it" held however the loader behaved. Every read is now answered by its path (`filesOnDisk`). Proposes a CLAUDE.md "Test Assertions" rule: answer a mocked read by its path, and keep `…Once` for one resource whose answer changes over time.
- `2026-10-20-an-output-count-over-a-mocked-collaborator-silences-the-collaborators-own-lines` — a spec that mocks a collaborator which itself logs, then asserts an exact count or absence of logger output, pins output no run produces. `stacks-loader.test.ts` pinned one verbose line for a missing stacks file where the real run prints two (`loadConfig`'s `Config not found at` comes first). `matrix-loader.test.ts` pinned no warning for a schema-refused frontmatter where `parseFrontmatter` warns once. Both were rewritten over real files and pinned to the whole real output. A census (a sample with a path-fragment net) found one unverified candidate, `plugins/plugin-discovery.test.ts`; `plugin-settings.test.ts` was ruled out because `utils/fs` logs nothing. Proposes a CLAUDE.md 'Test Assertions' sibling of the mocked-validation rule. Status partial.
- `2026-10-20-a-catch-reached-only-by-stubbing-a-collaborator-into-a-failure-it-cannot-make` — two `plugin-discovery.test.ts` specs reached the outer `catch` of `discoverAllPluginSkills` and `listPluginNames` by stubbing `fileExists` to reject, which fs-extra's `pathExists` never does. `validatePluginManifest`'s `Manifest file not found` branch is likewise unreachable through `validatePlugin`. The specs were deleted, and so are the dead catches and branch. Proposes: a stub answers only what its real collaborator can answer — name the production input that makes it throw, or the branch is dead.
- `2026-10-20-a-helper-production-calls-in-its-own-file-and-only-tests-import-passes-the-tested-exports-gate` — twelve helpers in `lib/` (resolver, schemas, source-validator, versioning, agent-plugin-compiler, plugin-validator) were exported only so a test could call them. `tested-exports-reach-production` passes them because production invokes each one inside its own module. They were rewritten through their public entry and unexported in this lane; the rest of `packages/cli` has not been censused. Proposes: `npx knip --workspace packages/cli --production --include exports,types` as a census, not a gate.
- `2026-10-20-knip-cannot-see-the-vendored-copy-so-a-cli-type-the-matrix-package-re-exports-reads-as-unused` — knip lists `ValidationError` in `packages/cli/src/cli/types/matrix.ts` as unused. `packages/matrix/src/index.ts` re-exports it from the byte-for-byte vendored copy, and `packages/compile` imports it. knip ignores `src/vendor/**`, and the copy is a different file anyway, so it judges a vendored CLI type only by readers inside `packages/cli`. Any type that reaches another workspace only through the copy shows up as a false entry. Removing such an export would be caught (the matrix re-export stops compiling after `generate:matrix`), so the cost is a false row on every `deps:dead` worklist. Proposes `/** @public */`, which knip never reports (`isAlwaysIgnored`), or a note beside the ignore in `knip.jsonc`; the tag would be a new convention, so it is the owner's call. Discovery only.
- `2026-10-20-a-spy-on-inks-clear-passed-in-a-run-where-clear-does-nothing` — `prompt-confirm-holds-the-terminal.test.tsx` mocked the project's own `render.js` to spy on each Ink instance's `clear` and asserted the call. Ink 7 erases only when it paints interactively, and a vitest worker's stdout is no TTY, so `clear()` did nothing in the run that judged the spec. The spec now feeds a TTY stdout to `@xterm/headless` and reads the screen. Proposes: never assert that a terminal effect was called; assert what the screen shows.
- `2026-10-20-the-stand-at-terminal-census-misses-a-tty-stood-by-define-property` — the `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` census for hand-stood terminals greps only `stdin.isTTY = true`, and came back empty while `prompt-confirm-holds-the-terminal.test.tsx` stood stdin at a terminal through `Object.defineProperty`. That spec now calls `standAtTerminal()`. Proposes: a two-form census grep.
- `2026-10-20-a-comment-purge-deleted-the-mutation-check-records-the-e2e-standard-requires-and-cites` — commit 753afca8's comment pass deleted the mutation-check records and ruling citations from nine `e2e/lifecycle/` specs as history, while `e2e/README.md` requires the record and both it and `anti-patterns.md` still cite `install-update-source-drift`'s as the worked example; the purge criteria and the mutation rule conflict, and no honest gate can check that a record exists because a sentence satisfies it.
- `2026-10-20-lifecycle-specs-took-expected-values-from-the-production-code-they-check` — 18 lifecycle specs imported production values, which the e2e standard bans. In eleven, the expected value or the reader came from the code under test: doctor glyphs from UI_SYMBOLS (two rendering checks the earlier finding missed), plugin refs from buildMarketplacePluginRef, the recorded origin from EJECT_SOURCE, and the compiled roster read through listCompiledAgentNames. Thirteen specs now use e2e mirrors or literals. Five still import production code: preview-matches-install (the documented exception), typedKeys/typedEntries (CLAUDE.md requires them), cliVersion and DIRS.agents (no e2e copy yet). Partial.
- `2026-10-20-a-known-gap-docblock-outlived-its-it-fails-and-a-comment-pass-shortened-it-rather-than-deleting-it` — `plugin-build-versioning.e2e.test.ts` carried a "Known gap … Expected-fail" docblock on a plain `it` after 4885e5ae closed the gap. 753afca8's comment pass then shortened it instead of deleting it. `known-gap-retries.ts` ignores comments by design, so no gate sees a known-gap comment that has lost its `it.fails`. Docblock deleted. A census over `e2e/` and `src/` for known-gap comments on a plain `it(`/`test(` now finds none. Proposes: retiring a gap deletes its comment in the same change, and the census becomes a spec beside `spec-gates.test.ts`.
- `2026-10-20-a-request-log-assertion-pinned-the-revalidation-method-where-its-claim-was-one-request-and-no-download` — `source-revalidation.e2e.test.ts` required the unchanged arm's tarball request log to equal `["HEAD 200"]` and counted every GET on the cold arm. A conditional-GET revalidation (one request, no body, same output) would have failed both. Both arms now count downloads (`GET 200`, the only answer the stand-in sends bytes with). Checked by rigging the logs: green on a `GET 304` log, red on a second download and on a changed source. `user-journeys.md` row 22 still says "exactly one HEAD and no GET".
- `2026-10-20-e2e-specs-import-production-values-the-e2e-standard-bans-and-no-lint-block-reaches-them` — 38 e2e specs import values from `src/cli/` outside `__tests__/`, against `anti-patterns.md`'s type-imports-only rule. In five `commands/` specs the imported value is the expected rendered text (`provenanceMarker()`, `UI_SYMBOLS.CROSS`, `EJECT_SOURCE`, `DEFAULT_PUBLIC_SOURCE_NAME`, `RETIRED_SOURCE_FOLDER`), so those assertions cannot fail when the value changes. Every `no-restricted-imports` block in `eslint.config.js` ignores `**/e2e/**`. The five oracles now assert e2e mirrors held by `MIRRORS`; the lint block is not added. Proposes `@typescript-eslint/no-restricted-imports` with `allowTypeImports` for e2e specs. Partial.
- `2026-10-20-a-comment-purge-deleted-the-ruling-citations-e2e-commands-specs-owe-and-the-standards-quote` — commit 753afca8 cut ruling citations and red-test/measurement records from `e2e/commands/` specs. `standards/e2e/` requires both, and the standards still quoted two of the cut sentences as live examples. Ten citations are back, each checked against the archive or git; twelve specs have their red or measurement record back, each checked against the spec's own assertions. Three citations have no ruling on record (`doctor-content` is an owner question), and two red records contradict their own spec and need re-measuring. Partial.
- `2026-10-20-an-e2e-spec-stood-in-for-the-cli-with-its-own-internals-and-one-passed-with-no-browser-reachable` — specs in `integration/`, `interactive/` and `matchers/` used the product's own internals where the CLI's behaviour belonged. `init-dashboard`'s editor spec stubbed the opener with a script that logged nothing, so its assertions passed with no browser reachable (measured). Others called `validateKebabCaseName` in place of `build marketplace`, read the shipped template out of `src/`, or computed expected plugin refs with `buildMarketplacePluginRef`. Six of eight are fixed; `fixture-configs-round-trip` and `fixture-marketplace-namespace` still import production values. Proposes: a stub for a spawned process records what it was handed, and the spec reads that record.
- `2026-10-20-a-root-tool-config-missing-from-the-lint-roster-is-linted-by-nothing-and-eslint-says-nothing` — `eslint.config.js` lints a package-root tool file only when `ROOT_TOOL_CONFIGS` names it. Three root configs added since then (`vitest.loadtime.config.ts`, `vitest.loadtime.preload.setup.ts`, `vitest.mutation.config.ts`) are type-checked through `tsconfig.json`'s `include` but have no lint config: `eslint --print-config` answers `undefined` for each, and `eslint .` skips them silently. Linted like the other root configs, `vitest.loadtime.config.ts` reported two `no-unsafe-assignment` errors. The three are now in the roster, both errors are fixed, and `root-tool-configs-are-linted.test.ts` checks every root file `tsconfig.json` includes has an ESLint config. Resolved.
- `2026-10-20-a-guard-that-a-scan-judged-something-read-the-registry-it-was-handed-not-the-verdicts-it-returned` — `check-enumeration-drift` and `check-mirrored-constants` guarded their repository runs against passing vacuously with `REGISTRY.length` / `MIRRORS.length` > 0. Both `check()`s answer an empty registry with `{clean: true, verdicts: []}`, so the guard read the scan's input rather than its result, and it was each export's only reader. Both now assert `check().verdicts` is not `[]`, and neither constant is exported. Census over `packages` and `apps`: 3 other length guards, each on an input the test builds itself. Proposes: both `check()`s refuse an empty registry, as the other checkers refuse inputs that would leave them judging nothing.
- `2026-10-20-a-spec-reaching-past-the-public-writer-is-invisible-to-knip-and-its-synthetic-key-hid-prettiers-short-key-rule` — `installed-format.test.ts` called twelve helpers of a module only compile's two writers import, so renaming one turned it red with every emitted byte unchanged; `knip --production` cannot see this case, because every helper has a production reader. It is rewritten through `generateConfigSource`/`generateConfigTypesSource` and checked against prettier, and 24 mutants were checked. Its 90-column synthetic key hid a defect: the writer moved a too-wide value below a key shorter than 5 characters (`name`, `id`, `path`), where prettier keeps it on the key's line. The writer now keeps it there, held against prettier by three specs. Resolved.
- `2026-10-20-the-assertion-message-task-id-lint-reads-a-lone-literal-so-a-concatenated-message-carried-one` — `TASK_ID_SHAPES` refuses a task ID in an assertion message only when the message is a single `Literal` directly under `expect(`. The house style for a message over 100 columns is `"…" + "…"` (a `BinaryExpression`), so `This is NOT CLI-841` in `packages/compile/src/contract/emission-scenarios.test.ts` would have passed even the CLI's lint (eslint --stdin over the HEAD text: the CLI selector gives rc=0; a `> BinaryExpression Literal` selector gives 110:9 error). The same file pair also carried history in a user-facing error (`the standalone writer this used to fall through to`), which no rule covers. The comment purge read it as code and the test prune read it as not implementation-pinning, so both strings passed between the two stages. Both are fixed. The ban is now `workspace/no-task-id-in-test-text` in the shared ESLint config and reads concatenated and template messages in every workspace. Resolved.
- `2026-10-20-a-test-of-a-package-private-export-is-its-only-reader-and-knip-counts-it-as-one` — `packages/matrix/src/built-in-agents.test.ts` tested a roster schema the package does not export; its only production reader is `sub-agents.ts`. Folding the schema into that reader changes no behaviour, yet it turned the file red while the other 399 tests passed. Default `knip` counts a test as a reader, so it exited 0 on that tree; `knip --production --include exports,types` named `agentDefinitionSchema`. Fixed: the file is deleted and its refusals are rewritten through `SUB_AGENT_GROUPS` with a mocked roster. The new specs catch all four schema mutants tried, where the old file caught three, and the export was dropped. Census: `--production` exits 0 for matrix, api and api-mocks; no other workspace was run. Proposes `--production` as the way to find a private helper tested directly, in workspaces outside `packages/cli`.
- `2026-10-20-a-comment-purge-deleted-the-docblock-that-narrowed-a-section-comment-so-the-section-comment-went-false` — a comment purge in `api-mocks/src/handlers.ts` deleted the docblock that had limited the section comment "the refusals below carry no body", so that comment then also covered the compose refusals, which do send bodies. The section comment is deleted, and a search of the section comments in matrix, api and api-mocks found no other case. Proposes: before a deletion pass removes a comment, it checks whether a section comment above depends on it.
- `2026-10-20-the-task-id-lint-on-test-names-runs-only-in-the-cli-so-a-matrix-describe-carried-one-past-every-gate` — `TASK_ID_SHAPES` is declared only in `packages/cli/eslint.config.js`, so `describe("CLI-846: …")` in `packages/matrix` passed the matrix lint (reproduced with `eslint --rule`). The test is renamed, and the dated history is removed from `SelectionScenario.why`, a text field no runner reads and neither check covers. The ban now lives in `@workspace/eslint-config/base` as `workspace/no-task-id-in-test-text`, which every workspace extends. Resolved.
- `2026-10-20-a-story-asserting-an-svg-presentation-attribute-passes-while-css-overrides-it` — `OneGeometryForEveryGlyph` in `packages/ui/src/components/glyph.stories.tsx` checked the glyph's `stroke-*` attributes. Any CSS on the element overrides those attributes, so the story passed (5/5) on glyphs drawn with `[stroke-linecap:round]`, and failed when the stroke width moved into CSS with nothing on screen changed. It now reads `getComputedStyle` on every shape the glyph draws. Census of such attribute assertions across all tracked .ts/.tsx files: 1 file at HEAD, 0 now. Proposes that `packages/ui/CLAUDE.md` › Gates say "an ARIA or state attribute" and that its rule about classes cover styling attributes too. Not applied, and nothing enforces it.
- `2026-10-20-a-persist-test-that-calls-merge-itself-passes-for-a-store-that-never-reads-its-slot` — eight editor store tests (7 in `ui-store.test.ts`, 1 in `saved-stack-store.test.ts`) read zustand's `persist.getOptions()` and called `merge`/`partialize` themselves. That pins the middleware's config, not what a visitor gets back: with `skipHydration: true` added to `ui-store.ts`, the old file still passed 33/33. All eight now go through the stubbed localStorage slot, and a store that never restores now fails 2 of them. No `persist.getOptions()` is left in apps/ or packages/. `resolved`.
- `2026-10-20-editor-e2e-specs-read-how-a-line-is-drawn-so-a-redraw-that-looks-the-same-reddens-them` — the editor's Playwright specs asserted how the page is drawn: class names, `::after`/`::before`, `boxShadow`, computed margins, `gridTemplateColumns`, `tabindex`, `data-active`, `data-theme`, and app helpers imported from `@/`. So a redraw that painted the same pixels turned them red. They now read what is painted or reachable: `strokes()`, `paintsWith()`, bounding boxes, `aria-current`, and keyboard walks. A review then found two of those walks one-sided, and both now cross both edges of the group. Proposes an `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` rule: assert what is painted, never how; a tab stop is checked by a walk that crosses both edges and comes in onto the chosen item. Resolved.
- `2026-10-20-a-limiter-stub-keyed-on-the-tests-own-address-pins-how-the-worker-spells-its-keys` — `stacks.test.ts` stubbed `CONFIG_WRITES.limit` with `key !== refusedAddress` and asserted `mock.calls`, so renaming the worker's key, which still limits per address, broke the test. The same lane also read a 401 declaration off an imported `createRoute` object, pinned a stand-in wrangler's argv, and read `astro.config.ts` as text where a render check existed. Each now goes through the public surface: a stand-in that allows one write per key, `app.getOpenAPI31Document()`, and expected colours taken from `inkRampSyntaxTheme`. Each was watched failing on a real behaviour change, and the key rename was watched passing. Proposed for `standards/editor-and-worker.md`; nothing enforces it.
- `2026-10-20-an-underscore-parameter-on-a-private-method-is-a-dead-parameter-no-gate-sees` — `argsIgnorePattern: "^_"` exempts any underscore parameter by its name, and `noUnusedParameters` is off, so a dead parameter on a function whose every caller is visible passes lint and tsc. `edit.tsx`'s `logCompletionSummary(_changes)` printed `✓ Done` whatever it was passed, and the parameter is now deleted. The census also matches the one-parameter-per-line shape Prettier writes; it found `buildResolvedSkill` and `resolveAgents` carrying one each, and both are deleted. Resolved.
- `2026-10-20-a-tracker-id-survives-as-a-field-name-where-no-task-id-ban-reaches` — `SkillAuditEntry.deferredToD306` in `skill-audit.ts` names a tracker ID, and a retired one: D-306 is now CLI-740. The task-ID bans cover test names, assertion messages, comments and documents, and an identifier is in none of them. Census of every workspace's source: this field is the only hit. Not renamed, because `todo/cli.md`'s CLI-740 row cites the field by name, so the rename and the row edit land together. Proposes extending the ban to identifiers, enforced by `id-match` or `no-restricted-syntax`.
- `2026-10-20-doctor-names-a-codex-orphan-agent-file-with-an-extension-it-does-not-have` — `doctor`'s No Orphans row printed `- <name>.md (not in config)` whatever the provider, so on Codex it named `stray-agent.md` when the disk held `stray-agent.toml`. The row now prints each file name as listed from disk (`unclaimedAgentFiles`). `doctor-content.test.ts` pins it on a Codex installation. The other literal-`.md` agent sites are recorded in `2026-10-21-a-census-for-the-agent-extension-that-greps-a-quoted-md-misses-its-template-and-glob-spellings`.
- `2026-10-20-knip-never-reports-a-command-files-exports-so-an-export-for-testing-nothing-imports-reads-as-live` — `knip.jsonc` makes every `src/cli/commands/**` and `src/cli/hooks/**` file an entry point, and knip does not report an entry file's exports unless given `--include-entry-exports`. So the knip report a command lane is handed is always empty for its file. `uninstall.tsx` exported `UninstallTarget` "for testing" with no importer; the export has been removed. `npx knip --workspace packages/cli --include exports,types --include-entry-exports` filtered to non-default lines also names `showDashboard` and `DashboardCommand` in `init.tsx`, both confirmed to have no importer, both left to their lane. The proposal is that briefs carry that invocation for command files. It does not propose turning on `includeEntryExports`, which would flag every oclif default export that no spec imports.
- `2026-10-20-a-cast-guarded-by-a-parallel-set-re-encoded-the-type-guard-in-the-same-file` — `seedToInstallSelection` checked a sub-agent name with a module-local `new Set<string>(AGENT_NAMES)` and then cast `as AgentName`, while the same file declared the `isAgentName` guard over the same roster, so the cast was unnecessary and counted as a widening cast for `check-boundary-union-casts`. The cast and Set are gone; the file stays in `COMPILE_BACKLOG` on its three catalogue casts. Census: seven roster-backed Sets repo-wide, none another guard-then-cast. Proposes no new rule: a `Boundary cast` comment naming its guard is the tell.
- `2026-10-20-a-review-asked-for-a-test-to-kill-a-mutant-no-input-through-the-public-entry-can-reach` — a review of `apps/editor/src/features/configure/lib/output-preview.ts` asked for a test to pin two inputs of `projectTypesExtras` whose mutants survived every unit test: the stack's categories and the global config's `selectedDomains`. No such test can exist. The function is reached only through `buildOutputPreview`, which models a clean machine. There every stack category is the catalogue category of a placed skill, already in `deriveCategories`, and the global domains are a filtered subset of the project's, removed by `new Set`. A seeded probe of 400 random payloads, each built for both providers (758 previews with stacks, 730 with global domains), left both mutants byte-identical, while a control mutant changed 2568 lines. The inputs stay as copies of the CLI's `buildProjectTypesExtras`. Proposes one clause beside briefing.md rule 9: a mutation finding that asks for a test shows an input, through the public entry, under which the mutant's output differs. Where none exists, it says "equivalent" and offers deletion or a recorded copy of the CLI's code instead.
- `2026-10-20-a-cast-matcher-satisfies-no-unsafe-assignment-and-a-shared-syntax-ban-is-replaced-by-any-workspace-block` — specs cast asymmetric matchers (`expect.any(String) as string`). The reason was not `toStrictEqual`: vitest types matchers `any`, and `no-unsafe-assignment` accepts `any` only in an `unknown` slot. A shared `no-restricted-syntax` ban would have been replaced where it was needed, because the editor and compile turn that rule off for specs and the CLI sets its own spec block. `workspace/no-matcher-cast` in `packages/eslint-config` now refuses the cast in every workspace, and the census sites are typed expected objects. Nothing tests the rule itself.
- `2026-10-20-a-dependency-knip-already-called-dead-stays-declared-because-its-report-is-read-by-hand` — `deps:dead` is report-only, so `gray-matter` and `operations/types.ts`, graded CONFIRMED-DEAD on 2026-08-09, were still there on 2026-10-20. The editor also kept `hono` and `server` after its API client moved into `@workspace/api`. `knip.jsonc` now names the tool entries and the dead entries are gone, so `knip --files --dependencies` exits 0. Proposes: add it to `deps:check`. That reverses the root `//deps:dead` note, so it is the owner's call.
- `2026-10-21-a-withheld-notice-was-left-as-the-only-notice-assertion-in-its-file` — removing compile's default Stop hook also took the trust-notice line out of the first spec's full-output `toBe` in `edit-from-says-what-it-did.test.ts`, the file's only check that a successful `edit --from` prints the notice, so stubbing the notice out of the built `edit` left the file green. The first spec now declares the hook and its `toBe` carries the notice beside the failure spec's negative. Proposes widening the census of the refused-and-allowed rule: when a change deletes a line from a full-output assertion, grep the same file for the line, and any negative still naming it gets a positive in the file or goes with it.
- `2026-10-21-a-plugin-version-keyed-on-its-source-stays-put-when-a-build-fix-changes-what-it-ships` — `determinePluginVersion` (`versioning.ts`) bumped a skill plugin's version only when its source hash changed, so the rebuild that dropped orphan files from the skills repository's plugins left 38 of them at their old version, and Claude Code's version-keyed plugin cache on the reporting machine still held some dropped files under it. The owner's one-off bump of those 38 is in the skills repository's working tree, uncommitted on 2026-10-21. A skill plugin's version now follows what its build ships: `compileSkillPlugin` hashes the earlier build on disk before clearing it and bumps the major only when the rebuild hashes differently (`readEarlierBuild`, `computeShippedHash`, `versionAfterRebuild`). Because it compares against the build on disk, the change of hash basis bumped nothing. Status resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-finding-has-no-stated-form-for-a-skills-repository-file-so-skill-lanes-annotate-the-path-and-it-dangles` — `TEMPLATE.md` rule 6 gives no form for a file in the `agents-inc/skills` checkout, so skill lanes wrote `src/skills/<path> (agents-inc/skills)` in `affected_files`, which the dangling-reference scan judges as a path and reports. Proposes a rule 6 sentence that such a file is written `../skills/<path>`, the form the scan skips, and a guard refusing a list member that contains whitespace. Discovery only.
- `2026-10-21-a-null-check-on-a-property-path-does-not-narrow-it-inside-a-nested-callback-and-a-harness-predicted-its-output-unrun` — once `CREATE_POST` was a `TypedDocumentNode`, the Apollo skill's create-post-form read `data.createPost` inside the nested `posts()` callback, where the outer null check no longer narrows it: TS2345 whenever the field is nullable. The fix round that typed the block had predicted its harness output and a TS18049 control without running either; both proved false, and the TS18049 claim had justified an unneeded post-detail rewrite. The value is now held in a const before the callback and post-detail is restored; compiled verbatim under tsc 5.9.3 and 7.0.2, the blocks print only the two undeclared-`Error` artefacts. Proposes that briefing.md label an unrun expected output as a prediction.
- `2026-10-21-a-roster-equality-rewritten-as-a-render-check-kept-only-the-rosters-direction` — a prune pass rewrote `agent-template-renders-its-partials`' render-tag equality as a check that each roster member appears in a compiled sub-agent, so a rendered partial could move to the unrendered roster and stay green. The spec now renders every partial on disk and asserts the found list `toStrictEqual` the roster, in order. Proposes a `CLAUDE.md` › Test Assertions rule: collect a roster's product answer from every candidate, not from the roster.
- `2026-10-21-a-command-spec-named-as-a-deleted-unit-specs-replacement-stopped-at-an-early-return-before-the-guard` — a prune deleted `edit.test.ts`'s unit spec for the eject guard in `migratePluginSkillScopes` and named as its replacement a command spec whose plugin never moved. `applyScopeChanges` returned at `movesAPluginSkill` before the guard ran, so removing the guard passed the whole suite. A new spec moves a plugin and a saved copy together and fails when the guard is removed. Proposes that a spec named as a replacement be shown to fail on a mutation of the deleted spec's subject.
- `2026-10-21-a-spec-on-an-isolated-home-cannot-tell-a-directory-argument-from-the-cwd-default` — `setupIsolatedHome` changes into `projectDir`, so a spec passing `home.projectDir` to a function whose directory argument falls back to `process.cwd()` cannot catch the argument being ignored; `detect-project.test.ts` lost its only such spec in a mock prune and a review's mutant survived the whole file. Fixed there with a spec that uses a directory other than the cwd. Proposes a rule in `setupIsolatedHome`'s docblock and CLAUDE.md: every function whose directory defaults to the cwd needs one spec passing a different directory, with the cwd-default grep as the worklist; `loadSource`'s `projectDir || process.cwd()` is unchecked.
- `2026-10-21-a-mint-time-schema-parse-no-reachable-input-could-fail-outlived-the-stubbed-spec-that-was-its-only-test` — `configToSeedPayload` (`seed/config-to-seed.ts`) ended in `installableSeedPayloadSchema.parse(...)`, which no input the mapper accepts can fail: `readStack` refuses the unwritable pair with the same rule (`isScopePairCompatible` returns `isSeedScopePairWritable`), external entries are already `seedExternalSkillSchema`-parsed, and models, codex models and efforts come from enums the seed accepts. The only spec that failed without it stubbed `isScopePairCompatible` and was pruned, so the parse's mutant survived unrecorded. The parse, its comment and its import were deleted. Drift is now caught by tsc (the return type), the spec "mints a payload the store's own POST schema accepts" and the store's POST validation. Instance of `2026-10-20-a-catch-reached-only-by-stubbing-a-collaborator-into-a-failure-it-cannot-make`. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-refusal-spec-run-without-the-gate-token-was-held-by-the-fs-tripwire-not-the-guard-it-names` — a test prune rewrote `regenerateConfigTypes`' home-refusal spec without the gate token, trusting a guard comment that read as redundant with the `utils/fs` tripwire. The tripwire then answered for the guard, and deleting the guard left the spec green. Under the token, which both production callers hold, the guard is the only refusal, and without it the home's global `config-types.ts` gets overwritten. The spec now runs under `withGateToken` and goes red when the guard is deleted. The proposed rule: a spec that pins a guard inside a token-holding path runs in the same state the production callers are in.
- `2026-10-21-a-helper-spec-rewritten-through-its-public-entry-kept-the-warnings-words-and-dropped-the-file-it-points-at` — A prune deleted the `checkDirNameMatchesSkillId` unit specs as covered by `validateSource`. The surviving spec checked the warning's count, severity and message one field at a time and never checked which file it points at, so a mutant setting `file` to the directory name left the whole `src` suite green. The spec now compares the whole issue with `toStrictEqual` and fails on that mutant. Three older specs in the file (malformed YAML, two cliDescription) still leave `file` unchecked. Proposes two additions to the sibling rule: mutate each deleted assertion rather than the deleted spec, and compare a record a user reads as a whole with `toStrictEqual`.
- `2026-10-21-a-deleted-e2e-spec-was-named-covered-by-specs-that-overwrite-the-file-it-read` — a test prune deleted the eject-integration spec that checked the ejected `identity.md` and `playbook.md` hold text. It named three covering specs: two write their own content over the ejected `identity.md` before compiling, and one checks directory entries only. So a build whose `eject agent-partials` wrote every `identity.md` empty passed all seven spec files that run it. A new spec compiles a minimal project, ejects partials, recompiles and requires identical compiled agents (`toStrictEqual`); it fails with either partial emptied. Second instance of the proposed rule in `2026-10-21-a-command-spec-named-as-a-deleted-unit-specs-replacement-stopped-at-an-early-return-before-the-guard`, adding that a spec writing its own content into the file a deleted spec read cannot replace it. Status partial.
- `2026-10-21-a-pin-rewritten-as-a-behaviour-spec-kept-only-the-check-half-and-a-substring-let-the-check-stand-in-for-the-writer` — a prune rewrote the pin on `generate:compile` and `generate:compile:check` into a spawn of the check plus a key-exists line. Nothing held what the writer runs, so wiring it to `--check` left every spec green. The chain spec's `toContain("generate:compile")` was also satisfied by `generate:compile:check`. The runner now takes `--compile-root <dir>`, the suite spawns the writer into a temp dir and compares the files with the committed ones, and the chain spec matches the whole command. Proposes: a pin rewritten as behaviour keeps a behaviour form for every value it held, checked by mutating each one, and a script-name assertion matches the whole command. Status resolved.
- `2026-10-21-doctor-resolved-skills-by-a-second-disk-lookup-that-counted-a-local-skill-the-loader-refused` — `checkSkillsResolved` fell back to its own `discoverLocalSkills` lookup in the project and HOME after a matrix miss. The loader had already merged both scopes, so the lookup only changed the answer for a local skill the loader refuses (a category nothing declares), and it called that skill found. A review asked for a test to kill the global half of the lookup, but for an accepted skill that mutant is equivalent. The lookup is deleted. A loader mutant shows the global control spec now covers the global half; the specs are in `doctor.test.ts`.
- `2026-10-21-doctor-lists-the-global-agents-folder-with-the-projects-codec-so-a-codex-project-misses-a-claude-global-orphan` — `checkNoOrphans` globs the global agents folder, which is resolved with HOME's provider, using the project's codec. A Codex project under a Claude global therefore never lists a stray `~/.claude/agents/*.md`, while Agents Compiled and the content check in the same run do read that folder. Not fixed: giving each folder its own codec would flag every Claude global agent a Codex project does not claim. An owner ruling is owed on which global a project on another provider relates to.
- `2026-10-21-init-at-home-names-the-project-scope-folders-so-a-codex-global-install-is-reported-where-it-was-not-written` — at home, `init --from` names `resolveInstallPaths(home, "project")` in its "Skills copied to:" and "Agents compiled to:" blocks. For Claude those are the global folders; for Codex they are not. The skills land in `$CODEX_HOME/skills` (else `~/.codex/skills`) while the block names `~/.agents/skills`, and with `CODEX_HOME` set the agents block names `~/.codex/agents` as well. The baseline tree already had this; Stryker surfaced it as surviving `isHomeDirectory -> false` mutants. Pinned by two specs in `init-from-with-a-provider.test.ts`, which pass now that each block names the folder of the scope its files were written at. The sibling home-directory branches are unverified. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-a-hand-mutant-under-an-as-can-be-served-to-one-suite-without-writing-the-shared-tree` — `docs/architecture.md` › Mutation testing says to break code under an `as` by hand but not how, and editing the file serves the change to every suite and typecheck running in the tree at once. Its "one run at a time" also cannot hold across parallel lanes, because every root config writes to the same `reports/mutation/mutation.json` and `.stryker-tmp`. On compile's changed lines, 19 hand changes under an `as` in `seed-to-config.ts` were served through a vitest config whose `enforce: "pre"` `transform` replaces one snippet exactly once. The file was never written; 17 were caught and 2 survived on a branch that only ever maps an empty list. Proposes: break such code with that plugin rather than by editing the file, and give each Stryker run that may overlap another its own `tempDirName`, `jsonReporter.fileName` and `incrementalFile`. Discovery only.
- `2026-10-21-a-mutant-only-playwright-reaches-can-be-served-to-the-dev-server-without-writing-the-shared-tree` — `docs/architecture.md` says Stryker cannot reach Playwright and gives no way to judge a NoCoverage mutant that only a browser runs. A scratch `.mts` Vite config that calls the editor's config and adds a transform plugin, plus a scratch Playwright config that points `webServer` at it, served `use-pinned.ts`'s `"data-bar-stuck"`→`""` to sticky-bar.spec.ts: 23 failed, against 26 passed unmutated. A file that matches no module id serves nothing and reads as Survived, so a positive control is needed (a served `throw` failed 32 of 46). Proposes: judge such mutants this way in "Mutation testing".
- `2026-10-21-a-deleted-prompt-bible-technique-leaves-its-number-unused-because-renumbering-re-points-ordinal-citations-in-records` — removing the completion-gate hook deleted `prompt-bible.md` Techniques #1 and #11, and §1 now runs #2 to #10. The numbers were left unused rather than renumbered, because records in files a guides pass may not edit cite techniques by number, and a renumbered citation finds the wrong heading where a citation to a deleted number finds none. One citation had already drifted that way (`summoner-context-engineering-2026-09-03.md`'s "Technique #10, Positive Framing"). Proposes: a technique's number is permanent, and a citation names the technique as well as its number. Not applied.
- `2026-10-21-a-mutation-exclusion-list-measured-once-kept-files-that-stopped-reading-source-and-one-that-was-deleted` — `READS_SOURCE_TEXT` in `vitest.mutation.config.ts` was measured once by a read recording nobody committed.
