After the REPO-44 splits it named one deleted file and twelve specs that no longer read source, and lacked the twelve that do, so ordinary specs sat out every Stryker run while source-reading ones ran against instrumented source. Re-measured with a scratch recorder over every CLI test file and rewritten to the measured set. Proposes committing the recorder as a script and re-measuring on every split, rename or deletion of a source-reading spec; nothing cheap catches a spec that stopped reading source.
- `2026-10-21-the-trust-notice-matched-the-compilers-hooks-line-so-a-hook-written-as-block-yaml-got-no-notice` — `claudeCompileNotices` (`hosts/claude-project-trust.ts`) reads every `.md` in the project's `.claude/agents/`, hand-written ones included, and decided on the folder-trust notice by matching the one-line JSON `hooks:` that `agent.liquid` writes, for `Stop`/`SubagentStop` only. A block-YAML `hooks:` key, or a hook on any other event, got no notice, though Claude Code drops a project sub-agent's hooks until the folder is trusted; that this holds for block YAML is inferred, not checked against Claude Code. The event scope outlived compile's completion gate until the owner ruled on 2026-10-21 that the notice covers every hook. `declaresAHook` now reads the top-level `hooks` record through `extractFrontmatter`, and any event holding a definition counts; the block-YAML spec in `the-claude-host-asks-for-folder-trust.test.ts` failed against the old reader. Proposes widening `clean-code-standards.md` 7.11 from SKILL.md to every frontmatter: read it through the shared parser, never by matching the line the compiler writes. Status resolved.
- `2026-10-21-the-golden-install-trees-compile-two-sub-agents-so-a-prompt-change-to-any-other-leaves-them-unchanged` — every phase of the three Claude golden install trees (`e2e/fixtures/claude-golden-trees/`) compiles `web-developer` and `api-developer` and no other sub-agent, so the tester-prompt change a brief asked them to be re-recorded for left them byte-identical, and `claude-install-byte-identity` passed with no `-u`. The tester instruction is pinned instead by `compile-tells-each-sub-agent-that-writes-code-to-run-the-typecheck`. Proposes no document change: a brief that changes agent partials runs the finding's census first, and asks for a re-record only when the changed agent is in it or the change is under `src/agents/_templates/`. Discovery only; status open.
- `2026-10-21-an-agent-plugin-still-versions-by-its-source-and-is-built-over-its-earlier-build` — `compileAgentPlugin` (`agent-plugin-compiler.ts`) took its version from `determinePluginVersion`, keyed on the agent's source file alone, and wrote into `agent-<name>/` without clearing it. So an agent plugin kept both defects the skill-plugin compiler no longer had: a build-only change shipped at the same version, and a file an earlier build left kept shipping. The default marketplace ships no agent plugins, and its `update-marketplace` script does not pass `--agents-dir`. `compileAgentPlugin` now reads, clears and re-versions by what it ships (`readEarlierBuild`, `clearEarlierBuild`, `versionAfterRebuild` over `computeShippedHash`), and refuses a folder holding its own source or files no build made. The clearing helpers are shared from `lib/plugins/earlier-build.ts`, and `determinePluginVersion` is deleted. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-the-apply-confirm-came-before-the-no-change-decision-so-a-true-no-op-asked` — `edit --from` asked its destructive-apply question before deciding whether anything changed (`confirmAndWriteCarried` above `hasAnyChanges`), because a carried skill was only known to change once written. Re-applying the installed configuration therefore asked, rewrote carried files with the same content on a yes, then said "No changes made." The write is now measured against scratch copies (`carriedSkillsThatDiffer`), the run decides first, and it asks only when something changes. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-the-fan-out-summary-counted-compiled-agents-while-its-line-named-projects` — the propagated-recompile line counted a registered project as rewritten only when its compiled agents changed. A project whose config pair the global change rewrote, with no agents of its own, read as "unchanged", and a T2 fan-out printed 0/0. A pair snapshot around each fan-out (`config-gate/pair-snapshot.ts`) now counts it, and the line reads "Registered projects: N rewritten, M unchanged". Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-an-agent-count-hard-coded-to-md-reads-every-codex-installation-as-compiled-to-nothing` — `plugin-info.ts` counts compiled agents with `AGENT_FILE_EXTENSION = ".md"`, so a Codex installation's `.toml` roles count 0 (`list --provider codex` printed "Agents: 0"). The dashboard's per-provider read now asks `agentCodec`; `getInstallationInfo`, which `list` reads, still does not. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-a-snapshot-read-ahead-of-a-degrading-fan-out-took-no-posture-so-one-unreadable-project-stopped-every-visit` — `fanOut` read every registered project's config pair before the fan-out, outside the per-project `try`, so one EACCES `config.ts` aborted every visit and the run blamed `config-types.ts`. An unreadable half now reads as a fresh symbol, so the fan-out skips only that project. Proposes widening CLAUDE.md's call-site posture rule to a new read placed in front of a loop whose members degrade one at a time. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-shared-e2e-fixture-loosened-for-two-no-op-callers-let-every-caller-pass-a-run-that-never-asked` — `applyEditFrom` was changed to confirm only if the question appeared, so every caller accepted a run that applied without asking. Making it strict again went red on seven no-op calls across five spec files, not the two the review named. They now use `applyUnchangedFromSeed`, which asserts the no-op. Proposes an e2e README rule: give callers on the new path their own helper rather than widening the shared fixture. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-the-sanitiser-landed-on-a-warning-no-payload-reaches-while-the-skip-warning-printed-payload-keys-raw` — the skip warnings printed payload keys, which no catalogue validates, with their terminal controls intact, while `stripTerminalControls` had been added to validation warnings that quote catalogue ids only. Both message functions now strip each key. Still open: `clean-code-standards.md` 5.8 says three chokepoints carry the sanitiser, and four other files strip for themselves. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-a-dry-run-against-scratch-copies-writes-wherever-the-payload-id-points` — `carriedSkillsThatDiffer` writes each carried skill under `path.join(scratch, id)` before `edit --from` asks, so an id like `../victim` writes outside scratch and the files survive a No. `externalSkillDir` has the same hole on install. Proposed refusing, in `registerExternalSkills` before anything is seated, any id that is not one directory name or that holds a terminal control. Superseded by `2026-10-21-a-carried-skill-id-off-the-wire-was-joined-into-a-path-unchecked-so-a-link-could-write-outside-its-folder`, which built it.
- `2026-10-21-a-carried-skills-round-trip-rewrites-its-description-because-a-share-reads-the-manifest-and-an-install-the-payload` — an install writes `cliDescription` from the payload's description, but a share reads the description back from `SKILL.md`. Where the two differ, the first `edit` → `edit --from` round trip of an untouched configuration rewrites one line and reports `files rewritten`; after that it is stable. `buildSeedExternalSkill`'s default has the same split, which made a round-trip spec see a change it did not make. Owner question: should an install write from the manifest? Open.
- `2026-10-21-doctor-reports-each-installation-by-re-expressing-two-helpers-that-read-the-provider-only-through-process-state` — `doctor`'s per-provider blocks need each installation's own reads without setting the run's provider, but three helpers read the provider only through `providerInUse`. `sourceScopesInPlay` now takes a `providerOf` parameter. `getVerifiedPluginInstallPaths` and `unofferablePlacementsFound` are re-expressed in `lib/doctor/installation-reads.ts` (`installedPluginSkillIds`, `unofferablePlacements`) and answer as the originals do when the hosts are read off the disk, the project's placement findings first; they are still second declarations of two rules. In a two-installation scope the content layer still reads only the installation in use. Proposes giving both helpers the provider parameter and deleting the copies. Open.
- `2026-10-21-an-optional-config-field-is-dropped-by-a-field-by-field-rebuild-and-kept-past-replacement-by-a-merging-set` — adding the optional `description` to the editor's `persistedConfigSchema` showed two ways an optional config field is lost. `pruneUnknownIds` rebuilt its result naming four fields, so every adoption and every storage read dropped it. zustand's merging `set` kept the previous configuration's value through `applyStack(null)`, `reset`, `importConfig` and `applySavedStack`, because none named a field it did not hold. A type check misses both, since the field is optional. `pruneUnknownIds` now spreads the config, `EMPTY` names `description: undefined`, and `importConfig` and `applySavedStack` set `{ ...EMPTY, ...config }`; held by `config-store.test.ts` and `persisted-schema.test.ts`. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-the-scratch-cell-is-pressed-on-a-scratch-selection-so-clear-and-start-from-scratch-does-nothing` — with no stack applied and skills selected, the first stack cell reads "Clear and start from scratch" and is drawn pressed, and pressing it does nothing: `isApplied` in `stack-grid.tsx` is `null === null` for the scratch cell, and `choose` returns early on an applied cell. After EDITOR-97, every CLI-minted link that is not a stack lands in this state. Discovery only; open on a product decision: whether the scratch cell counts as applied while something is selected.
- `2026-10-21-a-control-that-an-edit-wrote-a-projects-own-pair-is-satisfied-by-propagation-when-its-change-is-global` — journey 40's control, written with the refusal's global-scoped addition, could not fail: `propagateGlobalChangesToProjects` writes a global addition into every registered project's config pair whichever installation the run edited (measured by a variant that started the edit at HOME). The control now adds at project scope, which only an edit of the project can write. CLAUDE.md's refused-and-allowed pairing rule does not yet say the permitted case's change must be one only the permitted path can produce. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-a-cli-constant-another-workspaces-checker-parses-as-a-literal-broke-when-it-became-an-env-override` — `EDITOR_URL` in `consts.ts` became `process.env.AGENTS_INC_EDITOR_URL ?? DEFAULT_EDITOR_URL`. Two AST checkers require that constant to be a string literal: `check-mirrored-constants.ts` in the CLI package, and `apps/www/scripts/check-editor-address.ts` in another workspace. A package-scoped gate fixed only the first; the second reddened `www#test` and stopped `agents-inc:test`. Both now read `DEFAULT_EDITOR_URL`. Only the unfiltered root `bun run test` sees the second checker. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-an-env-override-read-at-module-load-was-withheld-at-every-e2e-door-and-not-in-the-unit-setup` — `AGENTS_INC_EDITOR_URL` is read at module load. It was cleared at all three e2e spawn doors and held by the rosters spec, but `vitest.setup.ts` did not clear it, so a developer exporting it got red unit specs on every printed editor link. The setup deleted it; the variable has since been removed, and `EDITOR_URL` follows `AGENTS_INC_API_URL`, which the setup pins. Nothing holds the unit setup against the module-load reads in `NAMED_ENV_READS`. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-a-carried-skill-id-off-the-wire-was-joined-into-a-path-unchecked-so-a-link-could-write-outside-its-folder` — a shared configuration's `external` key, the carried skill's id, was joined into paths raw (`externalSkillDir`, `scratchCopyOf`). So `../victim` wrote into `$TMPDIR/victim` while `edit --from` asked, and `init --from` with `../../../escaped-dir` wrote under `$HOME`. `externalIdsNamingNoDirectory` (matrix `seed.ts`) now refuses any id that is not one directory name or that holds a C0, DEL or C1 character, in `registerExternalSkills` before anything is seated and in `installableSeedPayloadSchema` at mint. The mint's acceptance of an escaping `external[*].files` key, left open here, closed later: `seedSkillTreeSchema` refuses a key through `..` or holding a NUL (the zod record-key finding below). Supersedes the dry-run finding. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-remedy-for-one-of-two-installations-named-neither-so-the-command-it-handed-out-refused` — beside a second installation every bare acting command exits 2, a rule only doctor's private `commandActingOn` applied. `recreateConfigFrom`, `uninstallNeedsTerminal`, `sharedConfigNeedsTerminal`, `sharedConfigExistingInstall` and `nothingInstalledToApplyTo` handed out commands without `--provider`, and `ConfigLoadError` recorded no provider. `providerArgument` in `messages.ts` is now the one rule, and `ConfigLoadError.providerToName` names the provider beside another installation. Still open: a lone Codex installation's remedies hand out a bare `init --from`, which installs Claude (reproduced), and `edit --ui --from` / `init --ui --from` beside two installations hand out a bare `edit --from`. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-every-skill-display-name-printed-through-one-accessor-that-returned-its-authors-bytes` — a carried skill's or a marketplace's `displayName` reached the terminal raw through `getSkillDisplayName`, which every plan, change and summary line prints, and through edit's added-skill line. A link cleared the screen and retitled the window from a piped `init --from`. Both now strip. Proposes naming the accessor in `clean-code-standards.md` 5.8, whose "three chokepoints" excludes it. The carried `description` was not checked. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-cli-line-the-www-docs-quote-verbatim-is-held-by-nothing-so-three-went-false-with-every-spec-green` — three verbatim quotes of CLI output in the www docs (doctor's No Orphans row on two pages, share's HTTP 429 line) went false when the CLI reworded them, while the specs pinning the new wording stayed green. `check-cli-claims.ts` binds the command table and flags; no check reads a quoted output line. The quotes were corrected and no guard added. Proposes, for the owner, a docs-pass grep rule in `documentation-bible.md` or a www check on `doctor` row messages. Open.
- `2026-10-21-a-config-pairs-sequential-writes-keep-it-agreeing-on-failure-and-read-as-a-missed-promise-all` — each config pair is written as two awaits, `config.ts` then `config-types.ts` (`writeGlobalPair`, `ensureBlankPair`, `writeProjectConfigPair`), which the expressive-ts skill's Pattern 7 reads as a missed `Promise.all`. The order keeps the pair agreeing when the first write fails, and nothing says so: no comment, and no spec reddens under `Promise.all`. Proposes a failure-path spec in `the-pair-writer-writes-only-what-changed.test.ts` and a `clean-code-standards.md` rule that writes describing one artefact are sequenced on purpose. Open.
- `2026-10-21-four-command-guards-are-private-copies-of-one-body-beside-the-base-command-that-hosts-their-siblings` — `refuseUnofferablePlacements` (`compile.ts`, `edit.tsx`, `share.ts`, `update.ts`) and `decodeSeedOrFail`, `registerExternalSkillsOrFail` and `writeCarriedSkills` (`init.tsx`, `edit.tsx`) are private copies of one body each, in commands sharing the `BaseCommand` that hosts their siblings, so a change to one copy reaches only the commands its author remembers. No check reports a duplicated body. Proposes hoisting each to `BaseCommand`. Open.
- `2026-10-21-a-dedupe-in-the-scope-diff-could-never-fire-because-inheritance-already-required-no-global-entry` — `computeScopeDiff` filtered global tombstones against the inherited ids, but an entry counted as inherited only when no tombstone shared its id, so the filter removed nothing and survived Stryker as a mutant that invites a spec for an unreachable state. The rewrite partitions the vacated global installs into inherited and removed, so neither dedupe is written. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-promise-all-over-two-awaits-that-can-each-reject-changes-which-error-the-user-sees` — the read-side companion of the config-pair finding above. Expressive-ts Pattern 7 turns independent sequential awaits into `Promise.all`, which preserves behaviour only when at most one member can reject (otherwise timing picks the error the user sees, e.g. `buildInstallConfig`'s stack refusal against `ConfigLoadError`) and no member logs (concurrent `verbose`/`warn` lines interleave, as in `discoverInstalledSkills`). Over a sample of `src/cli/lib`, three safe sites were converted and the rest left sequential. Proposes the read-side rule in `clean-code-standards.md` and a delayed double-failure spec per site, `buildInstallConfig` first. Open.
- `2026-10-21-the-retired-folder-notice-names-no-root-so-a-home-only-folder-reads-as-the-projects` — the `init` hook checks the cwd and HOME for `.claude-src/`, but `retiredSourceFolderUnsupported` names neither root, so a folder only in HOME reads, inside a project, as the project's, and "Move it to .agents-inc/claude/" points at the wrong directory (reproduced with `compile`). `bare-run.test.ts` and `e2e/pages/constants.ts` pin the root-less text, and journey 52 records it COVERED. Proposes naming each root that holds the folder, and widening CLAUDE.md's single-path rule for scope-split artifacts to any path from a multi-root check. Every run now names each root, HOME as `~` (CLI-931, owner 2026-10-24); the widening is not written. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-the-editor-spelled-what-a-parse-failure-may-send-to-sentry-at-three-boundaries` — what a failed parse may report (the first path segment and the zod code, since deeper segments can name a private repository) was mapped inline in the config store's `merge`, `readSavedMarketplaces` and `catalog.ts`'s `firstSegment`: three copies of a privacy rule, one beside catalog.ts's legitimate whole-path display form. `reportableIssues` in `lib/observability/report.ts` is now the one spelling, and the three stores' pins are unchanged. No honest gate exists. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-catch-over-two-awaits-needs-a-failing-spec-at-each-or-a-narrowed-try-survives` — `fetchManifest` in `apps/server/src/crawl.ts` holds `fetch` and `response.text()` under one catch that drops a skill whose SKILL.md fails, and no spec in `crawl.test.ts` reaches it: the unreachable-repository spec fails every `fetch`, so the crawl dies before any SKILL.md is read. Three hand mutants (rethrow, `try` narrowed to either await) left all 31 specs green, and under each one dropped connection stops the index build for every repository. A two-case spec (rejected fetch, erroring body stream) kills all three from a scratch copy; it is unwritten, the test file being outside the lane. Proposes a failing case at each await under one catch. Open.
- `2026-10-21-a-spec-proposed-for-another-lanes-test-file-can-be-run-from-outside-the-tree` — `briefing.md` rules 9 and 11 leave a test proposed for another lane's file unrun unless it runs outside the tree. For `apps/server`'s workers pool it does, past three traps: a module-typed `package.json` beside the scratch config, a `node_modules` symlink, and absolute imports; the config merges the app's and points `test.dir` at the scratch directory. Composes with the transform-plugin method of the `as`-mutant finding above. Proposes that rule 11 require both runs in the report, red on the mutant and green on the source. Open.
- `2026-10-21-a-hand-mutant-edited-in-place-trips-the-stale-dist-guard-whose-exit-1-reads-as-a-kill` — a hand mutant written into source makes it newer than `dist/`, so `assertDistIsFresh` refuses the run before collection and Vitest 4.1.10 exits 1 having run nothing, which an exit-code check reads as a kill; a review's first six mutants all did this. Reproduced by an mtime change alone, and `touch -r` restores the run. Both the mtime reset and the transform plugin reach only specs that import source, since `runCliCommand` loads `dist/commands`. Proposes counting a hand mutant only on a named failing test, and deleting CLAUDE.md's Pre-Commit claim that a zero-test run reads as a pass, false on this Vitest. Open.
- `2026-10-21-entries-cast-to-unknown-at-the-boundary-and-re-cast-in-each-accessor-let-a-swapped-accessor-compile` — `extractConfigArrays` in `packages/compile/src/config-source.ts` cast `skills` and `agents` to `unknown[]` and each accessor cast again, so an accessor applied to the wrong array compiled. Once an expressive-ts round folded both uses into `maskedByTombstones(…, keyOf)`, a swapped `keyOf` passed every spec too, because `FULL_GLOBAL` tombstones its only global skill and agent. The boundary now casts to `SkillConfig[]` / `AgentScopeConfig[]`, so a swap fails `tsc` (TS2322, TS2345) while Vitest, stripping types, still passes it. Proposes a `clean-code-standards.md` 7.2 sentence (cast once, to the narrowest true type) and `tsc` beside Vitest for hand mutants; that sentence and the behavioural spec are unwritten. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-the-writer-named-as-the-shape-for-the-bytewise-rule-carried-its-own-copy-of-bytewise` — `canonicalizeStackOrder`, which CLAUDE.md names as the shape for ordering with `bytewise`, sorted sub-agent keys with a private `compareNamesInCodeUnitOrder` that answers the same for every pair: one rule in two live declarations. It now calls `bytewise` and the copy is deleted; the `stack-ordering` scenario in `contract/emission-scenarios.ts` pins the order. Proposes no check, as none catches a second comparator honestly. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-commands-spelled-out-isactiveat-and-plural-inline-beside-the-helpers` — `compile.ts` counted global sub-agents with an inline `!excluded && scope === "global"` beside its own `isActiveAt` calls, and five count lines in `utils/messages.ts` and `uninstall.tsx` hand-built what `plural()` returns. The output was correct; each copy read as new logic. All now call the helpers with byte-identical text, and a census for each shape is recorded. The `isActiveAt` shape remains in `lib/operations/project/compile-agents.ts`, outside the lane. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-library-modules-re-declare-shared-helpers-privately-and-one-copy-points-at-its-twin-in-a-comment` — a census of `src/cli/lib` found one try/`JSON.parse`/`undefined` body under three names, the category-exclusivity rule written three times (the `seed-apply.ts` copy's docblock points at `propagate.ts` rather than importing it), and `CATALOGUE_SKILL_IDS` declared twice. `isFieldMapping` now calls `isRecord` and `publish-seed.ts` imports `arrivedAsText`; the other three need homes in files the lane did not own. Proposes one shared declaration of each, and a `clean-code-standards.md` line that a comment pointing at identical code elsewhere is a duplicate. The command-level sibling is the four-command-guards finding above. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-bun-cwd-before-run-prints-usage-and-exits-0-so-a-gate-written-that-way-passes-unrun` — on bun 1.2.6, `bun --cwd <dir> run <script>` prints `bun run`'s usage and exits 0 without running the script, while `--cwd=`, dropping `run`, or `bun run --cwd` all run it; a lane's editor typecheck, lint and test gates passed that way having run nothing. No tracked file uses the broken form, so the trap is in hand-run gates. Proposes a `docs/architecture.md` › "Tests" sentence naming `bun run --cwd <dir> <script>`, and that a gate's exit code counts only beside the tool's own output line. Open.
- `2026-10-21-two-editor-doors-said-they-ask-the-same-rule-and-each-built-it-from-parts` — the editor stated three rules more than once, each under a comment claiming the copies agree: a refused placement press (`skill-cell.tsx`, `skill-options-panel.tsx`), a refused sub-agent press (`sub-agent-card.tsx`, `roster-panel.tsx`), and a skill's name-or-id (twice in `derive.ts`, plus `marketplace-switch.ts` and `stack-grid.tsx`). `pressRefusal` and `agentRefusal` in `provider.ts` and `displayNameOf` in `derive.ts` are now the one statement of each. The tell: a comment saying two sites agree holds only when both call one function. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-a-helper-reading-process-env-by-a-name-parameter-hides-the-variable-from-the-environment-gate` — an expressive-ts fold of `codexUserRoot` and `claudeStateRoot` into `relocatedRootIn(variable)` read `process.env[variable]`, so `e2e-runner-rosters-match-the-tree.test.ts` saw a read of `variable` and lost `CODEX_HOME_VAR` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR_VAR`, which the e2e doors are told to pin. That form never landed: the helper takes the value and each call site keeps its constant. Proposes a `clean-code-standards.md` rule that a helper over environment reads takes the value, never the name; it is unwritten. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-plugin-path-builders-were-re-derived-beside-the-functions-that-own-them` — the plugin modules had drifted from two CLAUDE.md rules nothing reports: five constant-to-constant aliases (`plugin-manifest.ts`, `plugin-validator.ts`, `plugin-settings.ts`) and six `"skills"`/`"agents"` path literals spelling out `STANDARD_DIRS`, beside three path builders rebuilt next to their owners (`getPluginManifestPath`, `listPluginDirNames`, `getPluginAgentsDir`). All are fixed except one literal in `loading/loader.ts`, another lane's file. Proposes a `no-restricted-syntax` selector for the alias rule; the literal rule has no honest lint. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-the-matrix-read-model-declared-who-reviewer-and-pm-serve-twice-under-two-names` — `isCrossDomainRoleAgent` in `assignment-defaults.ts` (which sub-agents a skill reaches) and `isDomainlessRoleAgent` in `preload-defaults.ts` (whether a reached one preloads it) stated one rule in different text under different names, so changing one would split targeting from preloading with no spec noticing. The first is deleted and `implementationDomainReach` calls the second. Proposes one invariant spec: every sub-agent `resolveLoadState` preloads is among `resolveAssignment`'s targets. Resolved.
- `2026-10-21-the-merger-keys-a-roster-by-scope-for-a-state-no-cli-write-produces-so-deleting-the-scope-passes-every-spec` — dropping `:${scope}` from `rosterIdentity` in `config-merger.ts` leaves every configuration spec green. It changes the result only for one identity at both scopes with the same excluded flag, where `uniqueBy` then drops the project row, and a census of the merger's producers finds no CLI write that makes that shape; only a hand-written `config.ts` reaches it, through `init --from`. The scope part stays. Leaves cli-tester the choice: a direct `mergeConfigs` spec (red on the mutant from a scratch copy), or a comment beside `rosterIdentity` saying it guards hand-written rosters only. Open.
- `2026-10-21-an-empty-plugin-name-passes-validation-because-the-schema-and-the-kebab-check-each-leave-it-to-the-other` — `validatePlugin` answers valid for a `plugin.json` whose `name` or `version` is `""`: both schema fields are a bare `z.string()`, and the kebab-case and semver checks run only behind `isNonEmptyString`. `doctor` reaches it through `validateInstalledPlugins`. The other three kebab-case judges refuse `""`, and `NAMES` in `kebab-name-judges-agree.test.ts` never feeds it. Proposes adding `""` to `NAMES` and `.min(1)` on both schema fields. Open.
- `2026-10-21-a-census-for-the-agent-extension-that-greps-a-quoted-md-misses-its-template-and-glob-spellings` — every census written for agent files spelling Claude's `.md` missed a spelling: the agent-count finding's quoted `".md"` grep misses template literals, and the Codex-orphan finding's `}.md` grep misses the `"*.md"` glob, five sites in all. `plugin-info.ts`, `remove-compiled-agents.ts`, `agent-plugin-compiler.ts` and `plugin-validator.ts` now ask `agentCodec("claude")`; `edit.tsx`'s `cleanupStaleAgentFiles` was another lane's and is unchanged. `getInstallationInfo` still counts no `.toml`, the agent-count finding's open half. Proposes the union of the greps as the one census; nothing runs it. Partial.
- `2026-10-21-a-story-that-closes-a-base-ui-popup-ended-before-its-unmount-frame-so-axe-flagged-the-focus-guards` — Base UI unmounts a closed popup a frame later (`useAnimationsFinished`), and until then its focus guards are tabbable with no modal backdrop, so the a11y `afterEach`'s axe flagged `aria-hidden-focus` whenever that frame came late: `menu.stories.tsx`'s `PickingReportsTheChoice` went red at random. Starving `requestAnimationFrame` by 250ms reproduces it 3 of 3. Both stories that close the menu now end once it has unmounted; the rule for `packages/ui/CLAUDE.md` is proposed, not written. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-specs-passed-without-a-terminal-and-failed-in-a-wider-one` — four command specs (the `init --from` permission box and three `list` snapshots) passed headless and failed at a terminal wider than 80 columns. A Vitest worker's stdout is a pipe, so Ink sized it through `terminal-size`, which reads `COLUMNS` and `LINES` and then `/dev/tty`; headless that finds nothing and falls back to 80. `vitest.setup.ts` now pins `COLUMNS` and `LINES` to 80 by 24, and `terminal-size-pinned-process-wide.test.tsx` names the pin. Pinning `process.stdout.columns` instead turned four `search.test.ts` specs red, because `@oclif/table` draws a pipe at its natural width. No headless gate can see the pin go, so it proposes running the CLI unit suite once in a wider pseudo-terminal in `commit-protocol.md` › Gates and CI. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-toasts-close-button-is-out-of-the-accessibility-tree-until-hovered-and-an-axe-audit-outlasts-the-toast` — Base UI's `ToastClose` renders `aria-hidden` unless the toasts are hovered or focused, so `getByRole("button", { name: "Close" })` finds nothing at rest; the editor's e2e close timed out on it and two stories failed on it. A full-page axe audit (18.6 s) also outlasts the toast's 5 s, so an audit started with a toast on screen finishes without it. Fixed: `Toasts.close` hovers first, a story pins the hidden-until-hovered behaviour, and the a11y audit holds the toast and counts it again afterwards. Proposes both rules for `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` › Conventions; unwritten. Open.
- `2026-10-22-an-editor-build-given-its-own-outdir-deletes-everything-beside-that-folder` — `spaFallbackShell`'s `buildStart` runs `rmSync(recursive, force)` on every sibling of the resolved `outDir`, so `vite build --outDir X` from `apps/editor` deletes whatever sits beside `X` (measured: a whole scratch folder). A relative `--outDir build` would take `src/`, `e2e/` and `package.json`. Discovery only. Proposes sweeping only under the configured `dist`, and until then a note that `--outDir` deletes its siblings. Open.
- `2026-10-22-a-wait-for-no-toast-passes-once-a-wrongly-raised-toast-expires` — a Playwright wait for no toast retries 5 s and a Base UI toast lives 5 s plus a 200 ms fade, so a wrongly raised toast satisfies the wait by expiring whenever the wait starts more than the fade after it (begun 500 ms late, it passed after 4911 ms). A roster-row spec passed against the defect under 8 workers; it now keeps an earlier toast up and asserts it is the only one. Of the census's two sites, "stays on, saying nothing" now waits inside 2 s (`BEFORE_A_TOAST_EXPIRES`), and "closes on its ✕" holds because a hovered toast's timer pauses. Proposes the rule for `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` › Conventions. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-the-workers-id-hashing-spec-pins-a-stack-id-whose-digest-moves-with-every-seed-version` — `names a payload by the base64url SHA-256 of what it stores` (`apps/server/src/index.test.ts`) needs a stack id whose stored body's digest has `+/` in its first eight standard-base64 characters. The body includes `v`, so the 6→7 bump turned `url-unsafe-16` into `LP3mVw9W` and reddened the spec, and its comment says what the id hashes to but not how it was found. Re-derived by search: `url-unsafe-540` → `U+/5jvhV`. Proposes that the comment carry the search, or that the spec find the id at run time. Partial: the spec now finds its id at run time (2026-10-26); the standard is not written.
- `2026-10-22-the-editor-typechecks-the-worker-through-its-built-declarations-so-a-contract-change-reads-as-a-stale-type` — `apps/server` exports its types from the gitignored `dist/index.d.ts`, and `@workspace/api`'s `hc<AppType>` reads them. After a contract change, `tsc -b --noEmit` inside `apps/editor` reports `Type '7' is not assignable to type '6'` at `api.configs.$post`, an error in the editor naming the previous shape, until `apps/server` is rebuilt. `turbo run typecheck` hides it through `^build`. Proposes saying so in `docs/architecture.md`, or an editor `pretypecheck` that builds the server. Open.
- `2026-10-22-a-link-mark-re-minted-by-a-reader-qualifies-entries-it-never-described` — `fromProject: true` is one boolean the editor reads as "hold every global entry", and the payload has no list of which entries it means. Two places set it besides the one it was made for. `share`, which minted through the same function as a bare `edit`, made the editor hold a recipient's global entries for them. The editor's `toSeedPayload` copied it forward, so a skill or sub-agent picked during a held session came back held although nothing had installed it. Every spec stayed green because each pinned only that the mark was present. Now only `editorPayloadForInstallation` sets it (a bare `edit` in a project). Proposes a rule in `docs/architecture.md`, beside the seed contract: a reader that re-mints a payload never copies a where-it-was-minted flag forward. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-download-giget-served-from-its-own-saved-tarball-reads-as-a-reached-marketplace` — when the network fails, giget unpacks its own saved tarball without saying so, so a download that succeeds does not show the marketplace answered; only the HEAD `recordFetchedCopy` sends afterwards does. Separately, `classifyCachedCopy` reads a copy with no fetch record as `unrecorded`, and the next load then discards both our copy and giget's, so a load that refuses a download must write the record first or it destroys every later offline command's fallback. Doctor's `askingEachMarketplaceItself` scope now does both. The test copies of the default marketplace (`shared-marketplace-checkout.ts` in the unit suite, `seedDefaultSourceCache` in e2e) stay offline only because their records have no ETag, so any path that asks regardless needs the answer stubbed. Outside the scope a record with no ETag is still never asked about again; changing it is an owner decision. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-refused-head-reads-as-an-answer-because-fetch-resolves-on-every-status` — `fetch` resolves on every HTTP status and `fetchEtag` read only the ETag, so a 404 with no ETag looked the same as a 200 with no ETag. That 404 is what GitHub, Bitbucket and sourcehut send for a missing repository; GitLab sends 302 then 403. Doctor printed `✓ Connected to remote` over a repository that was gone or private, and giget also extracts its saved tarball after a refused GET, so the download path had the same gap. Inside `askingEachMarketplaceItself`, a response that is not ok, 405 and 501 included, now throws `TarballWithheldError`, detailed as `Repository not found` / `Authentication required for` / `Access denied to`, on both paths. It refines the giget finding above: an answer counts only when its status is ok. Its standard landed as `clean-code-standards.md` 15.19. Outside doctor, `init --from` and `edit --from` stop on any 4xx but 405 and warn over a 5xx or 405, and every other load warns over a 5xx or 405 and keeps silent on a 4xx (owner, 2026-10-24). Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-rule-repaired-downstream-of-the-function-that-broke-it-held-only-on-the-paths-through-the-repair` — two rules were each broken in one function and repaired in a later one, so each held only on the paths through the repair. The decode dropped a sub-agent whose skills all failed to load; `edit --from` and the skip warning patched around it, and `init --from` did neither. `mergeInlinedScalarFields` wrote back the global description the merger had withheld, while the merger's spec stayed green. Both rules now live at their source: the decode installs each sub-agent a skipped skill names, so both repairs are deleted, and `GLOBAL_IDENTITY_FIELDS` moved to `config-source.ts`, where the merger and the writer both read it. Proposes a `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` rule: when a docblock says a function undoes an earlier one, fix the earlier one. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-command-spec-asserting-stderr-exactly-fails-its-first-attempt-when-it-is-the-first-ink-render-in-its-worker` — yoga-layout loads its wasm by `fetch`ing a `data:` URL. `useMockWorker` listens with `onUnhandledRequest: "error"`, so the first Ink render in a Vitest worker prints `[MSW] Error: intercepted a request without a matching request handler: • GET null` and the whole base64 module to stderr, and a spec pinning stderr with `toBe` fails its first attempt when it is that render. The `commands` project's `retry: 1` hides it. Reproduce with `npx vitest run --retry=0 -t 'warns which skills the catalogue does not know' src/cli/lib/__tests__/commands/init-from-says-what-it-did.test.ts`. Fixed (CLI-986): `mock-worker.ts` keeps `"error"` and gives `data:` URLs a pass-through handler. The proposed callback was not used: with msw 2.15 a callback's `print.error()` only prints, and the request goes out. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-mark-drawn-beside-wrapping-ink-text-covers-its-last-letters` — in Ink, a mark drawn as a sibling after wrapping text is drawn over the text's last letters, and a label `Text` beside a long value in a row box shrinks and splits. `list` showed "acme-release-checklis ⏏" and "Marketplac"/"e" while every spec was green, because no fixture name was long. Fixed in `SkillRow` and `list`'s `HeaderRow`; pinned at 80 and 100 columns by `e2e/interactive/list-keeps-long-names-whole.e2e.test.ts`. Its Correction: `paddingRight={1}` did not keep a full-width name off its neighbour or the border (the ink-draws-a-text finding below). `PanelHeader` (`summary-panel.tsx`) since fixed the same way, pinned by `e2e/interactive/init-from-panel-keeps-long-names-whole.e2e.test.ts`. Open: the Proposed Standard is in no standards file. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-ink-draws-a-text-up-to-two-columns-past-a-box-that-starts-or-ends-mid-column` — Yoga rounds a text node's left edge down and its right edge up, and Ink draws it from its ancestors' rounded positions. A name in a half of the Skills table whose edges fall between columns therefore drew up to two columns past the half, and `paddingRight={1}` left the second on the table's border: `list` at one width in three from 82, the summary panel at every width congruent to 2 mod 3, 80 included. `SkillRow` now pads by `NAME_GUTTER_COLUMNS` (3), which also keeps one blank column. Checked at 2,412 renders over 60–260 columns and three surroundings. Pinned at 100 columns by `list-keeps-long-names-whole` (its second name now fills the right half) and at 80 by `skill-agent-summary.test.tsx`. A wrapper Box around the text was tried and still overran at some widths. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-refine-on-a-zod-record-key-schema-loses-its-message-to-invalid-key-in-record` — a `refine` on a `z.record` key schema reports a refused key as `invalid_key` "Invalid key in record"; its own message survives only in that issue's nested `issues`, which no formatter here reads. `seedSkillTreeSchema`'s new refusal of a carried file key that steps through `..` or holds a NUL is therefore a record-level `superRefine` adding one issue per bad key at `path: [key]`, pinned by matrix `seed.test.ts`. Census (`.record(` with a constrained key): one other site, the same tree's `z.string().min(1)` key, which refuses `""` with the generic message; left as found. Proposes the rule for `typescript-types-bible.md`. Open.
- `2026-10-22-a-sanitiser-that-keeps-line-breaks-let-a-label-print-a-line-of-its-own` — `stripTerminalControls` keeps line feeds by design (5.8b), so a shared link's carried display name, a sub-agent's `codexModel` and an unknown payload key could each print a line of their own inside lines the CLI writes; the `codexModel` warning was not sanitised at all. `inertLine` (strip, then fold each line feed into a space) now serves `getSkillDisplayName`, edit's added-skill line, `unknownKeys` and the compile-seat sink. Proposes splitting 5.8: a body printed on its own lines keeps them, a value printed inside a line goes through `inertLine`. Sibling named, not fixed: `formatZodIssue` on a marketplace's documents. A later round folded a payload's `stackId` in `stackNotOfferedMessage` and refused a `marketplace` holding a line break or control (`refuseLineBreaksAndControls`, after `refuseCredentials`), echoing it inert. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-global-skill-a-project-skill-masks-in-an-exclusive-category-reads-as-added-on-every-edit-from` — re-applying the configuration a project was installed from was not a no-op when a project skill masks a global one in an exclusive category (`excluded: true` in the project's `config.ts`). `activeEntriesOf` in `edit.tsx` dropped the mask from the old side while the held selection carried the global active, so `detectConfigChanges` reported it in `addedSkills` every time: without a terminal `edit --from` refused where it should print `No changes made.`, and at one it asked over a panel marking nothing, then printed `+ E2E Pinia [G]`. Reproduced through `dist/` beside a control with no global install, which exits 0. `activeEntriesOf` now lists such a hidden global on the old side, read from the installed global config (`globalInstallsHiddenHere`); pinned in unit and e2e beside a no-mask control. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-provider-a-dist-run-names-outlives-its-spec-and-the-source-reset-cannot-reach-it` — `runCliCommand` runs oclif from `./dist/commands`, so a `--provider` it passes sets `providerChosenForThisRun` in `dist/`'s `install-layout.js`, and later `runCliCommand` specs in the worker inherit it. `forgetTheProviderChosenForThisRun` imported from source resets a different module instance. Of the four `runCliCommand` files passing `"--provider"`, only `remedies-name-the-installation-beside-another.test.ts` depends on order: it runs its provider-naming spec last. Proposes that provider-naming specs import the command from source with `captureOutput`, or a reset that reaches `dist/`. Resolved by the second: the `init` hook resets before anything else (CLI-968).
- `2026-10-22-an-ink-import-in-base-command-loads-yogas-wasm-for-every-command-and-a-stubbed-fetch-aborts-it` — a panel helper on `BaseCommand` made every command load Ink and `yoga-layout`, whose emscripten loader prepares its WASM through the global `fetch`. `doctor-content.test.ts` stubs `fetch` with an empty `Response`, so `doctor` (which draws nothing with Ink) aborted on `WebAssembly.instantiate(): BufferSource argument is empty`. The helper moved to `summaryPanelBlock` in `summary-panel.tsx`, called by the three commands that already load Ink. Proposes a `no-restricted-imports` on `base-command.ts` for `ink` and `../components/**`, which would catch only a direct import. Open.
- `2026-10-22-a-unit-spec-pinning-a-catalogue-skills-name-passes-on-the-catalogue-dist-ships` — `what uninstall says` pinned `- React ⏏` while a real uninstall drew raw ids. The pass came from `BUILT_IN_MATRIX`, which `dist/` bundles and which names `web-framework-react` "React", not from the `beforeEach`'s `initializeMatrix`, which seeds a module instance the dist-run command never reads. A spec pinning a skill's display name proves a read only with an id or name the built-in catalogue lacks. Resolved by a spec whose copy records "Acme React", and an e2e spec over `init --from` copies with fixture ids.
- `2026-10-22-a-story-drawn-where-the-test-browsers-pointer-rests-is-hovered-as-it-arrives` — the `storybook` Vitest project applies no `layout`, so a story draws at the page's top-left corner, where Chromium's pointer rests and sends trusted hover events to whatever arrives under it. Base UI shows a hovered toast's `✕` to the accessibility tree, so `CloseGlyphHiddenUntilHovered` failed 6 of 7 gate runs. A temporary ledger of trusted events on the toast was red 4 of 4 runs before and empty 5 of 5 after `ToastOnScreen` drew it off the corner. Census of hover-driven components: `toast.tsx` only. Proposes a `packages/ui/CLAUDE.md` gate rule. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-dialog-that-opens-on-arrival-hides-the-page-from-a-readiness-wait-by-role` — `ConfigurePage.goto()` waited for the stacks by role, so a dialog that opens on arrival (the owed save) and is up before the wait first looks hides them for good. The gate saw it once under load. Made deterministic by holding the page's `load` until the dialog shows: red 3 of 3 against the old wait at the gate's line, green 20 of 20 with `includeHidden: true`. Also notes that `toBeVisible()`'s 5 s cap is too short for a whole page boot. Proposes an `apps/editor/e2e/README.md` convention. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-no-line-of-its-own-guard-passes-inside-a-bordered-panel-whatever-the-label-holds` — once the summary panel replaced `init --from`'s plan, `not.toContain("\nNothing removed.\n")` stayed green against a build whose `inertLine` keeps line feeds, because an unfolded label makes a row behind the panel's border, not a line at column 0. The leg now pins `panelRow(ADDED_MARKER, "House Nothing")`, seen red on that build. Census of the same guard: every other leg already pairs it with a folded positive. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-cloudflares-asset-router-carries-the-query-onto-a-redirect-so-old-apex-share-links-reach-the-editor` — `agentsinc.sh/` is now a `301` to `/editor` (`apps/www/public/_redirects`), and three facts behind it were written nowhere. Cloudflare's asset router runs `_redirects` before any asset and carries the request's query onto a destination that has none, so old `agentsinc.sh/?fromId=…` share links now open the shared configuration in the editor. Starlight links every docs page's site title to `/` through `siteTitleHref`, which no config key sets; `src/route-data.ts` points it at `EDITOR_URL`, and `check-apex-redirect.ts` refuses a built page linking `/`. `unstable_startWorker` never answers a `fetch` under bun, so no www check can drive the real router. Proposes no standard; `todo/www.md`'s "share links already in the wild" paragraph no longer says where those links land. Open.
- `2026-10-22-a-hold-guarded-at-every-press-is-dropped-by-a-door-that-replaces-the-whole-selection` — A project link's held global entries were guarded one press at a time in config-store, and every spec drove single presses. Three doors were outside that set. `applyStack` and `applySavedStack` replaced the selection wholesale, and the stack door released the hold, so one scratch press switched every held item off. A new skill's default rows reached a held sub-agent, so `edit --from` rewrote the global `web-developer.md`. On Codex, two refusals combined (plugin+project not offered, held install mode not changeable), leaving no route to project. Fixed: `keepingHeld` in both replacing actions, `assignmentRefusal` and `select` keep new rows off held sub-agents, and `pressOnHeldSkill` makes the Codex project copy ejected. Proposes a spec that runs every `ConfigActions` member against a held link. `pruneToCatalog` (marketplace switch) is left open. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-copy-no-etag-pair-confirms-was-read-as-current-by-the-load-that-must-ask` — Doctor asked the marketplace, then read the saved copy whenever the answer could not be compared: a record with no ETag, an answer with no ETag, or a HEAD refused with 405/501. It showed `✓` over a stale copy, and over a host serving nothing. Its own offline run wrote the ETag-less record that set this up. Inside `askingEachMarketplaceItself`, the question is now a GET cancelled at its headers. The copy is read only when both ETags exist and match; anything else downloads into `<cache dir>.download` and replaces the copy only after the marketplace answers, so a refusal leaves the copy, its record and giget's tarball untouched. This replaces the record-first refusal of the giget finding and the 405/501 exemption of the refused-HEAD finding. Doctor's `Fetched … over the network` is now `Reached … over the network`. Unit doctor specs use `fingerprintSharedCheckout` so a download never lands in the shared checkout. Proposes a `clean-code-standards.md` rule: every answer that cannot be compared must download or refuse, never count as unchanged. Outside the scope, a record with no ETag is still never asked about again. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-default-export-told-apart-by-its-value-reads-export-default-undefined-as-the-module` — `unwrapDefaultExport` in `config-loader.ts` took the default export only when `namespace.default !== undefined`, so `export default undefined` / `void 0` evaluated to `{ default: undefined }`, slipped past both `exportsNothing` and `declaresNoDefaultExport`, and the schema parsed the module itself: `build marketplace` built silently over a config declaring nothing, and a named export beside it was read as a config field. It now asks `"default" in namespace`; every `loadConfig` reader then treats the file as it treats an empty one. Pinned in config-loader.test.ts and by the build marketplace refusal rows (unit and e2e). Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-sub-agent-pin-does-not-record-who-set-it-and-the-cli-sets-one-on-every-sub-agent` — the editor's "a stack's sub-agents stay on" rule read the pin `agents[id].on`, which four writers set (a stack, a click, a link, the unknown-skill rescue) without recording which. The CLI's `toSeedAgent` pins every installed sub-agent, so on every CLI-minted link deselecting a sub-agent's last skill left it on with no toast, while every editor spec stayed green because none opened a CLI-minted link. The store now drops the pin when a sub-agent's last live skill goes, unless the stack declares that sub-agent or a project's link holds it; the CLI is unchanged, so no link id moved. Proposes that a rule reading the pin is specced against `cliMintedPayload` / `projectMintedPayload`, in the editor's `e2e/README.md`. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-sweep-pinned-only-where-it-fires-left-the-filter-sparing-a-clicked-off-pin-unchecked` — `emptiedPins` in the editor's `config-store.ts` unpins a sub-agent whose last live skill goes, and its `entry.on === true` filter spares one the user clicked off. The specs covered every case where the sweep fires, plus the stack, held-by-a-project-link and never-held-a-skill cases, but not the clicked-off pin: with the filter deleted all of `config-store.test.ts` stayed green, while deselecting and re-picking the skill switched the sub-agent back on. A unit spec and a Playwright spec now pin it, each red with the filter deleted. Proposes, for `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` › Test Assertions, the converse of the refused/allowed rule: a sweep gets one spec per filter term, each an entry it must leave alone, in the same file as the specs where it fires. Unwritten. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-an-answer-after-a-giget-download-cannot-show-the-marketplace-served-what-giget-extracted` — giget 1.2.5 answers a failed GET, and its own ETag match, by extracting the tarball it saved earlier, saying nothing and returning the same result a real download returns, so an answer asked after the download describes the marketplace now, not what was extracted. One `GET 503` after the marketplace moved on made doctor install giget's old tarball as the copy under the live ETag and print `✓ … 10 skills available / Reached … over the network` while 9 were served, and treat it as current on every later run; `search` hit by the same failure recorded the live ETag the same way. `gigetDownload` in `source-fetcher.ts` now compares each giget tarball's modification time before and after the download; a saved tarball is recorded under the ETag giget saved it with (or none). Doctor's acceptance of a saved tarball under the ETag the marketplace answers with was withdrawn by `2026-10-23-a-download-giget-can-answer-from-its-saved-tarball-is-a-download-only-once-that-tarball-is-set-aside`, once the owner ruled that doctor always downloads. The e2e tarball server gained `failOnce`, `doctor-marketplace-download.e2e.test.ts` reproduces both cases, and both halves are mutation-checked. The proposal of `2026-10-22-a-download-giget-served-from-its-own-saved-tarball-reads-as-a-reached-marketplace` holds only while the network stays down. Proposes a `clean-code-standards.md` rule: a library that can answer from its own cache is asked which it did, or the cache is read before and after. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-branch-holding-a-slash-saves-gigets-tarball-a-folder-deeper-than-a-one-level-glob-looks` — `gigetDownload` listed giget's tarballs with `*/*.tar.gz`, but giget saves `{name}/{version}.tar.gz` and a git ref may hold a slash, so `github:org/repo#feature/x` sat a folder deeper. Its saved tarball then passed as a fresh download: doctor showed ✓ with a withdrawn skill still counted, and a load outside doctor recorded the live ETag over old content. `GIGET_TARBALLS` is now `**/*.tar.gz`; a unit describe and the doctor e2e scenario use a slash branch and were red first. A ref starting with `/` is still resolved by giget outside the listing (traced, not run; doctor refuses it). Proposes a `clean-code-standards.md` rule: a copied third-party layout is read at any depth wherever a segment comes from user input, and tested with an input holding the separator. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-crossing-spec-cannot-see-a-precedence-flip-unless-a-row-sets-both-operands` — `resolveDescription` exists twice, in the CLI's `local-installer.ts` and the preview's `output-preview.ts`. A CLI fix flipped the CLI's to `shared ?? stack` and left the preview's `stack ?? shared`, so a link carrying a `stackId` and its own `description` previewed the stack's description while `init --from` wrote the link's. Every suite stayed green: each side's unit specs pin only their own order, and no `preview-matches-install.spec.ts` row set both operands. The preview now matches the CLI; a unit spec and the crossing row `HbrDesc7` were each red before the fix. Census (`grep -n " ??" apps/editor/src/features/configure/lib/output-preview.ts`): the agent `model` and `effort` choices order as the CLI's `assembleAgentConfig` does, but no crossing row pins either, and the activation description is unchecked. Proposes, for `apps/editor/e2e/README.md`: where the preview copies a two-source choice from the CLI, the crossing spec gets a row in which both operands are set and differ. No checker, since whether a `??` copies a CLI rule cannot be read lexically. Resolved by CLI-949: the rule is in that README, the model choice has crossing row `HbrModl8`, effort cannot be pinned while no agent definition sets one, and the activation description is not a choice the CLI makes.
- `2026-10-22-a-load-told-which-installation-it-is-about-still-read-the-other-installations-copied-in-skills` — `loadSkillsMatrixFromSource({ provider })` read the named provider's config but found copied-in skills by roster order, at both scopes. So in a scope holding a Claude and a Codex installation, doctor's Codex block never read `.agents/skills`, reported a carried skill not found and exited 1. `discoverLocalSkills` now takes the provider at both scopes. Applying it at the global scope then showed that `hostsOnDisk` named a new installation's provider (Claude) for a folder holding none under a Codex global; it now takes the inherited global's provider, which also points `Marketplace Reachable` at that config's marketplace. Three provider-blind siblings are listed with a census: global plugin seating in `source-loader.ts` and `collectPluginSkillIds` in `multi-source-loader.ts`, both unrepaired, and the global host `hostsOnDisk` picks for a project holding its own installation (reproduced as a `Skills Installed` warning), since repaired under `2026-10-22-a-home-holding-both-installations-answers-claude-to-every-read-that-asks-it-for-the-global-a-codex-project-inherits`. Another instance of `2026-10-21-doctor-reports-each-installation-by-re-expressing-two-helpers-that-read-the-provider-only-through-process-state`; proposes threading an installation-naming option to every read at both scopes, naming the installation in play. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-limiter-spec-that-never-flakes-can-still-miss-its-regression-when-its-burst-straddles-a-minute` — Miniflare's rate limiter clears every key when the clock's minute changes, not 60 s after the first call. So a burst that straddles a minute can turn a pass into a failure ("still spends" flaked, `expected 200 to be 429`) and a catch into a miss (a mutant charging before the length guard passed "spends no allowance on a sentence it refuses for length"). Both compose specs that counted the real `COMPOSE_CALLS` now stub it with a per-key counter that has no window (`stubAllowance`); probes forcing the 6th call past a minute show the flake gone and the mutant caught. The census covers all three of the worker's limiters: `CONFIG_WRITES` was already stubbed, and Better Auth's sign-in rule (`auth.test.ts`) is exempt because it resets 60 s after the last counted request, not on the clock's minute. Proposes, for `standards/editor-and-worker.md`, stubbing a windowed binding whether or not it flakes, with a spec that has two callers the key must separate. Written as rule 1 of that standard's rate-limiter section. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-vi-mock-in-a-server-spec-never-reaches-the-copy-of-a-module-the-worker-already-imported` — under `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` 0.20.1, a `vi.mock` of `src/compose` replaced the handler the spec imported directly, while `app` from `src/index` and `SELF` both still ran the real one (401 where the mock answered 299), and a mutant factory was never evaluated, so the spec stayed green. Inferred, not established: `src/index` bound its copy before the mocks registered. No server spec uses `vi.mock` today. Proposes, for `standards/editor-and-worker.md`, replacing a collaborator through its binding or mounting the handler on its own app, never mocking a module `src/index` imports. Discovery only; written as rule 3 of that standard's rate-limiter section. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-limiter-stub-built-afresh-for-each-spec-never-sees-a-second-caller-so-the-limits-key-goes-unchecked` — against the real binding every compose spec spent from one store, so a limiter keyed by a constant failed 8 of 16 specs (the review's figure, not re-run). Once each spec built its own per-key stub and used one person, the stub never saw a second key, and mutants keyed by a constant, by address or by session all passed 16/16. A new spec, "counts a person's calls across their sessions, and apart from another person's at the same address", refuses the person's second sign-in and lets a second person at the same address through; it turns all three mutants red and passes the real route. `signInAgain` in `apps/server/src/db/seed-session.ts` makes the second session. A probe against the real binding at its configured limit gave ten 200s then a 429, then 429 for the second sign-in and 200 for the other person. Proposes that a file stubbing a limiter keep one spec with two callers the key must separate and two it must merge. Written as rule 2 of that standard's rate-limiter section; `stacks.test.ts` gained the `CONFIG_WRITES` pair, a signed-in editor and the CLI at one address, which a key also reading the cookie or the user agent fails. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-the-commit-plan-skill-is-gitignored-so-no-diff-or-gate-shows-it-drifting-from-the-commit-protocol` — `.claude/skills/commit-plan/SKILL.md` is matched by `.gitignore`'s `.claude` line, so no diff, commit or document checker ever sees it. It restated the commit protocol instead of pointing at it, and a full read found rules that had expired: generate checks described as `git diff --exit-code` (retired in `b4ea84aa`), a `prettier --check` pre-pass, "the gates run per commit", one concern per commit (root CLAUDE.md says about 10–20 commits) and staged-index divergence decisions (root CLAUDE.md says the owner unstages first). Each was rewritten or deleted. Proposes, for the owner, either tracking `.claude/skills/` (after removing the skill's machine path) or having the skill point at `commit-protocol.md` for every rule the protocol owns. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-the-summary-panel-is-drawn-only-by-render-to-string-so-its-scroll-hooks-never-act` — `SummaryPanel`'s only production mount is `summaryPanelText`'s `renderToString`. There Ink 7.1.1 makes `useInput`/`useStdout` no-ops and drops state a `useEffect` sets, so `usePanelScroll`, `useMeasuredHeight` and `ScrollAffordance` keep their initial values: a 60-skill panel printed 40 lines and no `more below`. The wizard mounted the panel live until 67ac2fc6, and 417dc942 brought it back only as text. The hooks' specs mount live through ink-testing-library, so they stay green while covering behaviour no command reaches. Discovery only; the owner chooses between deleting the hooks and their specs or mounting the panel live again. Resolved: the owner chose deletion (2026-10-23), so the hooks, `ScrollAffordance`, `SCROLL_VIEWPORT` and their specs are gone and the panel draws plain boxes (CLI-947).
- `2026-10-22-a-spec-that-a-command-survives-a-broken-dependency-holds-nothing-unless-its-file-shows-the-fixture-breaks-it` — `planEject` loads the marketplace for `skills` and `all` only, and nothing held that rule: with `|| type === "templates"` deleted, the eject specs stayed green and `tsc` reported 0 diagnostics, while `eject templates` against a marketplace that cannot be loaded went from exit 0 to exit 1. `eject.test.ts` now pins `templates` and `agent-partials` completing in that state, beside a control, `eject skills` refusing with the same config, which guards against a fixture that stops breaking the load. Red against M11, M11c and M11b. Measured in memory but not applied: `sourceResult?: never` on `EjectPlan`'s source-less member makes `tsc` reject M11 (TS2322). Proposes the control rule in `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` › Test Assertions, as the converse of the REFUSED/ALLOWED pair. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-the-expressive-typescript-skill-recommends-as-const-satisfies-which-stryker-never-mutates` — Pattern 8 of the `meta-design-expressive-typescript` skill recommends `as const satisfies Shape` for static configuration, which Stryker gives no mutant and does not report (`2026-10-20-stryker-never-mutates-code-under-a-typescript-as-and-the-report-does-not-say-so`). The skill does not say so, and no gate notices a conversion. The configuration lane's annotated constants were left as they were: an annotation already checks their shape and nothing reads their literal types, and in `config.ts` (the SSRF tables) and `scope-diff.ts` (`AGENT_SETTINGS`) an `as const` would also take them out of mutation testing. `REMOTE_PROTOCOLS`, already `as const`, now types `validateRemoteSource`'s protocol, so a misspelt prefix is TS2367. Proposes that Pattern 8 keep `as const satisfies` for constants whose literal types are read, and name the Stryker cost; the skill lives in `agents-inc/skills`, and a pass can census its own diff with `git diff | grep -n '^+.*as const satisfies'`. Open.
- `2026-10-22-a-dispatch-over-a-closed-union-written-as-literal-subsets-compiles-unchanged-when-the-union-grows` — dispatches over a closed union that name only some of its members compile unchanged when the union grows. `tierNeedsDeps` / `tierRegeneratesTypes` / `tierPropagates` in `classify.ts` would give a fifth `ConsequenceTier` no deps, no types and no propagation, and `hostCompileNotices` would print nothing for a third provider; both type-level mutations left `tsc` at exit 0. The tiers now read one table that `satisfies Record<ConsequenceTier, …>`, without `as const` so Stryker still mutates it, and `hostCompileNotices` is a `never`-default switch, so the same mutations fail with TS1360 and TS2322. `announceRefetch` in `source-fetcher.ts` and the `requireMetadata` loop in `loadSkillsFromDir` (`loader.ts`) have the same shape and are left alone, because each union is read in one file (the expressive-typescript skill's exemption). Proposes that the if-chain finding's `typescript-types-bible.md` rule also name the boolean-subset and self-filtering fan-out shapes, and say whether one-file unions are exempt. Not written. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-the-config-gates-copy-of-the-writers-extracted-fields-is-held-to-it-by-a-docblock` — the writer's four extracted fields (`skills`, `agents`, `stack`, `selectedDomains`) were declared twice: `EXTRACTED_FIELDS` in `packages/compile/src/config-source.ts`, and a private Set in `packages/cli/src/cli/lib/config-gate/classify.ts`, which decides what `scalarKeys` counts as an inlined scalar, held to it only by a docblock. So a fifth field, which now fails `tsc` in compile, left the gate's classification on the old four; drift would under-tier a global scalar change (inferred, not measured). Compile now exports `EXTRACTED_FIELDS` and `classify.ts` builds its set from the import, as `config-merger.ts` already does with `HAND_WRITTEN_FIELDS`; `grep -rn 'EXTRACTED_FIELDS' packages/cli/src packages/compile/src --include='*.ts'` names one declaration and one import. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-installation-skillsdir-names-claudes-folder-for-a-codex-installation-and-only-specs-read-it` — `Installation.skillsDir` (`detectInstallationInDir`, `installation.ts`) joins `CLAUDE_DIR` for every provider, so for a Codex installation it names `.claude/skills` or `.claude/plugins`, while the `agentsDir` beside it is provider-aware. A language-service reference census finds no product reader, only specs and a factory asserting the Claude path, which is why no unused-symbol grep sees it. CLAUDE.md already says to delete a field with no reader; not done here because the expressive-ts pass had to leave specs unchanged. Proposes no new rule; a census for members read only by specs is cheap to run but would be a noisy gate. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-constant-reaching-its-options-through-a-spread-or-a-variable-is-never-checked-for-unknown-keys` — TypeScript checks only a fresh object literal for unknown keys, so `DECLINE_LOCATION` (`apps/server/src/auth.ts`), `EXTERNAL_LINK_ATTRS` (`apps/www/src/lib/links.ts`) and the three served skill trees in `packages/api-mocks/src/fixtures.ts` compiled with a misspelt key while their consumers' defaults applied. Each now carries `satisfies` the consumer's own type, so the same probes fail at the definition (TS2561, TS2561, TS2344); type-equality probes show no constant's type changed, and `handlers.ts` lost its uncommented cast. Nothing would catch the clause being removed, and the census covers that lane's files only. Its `## Correction` withdraws the `as const` half of its Proposed Standard: `satisfies` alone refuses the key, so whether `as const` comes first is `2026-10-22-the-expressive-typescript-skill-recommends-as-const-satisfies-which-stryker-never-mutates`'s rule. Proposes a `typescript-types-bible.md` § 10 rule. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-declaration-emitting-tsc-probe-writes-the-d-ts-of-a-file-outside-its-root-beside-that-files-source` — a scratch `tsc` probe with `declaration`, an `outDir` and no `rootDir` imported `packages/api-mocks/src/fixtures.ts` by absolute path. `tsc` 6.0.3 reported TS5011 and still wrote `fixtures.d.ts` beside the source, which failed `@workspace/api-mocks#lint` until it was deleted. Reproduced in scratch: `"rootDir": "/"` keeps the output under `outDir`, and `noEmitOnError` writes nothing (its TS6059 names the config's own directory as the defaulted `rootDir`). Proposes, beside `typescript-types-bible.md` § 10's type-probe advice, that a probe checks with `noEmit`, and one emitting declarations sets `rootDir` or `noEmitOnError`. Open.
- `2026-10-22-an-if-chain-over-a-union-compiles-a-new-variant-into-its-last-branch-unless-that-branch-reads-a-field-it-lacks` — five editor handlers branched over a discriminated union with an if-chain whose closing `return` stood for the leftover variant: `targetNameOf`, `isApplied` and the `useApplyStackRequest` handler over `StackRequest`, `toGridCell` over `SelectionVerdict` (its switch now in `unselectedCell`), and `descriptionFor` over `TargetState`. A new variant therefore compiled into the last branch: a compiler-API probe that splices a variant in memory showed 0 errors, or one accidental TS2339 about a missing property, before the rewrite, and one TS2322 per handler after it. Each is now a `switch` with a `never` default. The proposed `typescript-types-bible.md` rule (three or more variants means a switch with a `never` default) is unwritten, and no lint or tsc gate holds the shape. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-the-nav-rails-refusal-lines-are-pinned-by-no-spec-so-only-a-nocoverage-mutant-shows-it` — `REFUSAL_COPY` in `nav-rail.tsx` words six sign-in and sign-out refusals, keyed by action so a refused sign-out does not say sign-in, and no spec asserts any of the six lines: a census over `apps` and `packages` finds them only in `nav-rail.tsx`. `accounts.spec.ts` asserts the rail's alert only as absent, and its "a sign-in the worker will not serve" specs check only that the page survives, so swapped tables, a wrong `attemptOf` action or a deleted `RefusalLine` all pass. The lines are correct today, read off with the worker's auth routes answering 429, 500 and an aborted request. Their only signal is the editor's Stryker NoCoverage mutants, which a Pattern 8 `as const satisfies` conversion would erase, so the expressive-ts pass reverted that conversion. Proposes asserting `accountNotice` text on the unreachable sign-in and sign-out, and naming the pinning spec in `editor-and-worker.md`'s refusal table. Open.
- `2026-10-22-compiles-coverage-floor-admits-one-uncovered-line-so-no-test-can-offset-a-never-default-arm` — `packages/compile`'s line floor left room for one uncovered line, so the round-3 `never` default arms, unreachable by construction, put it beyond any test; most of its uncovered branches likewise guarded input the types refuse. Deleted as moot: the owner dropped coverage floors the same day, since mutation testing replaces them.
- `2026-10-22-a-home-holding-both-installations-answers-claude-to-every-read-that-asks-it-for-the-global-a-codex-project-inherits` — a project inherits only its own provider's global (`writeFromProjectContext` says so), but reads elsewhere ask `providerInUse(<home>)`, which answers Claude by roster order for a home holding both. In a Codex project under such a home `doctor` looked for the Codex global's carried skill in `~/.claude/skills` and warned it missing, exit 0. `hostsOnDisk` now takes the project's own provider for the global where the home holds it; pinned by `installation-reads.test.ts` and `codex-doctor-under-a-home-holding-both-global-installations.e2e.test.ts`, Claude the control. `mutateGlobal` from `uninstall` is repaired too (the Codex global had kept an uninstalled Codex project in `projects`): a `deregister-project` mutation carries the project's provider, pinned by `mutate-global.test.ts`, `uninstall.test.ts` and `uninstall-under-a-home-holding-both-global-installations.e2e.test.ts`, Claude the control. CLI-984 repaired the rest: every read of the inherited global asks `inheritedGlobalProvider` (`install-layout.ts`), which `hostsOnDisk` now shares, through `resolveInstallPaths`, discovery, the global compile pass, `configsInPlay`, the source loader and the source-config reads; pinned by the discovery, copy and all-scopes unit specs and the compile and `edit --from` e2e specs, Claude the control. Proposes a Scope Awareness rule in `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md`, still unwritten. Partial.
- `2026-10-22-a-process-wide-timer-count-judges-every-timer-in-the-worker-not-the-one-the-run-started` — `base-command.test.ts` judged "leaves no poll running" by counting `Timeout` entries in `process.getActiveResourcesInfo()`, which is every timer in the vitest worker, so a timer the run does not own ending mid-spec reddened it once in the unit gate; one planted 40 ms timer made it fail every time. The spec now spies on `setInterval`/`clearInterval` and asserts each interval the run started was cleared; removing the product's `clearInterval` still reddens it. Census `listenerCount(`/`getActiveResourcesInfo`: only the same file's stdout `resize` listener counts remain, owned by the code under test. Proposes a Test Assertions rule in `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md`. Resolved.
- `2026-10-22-a-persisted-store-writes-before-its-first-read-so-a-pick-during-the-opening-overwrote-the-saved-configuration` — zustand 5.0.14's `persist` writes on every `set`, hydrated or not. So the editor's `skipHydration` config store wrote a pick made while a parked restore waited over a saved configuration it had not yet read: the only slot before per-marketplace slots, the public catalogue's after them. The store now starts on `UNWRITTEN_SLOT`, and only `readSavedConfig` switches writes on. Served as `storage: OWN_SLOT`, the change reddens `config-store.test.ts`' "writes nothing before a slot has been read" and `catalog-first.spec.ts`' "keeps the saved picks through a pick made while it waits". Partial: `editor-and-worker.md`'s corollary still calls the window uncovered.
- `2026-10-22-the-stored-marketplace-choice-is-not-the-saved-configuration-that-was-read-so-loading-it-wrote-nothing` — `useMarketplaceStore`'s `current` names the marketplace a restore will read; while that restore is parked, nothing has been read and the config store writes nothing. Two decisions asked the stored choice: a stack applied on that marketplace once it loaded, and a load of it after another payload parked in the restore's place. Both skipped `readSavedConfig` and dropped the edits after. Both now ask `marketplaceOnScreen`, each pinned by a `catalog-first.spec.ts` spec that reddens with the stored choice served back. The rule saying which fact to ask is in `editor-and-worker.md` (CLI-952), and the switch question's `costOfPayload` is pinned by three `use-apply-stack-request.test.ts` specs, with a restore parked and with a link open, red with the stored choice served back (CLI-953). Resolved.
- `2026-10-23-a-frame-sampled-once-after-a-fixed-delay-catches-the-scroll-panel-before-its-heights-settle-in-a-busy-worker` — `use-panel-scroll.test.tsx` › "shows the top lines…" read `lastFrame()` once, `RENDER_DELAY_MS` after mounting, and reddened the unit gate once with `…2 more below`. The panel's heights settle over several renders, each a React-scheduler task on `setImmediate` that a busy worker starts late. A setup file delaying `setImmediate` by SLOW_MS reproduces it. At HEAD the three spec files rendering these hooks go red at 60 (this spec), 100 (`summary-panel`) and 150 (two `use-measured-height` specs and a key spec). Fixed: they wait with `vi.waitFor` and `SETTLE_TIMEOUT_MS`, keeping the delays that hold a spec past the hook's own retry timers. They pass 32/32 up to 250, and each mutant still turns them red. The scroll specs, the `Math.max` guard and the shared `SETTLE_TIMEOUT_MS` went with the scrolling code (CLI-947). The two `resizedTo` specs in `use-terminal-dimensions-follows-the-terminal` now wait the same way (CLI-955), and all seven census files pass at 150 and 250. Resolved: `clean-code-standards.md` 6.5 carries the rule.
- `2026-10-23-a-command-spec-cannot-mock-giget-and-giget-keeps-whichever-fetch-it-found-when-it-loaded` — once doctor downloads on every run, each doctor command spec over the default marketplace calls giget, and two unrecorded facts decide what that call does. `vi.mock("giget")` cannot reach it: `runCliCommand` runs oclif, which loads `./dist/commands` with a native `import()`, so the bundled source-fetcher's giget never passes through vitest's module graph. And giget 1.2.5 reaches `fetch` through `node-fetch-native`, which binds `globalThis.fetch` once at load, so a spec that stubs `fetch` before giget first loads leaves giget on that stub for the rest of the worker, past `vi.unstubAllGlobals()`. Fixed: `serveDefaultMarketplaceHere` in `shared-marketplace-checkout.ts` serves a one-file stand-in on 127.0.0.1 and points `GIGET_GITHUB_URL`, which giget reads at call time, at it; `doctor.test.ts` and `doctor-content.test.ts` start it in `beforeEach`, and the helper's own spec has giget download it. Proposes, for `packages/cli/CLAUDE.md` › Test Data, redirecting a dependency a command spec reaches through `dist/` by what it reads at call time, never `vi.mock`. Resolved.
- `2026-10-23-a-download-giget-can-answer-from-its-saved-tarball-is-a-download-only-once-that-tarball-is-set-aside` — under the owner's "Always download" for doctor, skipping doctor's own "is the copy current" question was not enough. giget 1.2.5's `downloadTemplate` sends a HEAD and, when the ETag matches the one recorded beside its saved tarball, extracts that tarball with no GET; a failed GET falls back to it too, silently and with the result a download returns. So a doctor run could send no GET at all: e2e `doctor-marketplace-download` › "a marketplace doctor downloads on every run" passed with exit 0 while its `failOnce("GET", 503, 2)` was never reached. `downloadPast` in `source-fetcher.ts` still refuses a saved tarball under another ETag; one under the live ETag is renamed to `{tarball}.set-aside` so giget must fetch, renamed back if that download fails and removed once it succeeds, so a failed download leaves the copy, its record and giget's tarball byte-identical. The e2e leg "fails the marketplace row when the download fails though the marketplace answered" and the unit specs whose names begin "downloads past the tarball giget saved" were red first. This withdraws the saved-tarball acceptance that `2026-10-22-an-answer-after-a-giget-download-cannot-show-the-marketplace-served-what-giget-extracted` resolved with. Proposes a `clean-code-standards.md` rule: a library's own cache is a saved copy, and where the library has no switch to bypass it (`force`, `forceClean` and `offline: false` all keep giget's HEAD shortcut) the caller removes what it would fall back on. Partial: an interrupted set-aside download left a cut tarball under the live ETag and failed every later doctor run, until the record was set aside first and put back last (e2e "a doctor run interrupted while it downloads the marketplace" red first); the Proposed Standard is not written.
- `2026-10-23-a-fetch-failure-is-classified-by-a-substring-of-a-message-that-holds-the-marketplace-url` — `createDetailedFetchError` in `source-fetcher.ts` picks its remedy by searching the whole error message for `404`, `401`, `403`, `ENOTFOUND`, `ETIMEDOUT` and `network`, and that message holds the marketplace's URL. So a host, port or path holding one of them gets the wrong remedy whatever the marketplace answered: a server answering `503` to everything printed `Repository not found` on port 14041 and the true `503 Service Unavailable` on port 15031 (hand-run through the built CLI). `startTarballSourceServer` listens on a random port, so an e2e spec asserting a non-404 refusal, such as `doctor-diagnostics`' `405` case, fails whenever the port holds `404`, `401` or `403`. Proposes classifying by status, and a `clean-code-standards.md` line: never match an error message that interpolates user input.
